# Test Overview

A catalogue of CoGA's automated tests: **what each test file verifies**, with a link to the
file. This is the file-centric view. For the requirement-centric view (each requirement and
its verifying test), see the Requirements Traceability Matrix,
[TF-09b](regulatory/TF-09b-requirements-traceability-matrix.md).

The backend tests run with `pytest` (Python), the frontend tests with `vitest`
(TypeScript/React). The test tree is the source of truth for how many there are; the
commands under [Keeping this current](#keeping-this-current) print the counts.

---

## Test strategy

- **Unit-first, self-contained.** The backend unit suite mocks Postgres and ClickHouse, so
  it runs without service containers. Pure analysis logic (NIPT, haplotype/PGT, ACMG/CNV,
  mtDNA, prioritisation) is tested directly against synthetic inputs.
- **Integration and end-to-end.** Integration tests boot the real app against Postgres and
  ClickHouse (schema, admin seed, health probe) to catch startup and schema failures the
  unit suite cannot. The end-to-end harness runs the golden-trio pipeline and the demo
  bundles against documented expected results.
- **Frontend components and logic.** `vitest` and Testing Library cover the pure `lib/`
  logic, the visualization components (with the canvas mocked) and page behaviour.
  Safety-relevant UI (route guards, ACMG/CNV/NIPT readouts, sign-out and drift) is covered
  explicitly.
- **Fail-safe on degraded input.** Across NIPT, ACMG, CNV and haplotypes, degraded or empty
  input must abstain (low confidence, VUS, uninformative), never silently mis-call.
- **One backend test root.** Every backend test lives under `backend/tests/`, the only
  `testpaths` entry in `pyproject.toml`, so `pytest` collects the same suite from the repository
  root and from `backend/`. `scripts/check-test-catalogue.sh` fails if a test file appears
  outside it.

## How to run

```bash
# Backend: the unit suite (mocked datastores)
python -m pytest -q

# Backend: integration and end-to-end tests (need Postgres and ClickHouse running)
RUN_INTEGRATION=1 python -m pytest backend/tests/integration -q
RUN_INTEGRATION=1 python -m pytest backend/tests/e2e -q

# Frontend: the full gate, as in CI
cd frontend && npm run tsc && npm run lint && npm run test
```

Without `RUN_INTEGRATION=1` the integration and end-to-end tests are skipped. The settings
the CI jobs use for the datastores are in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`.

## Continuous integration

`.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs these jobs on every pull request and push to `main`; the
security gates (`deps`, `secret-scan`, `codeql`) are in `.github/workflows/security.yml`.

| Job | What it runs | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **backend** | `pip install -r backend/requirements-dev.txt`, `ruff check .`, `mypy`, `scripts/generate-api-types.py --check`, `pytest -q` | Lint (`[tool.ruff]` in `pyproject.toml`); a type check of the clinical-critical modules listed under `[tool.mypy]`; a check that the frontend's generated API types (`frontend/src/lib/apiSchema.generated.ts`) match the backend's OpenAPI schema; then the unit suite with its coverage floors, which fail if a module the type check lists has none. |
| **smoke** | `pytest backend/tests/integration` against Postgres and ClickHouse service containers | The real startup: schema migration (baselines and revisions), admin seed, health probe. Records coverage for the `coverage` job. |
| **e2e** | `pytest backend/tests/e2e` against the same services | The golden-trio pipeline and the demo bundles against documented expected results (see [TF-09c](regulatory/TF-09c-e2e-pipeline-verification.md)). Records coverage for the `coverage` job. |
| **coverage** | combines the `backend`, `smoke` and `e2e` coverage data, then `scripts/check-coverage-floor.py --combined` | Floors on the combined figure for the modules only real datastores exercise (import pipeline, integrity anchors, sign-out), which the unit job under-reports. A required check. |
| **frontend** | `npm ci`, `npm run tsc`, `npm run lint`, `npm run test:coverage` | Type check, ESLint and vitest. The type check includes `src/lib/apiContract.ts`: each hand-written API type in `apiTypes.ts` must accept the response shape the backend's schema declares. The React hooks and jsx-a11y rules are errors, and `npm run lint` fails on any warning (`--max-warnings 0`). vitest fails below the coverage floors in `vite.config.mts`: a global one, and a separate one for `components/visualizations`. |
| **e2e-playwright** | seeds the golden trio, then Playwright drives Chromium against a uvicorn backend and the production frontend (the built bundle served by `server.mjs`, with its CSP) | Browser journeys; see [TF-09d](regulatory/TF-09d-browser-e2e-verification.md), which includes a manual procedure for auditors. |
| **sbom** | CycloneDX SBOMs for the backend and the frontend, uploaded as artifacts | Supply-chain evidence (see [sbom/README.md](../sbom/README.md)). Not a required check. |
| **images** | `docker build` of `backend/Dockerfile` and `frontend/Dockerfile` at their `production` targets, as `ci/cloudbuild.*.yaml` build them: the repository root as context, the same build arguments, the backend stamped with `VERSION` and the 12-character commit as `build.yml` stamps a release. Then each image runs one command: the backend imports the app and reports that version and commit, the frontend loads `server.mjs` and holds `dist/index.html`; neither runs as root | `build.yml` builds the images only after a merge, and not at all until Google Cloud is configured, so this is the build that checks a pull request's Dockerfiles. Nothing is pushed: the job has no registry credentials. `backend/tests/test_deployment_config.py` fails when these builds drift from Cloud Build's. Not a required check: making it one is the owner's setting in branch protection. |
| **catalogue** | `scripts/check-test-catalogue.sh`, then `scripts/check-handleiding-sync.sh` | Fails when a test file has no row here or a row names a file that no longer exists, and when the handleiding HTML no longer matches its Markdown chapters. |

Twelve checks are required status checks on `main` (the ruleset `CoGA_branch_protect`):
`backend`, `frontend`, `coverage`, `smoke`, `e2e`, `e2e-playwright` and `catalogue` from
`ci.yml`, `deps`, `secret-scan` and the two `codeql` checks (Python, JavaScript/TypeScript)
from `security.yml`, and GitHub's `CodeQL` code-scanning result. A branch need not be up to
date with `main` before it merges. `sbom` and `images` run but are not required.
How the protection is enforced, and where it falls short, is in
[TF-18 §6](regulatory/TF-18-change-configuration-management.md).

### Browser end-to-end (Playwright)

`frontend/e2e/*.spec.ts` drive a real Chromium against a running stack: a uvicorn backend and
the production frontend, that is the built bundle served by `server.mjs` with its enforcing
CSP and `/api` proxy. It is the only suite that exercises the actual UI, and every journey
fails if the browser reports a CSP violation (`e2e/fixtures.ts`). Run it locally with the
datastores up:

```bash
RUN_INTEGRATION=1 python scripts/seed_playwright_e2e.py   # golden trio + a known e2e user
cd frontend && E2E_PYTHON=/path/to/python npx playwright test
```

`E2E_FRONTEND=dev` runs the journeys against the Vite dev server instead (faster to iterate,
but no build and no CSP), and `E2E_BACKEND_PORT` moves the backend off port 8000 when it is
taken.

| Spec | Journey |
| --- | --- |
| [frontend/e2e/auth.spec.ts](../frontend/e2e/auth.spec.ts) | Login form: bad credentials stay on /login; the seeded e2e user logs in and lands authenticated. |
| [frontend/e2e/family.spec.ts](../frontend/e2e/family.spec.ts) | Open the golden family workspace; render the genome overview (SVG/canvas tracks) — frontend↔backend wiring + viz render. |
| [frontend/e2e/security-headers.spec.ts](../frontend/e2e/security-headers.spec.ts) | The production frontend server sends its security headers: the CSP (`default-src 'self'`, `frame-ancestors 'none'`, `object-src 'none'`), `X-Content-Type-Options` and the `Permissions-Policy`. |
| [frontend/e2e/signout.spec.ts](../frontend/e2e/signout.spec.ts) | Clinical report sign-out from the browser: the live report of the seeded report-tagged variant renders, sign-out handles the drift and sample-QC override dialogs, and the page then shows the version just signed, rendered from its record, whose version advanced and which the family's data still matches. |

### Stylesheet changes (computed-style diff)

A CSS change can be checked for what it renders, not only for what it says.
[frontend/scripts/stylediff](../frontend/scripts/stylediff/README.md) renders two production builds side by side against the same
seeded stack (golden trio, NIPT demo, demo quartet) and compares every element's computed style,
pseudo-elements and box, over ~110 routes at three widths, with forced `:hover`/`:focus`,
expanded `<details>` and print media. It is a manual tool, not a CI job. It verified the CSS
clean-up of #712: no element rendered differently.

---

## Backend tests

### Monogenic NIPT
| Test file | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| [backend/tests/test_nipt.py](../backend/tests/test_nipt.py) | NIPT family/trio identification and assay-key resolution. |
| [backend/tests/test_nipt_analysis.py](../backend/tests/test_nipt_analysis.py) | Fetal-fraction estimation, fetal-sex inference, 8-category site classification, empty-input fail-safe. Categories stay within the fetal states the father's genotype allows; a father call below `min_father_dp` counts as no call; paternity evidence counts only sites with a usable father call. The R NIPT-M model (v0.5.1): its validated constants; the quality filter names each failure reason, and a value the call lacks passes; the funnel counts the plasma's calls (a paternal allele without a plasma call apart, as paternal-only) and applies the fetal-VAF floor; the FF estimate reports the 5th–95th percentile of its sites' allele fractions; the variant class; the maternal-allele, paternal-transmission and fetal-homozygous probabilities beside each father genotype (a hom-alt mother always passes her allele; an indel and an allele-balance outlier are flagged); a paternal allele the plasma does not show, read at its depth from the target coverage, and a few reads where few were expected read as transmitted; the father's classes and their flags; paternity evidence split into his homozygous (all expected) and heterozygous (half expected) alleles; a site read off another representation counts once. The fetal sex needs most (female) or hardly any (male) of the father's X alleles seen, else it is indeterminate; a plasma call read off another record is left out; the combined fetal sex of the paternal X and the chrY coverage (agreement, one signal, discordance, the 4% FF floor of a female chrY reading). |
| [backend/tests/test_nipt_triage.py](../backend/tests/test_nipt_triage.py) | The de novo triage (the R NIPT-M v0.5.1 rules; synthetic sites): the window is the fetal band of the FF sites (strict, and a quarter wider loose, within 0.5–35%); a clean rare missense SNV in the strict window is high priority, a common known variant scores low, the loose window reaches medium at most, a call outside the window is not prioritised; the technical and variant-class terms (a repeat indel or an MNV is capped at medium); a paternal low-level signal, or a father calling part of the allele, costs two and caps at medium; an allele another cfDNA sample carries is excluded as recurrent, unless ClinVar may call it pathogenic (same score); no window, no triage; a candidate needs a passing plasma call of its own (not one read off another record, nor a depth from the coverage alone) and no supported paternal call. The recessive fetal risk: a compound pair's risk is the product of the two inheritance probabilities, a site both parents carry reads the fetal hom-alt probability, a paternal allele not transmitted makes it low, an untold inheritance uses the 50% prior and says so, a homozygous parent is not a carrier, the father's allele at a site the mother is homozygous for was inherited only by a homozygous fetus, an allele without a population frequency (an MNV) is noted, and genes need both parents and are ordered by risk. |
| [backend/tests/test_nipt_coverage.py](../backend/tests/test_nipt_coverage.py) | On-target coverage summarization, weighted-median, low-coverage flagging. |
| [backend/tests/test_nipt_target_coverage.py](../backend/tests/test_nipt_target_coverage.py) | A capture panel's per-target coverage table (synthetic values): numbers with a decimal comma or thousands separators; the targets parsed, a row that does not parse skipped and a table without its columns refused; a stored `target_coverage` row reads back as the table row; the depth at a position uses BED coordinates (the lower of mean and median), and overlapping targets resolve to the deepest; the target QC flags targets below 300× or not fully covered, counts those below 1000×, and lists a requested gene the table lacks; for the coverage page, every gene in scope, those whose targets all pass too; the plasma's sex profile from the chrX and chrY depth (no chrY signal, a male fetal signal with its indicative FF, male DNA that cannot be maternal plasma). |
| [backend/tests/test_nipt_end_to_end.py](../backend/tests/test_nipt_end_to_end.py) | End-to-end NIPT on the demo's combined VCF: FF and category recovery; the funnel counts the plasma's calls, with the category-8 site (no plasma call) counted apart as paternal-only; the recessive view lists the genes where both parents are heterozygous carriers (a compound pair, and a site the fetus is homozygous for), not one both parents are homozygous for, with each gene's fetal risk. |
| [backend/tests/test_nipt_artifact_pg.py](../backend/tests/test_nipt_artifact_pg.py) | Recurrent-artifact list CRUD, per-assay scoping. Auto-seed counts only the assay's own cfDNA samples and never returns a common variant or one with a ClinVar P/LP or conflicting record. An auto-seed that seeds variants records one audit event naming them; one that seeds none records nothing. The import of the R pipeline's recurrent table reads only the rows flagged `filter_as_recurrent_artifact` (with their family count and profile), reads a plain allele list too, refuses a table without alleles (400), never lists a common allele or one ClinVar may call pathogenic (read from the pooled annotation), and records one audit event naming the file and the alleles it listed. |
| [backend/tests/test_nipt_package_import.py](../backend/tests/test_nipt_package_import.py) | Discovery/validation of NIPT family packages (manifest/PED, analysis-type tags). A parent pair with one VCF each: Discover proposes a NIPT manifest (`snv.per_sample`, the coverage tables as `target_table`, the plasma's assay) with the `nipt_pair_detected` warning; such a package validates; a per-sample SNV callset is refused outside a NIPT family, a per-sample VCF must hold one sample and a coverage table its columns. |
| [backend/tests/test_nipt_pair_import.py](../backend/tests/test_nipt_pair_import.py) | A monogenic NIPT pair, two one-sample Mutect2 VCFs (synthetic): the caller metrics kept with a call (TLOD, FS, the per-allele values with the reference's as `_REF`, the STR flag and repeat units, VarDict's; a missing value left out) and its FILTER values; a multi-sample record keeps none for its calls; the record filter leaves records out and counts them (`skipped_by_filter`); the paternal filter keeps a call from 15% alt reads (the allele depths before the caller's AF) or over a plasma call position (an MNV over any of its bases), and the plasma scan lists every base of an MNV; the plasma is the tagged sample, else the mother, and goes first; Discover finds a parent pair with one VCF each and the coverage tables, and no pair without both parents' VCFs, with one parent as both, or with a child that has a VCF of its own; the importer stores both files as one `nipt` callset, the plasma first and the paternal file filtered, and reports the bytes of both files read; a record the paternal filter leaves out has no annotation parsed. |
| [backend/tests/test_nipt_service.py](../backend/tests/test_nipt_service.py) | NIPT orchestration: observation building, filters, coverage, inheritance presets. The interval list, excluded intervals and excluded genes reach the variant load, and an unreadable interval is refused (422) before the cohort load (#615). The variant list reports the Summary's fetal fraction; the father's state follows the shared genotype classes, so a haploid chrX `1` sexes the fetus. Past its classification limit the variant list says so (`total_is_estimated`, `count_limit`) and classifies the same first variants; a list within the limit is exact. A de novo candidate carries its triage (window, score, priority); without a per-target table the coverage reads the plasma's coverage BED, its track of read depth, over the ROI (CLIN-46). A variant the list keeps by SIFT deleterious on its second transcript names that transcript beside its primary one, and none without the filter (CLIN-51). |
| [backend/tests/test_nipt_pair_service.py](../backend/tests/test_nipt_pair_service.py) | The NIPT service on a per-sample (two-VCF) callset (synthetic): the father's class from his allele depths (Mutect2 writes `0/1` whatever the fraction), his missing call read as absent; a split multi-allelic record's site depth counts every allele's reads; a paternal allele the plasma did not show, its depth from the target coverage; the father's MNV carrying a plasma SNV, a plasma MNV he calls in part, the other representation looked up in the family's records, and none for an indel; the triage annotation; the summary's paternal evidence and target coverage; the paternal and maternal views (with and without the alleles the fetus did not inherit; the paternal view lists an allele the plasma calls only in part), the recessive view's gene risks, the de novo view's triage and priority; a de novo candidate another pregnancy carries is left out as recurrent unless ClinVar may call it pathogenic; what protects a listed artefact (ClinVar, conflicting, common), and a listed allele the family's annotation protects stays in its analysis, flagged; a call failing the quality filter is listed with its reason and counted by reason; the quality-checks payload (paternity, the combined fetal sex, the plasma-profile verdicts: male DNA fails); the coverage from the target table; the coverage page's genes, passing ones too, and one gene's targets, read from the table alone. The summary serves the fetal-fraction and fetal-sex verdicts the page made itself before, from the backend (`nipt_quality_checks`). |
| [backend/tests/test_nipt_quality_checks.py](../backend/tests/test_nipt_quality_checks.py) | The NIPT quality checks' verdicts (REQ-NIPT-015; `services/nipt_quality_checks.py`, made once for the NIPT page and the signed record; synthetic values): no fetal-fraction estimate fails, a low-confidence one warns, a confident one passes, each with its sentence; fetal-sex signals that disagree fail, none that tells warns, one or both telling passes, naming which; the chrY coverage alone reads a male, or a female only at an FF of 4% or more; each sex profile of the plasma has its verdict, male DNA failing and no coverage unknown; paternity read as the Sample QC reads it; every check of a family's analysis assembled with its verdict, with and without per-target coverage. |

### Expanded carrier screening / gene panels
| Test file | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| [backend/tests/test_panel_filter_constraints.py](../backend/tests/test_panel_filter_constraints.py) | Panel-filter constraint fetch and application in variant queries; stored regions read for the family's own assembly only, and none without a resolved assembly (#515). |
| [backend/tests/test_panel_resolution_cache.py](../backend/tests/test_panel_resolution_cache.py) | A panel's resolution (its genes, its regions in the assembly, its genes' loci) kept per panel and assembly while its token is unchanged (ENG-14), over an in-memory stand-in for the tables: an unchanged panel served from its token alone, the same resolution, also in the CLIN-27 check of a broader ranking's panel; a panel edit, a change of its gene or region rows without a new version, and a gene-reference load each read it again; a resolution read while the panel changed is not kept; each assembly kept apart; nothing kept without an assembly; a deleted panel still 404; at most `_PANEL_RESOLUTION_CACHE_SIZE`, the most recently used; the digest kept with it; a kept resolution frozen; the gene-reference lookup binds any number of genes as one array parameter (asyncpg takes 32,767); genes kept once each in the order read. |
| [backend/tests/test_panel_filter_cost.py](../backend/tests/test_panel_filter_cost.py) | Applying a gene panel costs the panel once per search, not once per row (ENG-15). The record filter (`_kept_small_candidates`) casefolds the panel's gene names once per search instead of once per candidate, reads no region of a record a panel gene names, and finds the others in the regions by bisection (`_SmallPanelMatch`, `_RegionLookup`); it keeps exactly what testing each record against every region kept, over a grid of chromosome spellings, positions, gene spellings and annotation keys, 12,000 random region queries and a Mendeliome-sized panel, whose records annotated with another symbol are kept in their gene's locus and left out past it (owner decision 2026-10-14: a panel of more than 1,000 regions is matched by position too). The family page's panel-region condition is a join on 64-kb windows over the family's own rows (`_small_family_region_condition`), whose rows are narrowed by the family, its projects and the search's own locus clauses as the search's WHERE has them, and by nothing else, so it reads every row the search reads (seven loci, and a family without projects); without an assembly (no search reaches it then) the regions fall back to the per-row test; a Mendeliome-sized gene panel (5,300 genes) joins its regions beside its genes, the region arrays bound once; the regions of a chromosome that share a base are bound merged (`_small_region_bounds`: a gene's eight transcript loci and PanelApp's reversed span become its span, 47,700 loci bound as 5,300), and keep exactly what each region kept, over 24,000 random rows, a REF of no base included (regions that only touch stay apart); the intervals and the excluded regions keep their per-row test. |
| [backend/tests/test_panel_region_assembly_scope.py](../backend/tests/test_panel_region_assembly_scope.py) | Panel coordinates per assembly (#515): a gene resolved in every assembly that has it (GRCh38 first), counts of genes not rows, regions stored with their assembly, PanelApp coordinates kept for the requested assembly only and dropped (with a message) when it is not loaded. |
| [backend/tests/test_panel_metadata_service.py](../backend/tests/test_panel_metadata_service.py) | The panel-name uniqueness check: a new panel's name is looked up without an untyped NULL parameter, and an existing panel is left out of its own check by an explicit id filter. |
| [backend/tests/test_panelapp_service.py](../backend/tests/test_panelapp_service.py) | PanelApp import parsing and summary extraction (green genes, regions and STRs; amber entities on request); a panel's request path is built from its numeric id, so no `/` or traversal can enter it. |
| [backend/tests/test_gene_panel_versions.py](../backend/tests/test_gene_panel_versions.py) | Gene-panel maintenance and version history (REQ-CARR-003): the JSONB helpers; the Mendeliome takes Monarch's disease-causing and disease-associated predicates; a generated or imported panel cannot be edited by hand (400) and the refusal writes nothing, an update of a missing panel is 404, regenerating the Mendeliome without Monarch data is 409, and a viewer can neither regenerate nor update (403). Against an in-memory stand-in for the panel tables: creating a panel archives it as version 1 (its genes trimmed, once each, in the order given; their regions with the assembly; the description and the author) before the commit; an edit of the genes, or of the description alone, archives the next version and leaves the earlier one as it was, an edit that changes nothing archives nothing, and a change whose version cannot be archived is not committed; the history lists the versions newest first, for the panel id in braces and capitals too, and each reads back with its own genes, regions and description; an unknown version or panel is 404, an id that is no UUID 400; the Mendeliome is archived when built and again only when its genes change or a new version is forced (an unchanged refresh re-stamps its Monarch release); a PanelApp import archives version 1 (its genes as PanelApp gave them, PanelApp's coordinates and each gene in every loaded assembly, PanelApp's version, the options, the author) before the commit; importing it again with other genes (a gene with coordinates, or one no loaded assembly has), or with other regions only (a region entity taken in, PanelApp's coordinates of a gene moved, a gene found in another loaded assembly at the same coordinates), archives the next version with the panel's row locked, leaves the earlier one as it was and keeps PanelApp's version the release imported; an import that changes neither keeps the version, rewrites no gene or region and archives nothing, but records PanelApp's new version and the options; an import whose version cannot be archived is not committed; and the prioritised-ranking cache's exact key moves after an import of the same release that changes the genes, and stays after one that changes nothing. The same on real Postgres: `integration/test_gene_panel_version_history.py`. |

### Rare-disorder diagnostics (trio, SV, repeats, Paraphase, mtDNA)
| Test file | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| [backend/tests/test_de_novo_detection.py](../backend/tests/test_de_novo_detection.py) | Trio de-novo detection with confident hom-ref matching and full-trio requirement. |
| [backend/tests/test_hemizygous_de_novo.py](../backend/tests/test_hemizygous_de_novo.py) | A son's hemizygous call on chrX/chrY outside the PARs is a de novo candidate (#545, #620): haploid `1`, `1/1` or `0/1` with a confidently reference mother (X) or father (Y); the other parent need not be genotyped but must not carry the ALT; a duo suffices; the diploid rules in a PAR, in a daughter and off-scope; the de novo / dominant pattern, the SQL pre-filter (hemizygous branch only for an affected male on a known assembly) and the segregation mode. |
| [backend/tests/test_sv_gene_index.py](../backend/tests/test_sv_gene_index.py) | SV→gene index and SV second-hit (compound-het) summarization. The segregation phase counts only informative relatives: relatives carrying neither hit, or not recorded as parents, leave it unknown; a de novo deletion, a missing SV call or a shallow reference call places nothing; a male on chrX outside the PARs is not traced. Read phasing: a half call (one allele missing) of the SV or of the SNV places nothing, so a singleton stays unknown and a trio is phased by segregation, while fully called phased genotypes keep their read-backed trans/cis. Each SV of the gene gets its own phase, listed by position (CLIN-28): a deletion in trans wins over a duplication in cis in one phase block, a duplication in trans beside a deletion in cis is a trans candidate, two SVs in cis keep the gene cis (read-backed only when every one is), a deletion without read-backed phase leaves a singleton unknown and is placed by the parents in a trio; an unaffected parent's other SV does not put the deletion in cis, and in per-sample SV files a parent's own call stands in only for an SV of the same type within 1 kb; an SV no affected member carries gets no phase, and one an affected sibling lacks is not placed by segregation, while one SV's own cis in an affected sibling keeps it from unmasking; which types count as a deletion (DEL of any subtype, LOSS and CNV_LOSS; CNV unless its copy numbers show no loss, one copy kept by a male on chrX; never DUP of any subtype, GAIN, INS, INV, BND or TRA). The scan reads each call's copy number and lists an SV stored for several projects once. The index is rebuilt when the family's SV data version has moved since it was built, and reused while it has not. |
| [backend/tests/test_sv_copy_change.py](../backend/tests/test_sv_copy_change.py) | One rule for whether an SV removes a copy, adds one or neither (CLIN-47), `sv_copy_change.copy_change`: a DEL of any subtype (DEL:ME), LOSS or CNV_LOSS is a loss and a DUP of any subtype (DUP:TANDEM), GAIN or CNV_GAIN a gain, whatever copy numbers they store; a CNV is a loss when a carrier has fewer copies than usual there and else a gain when one has more, a reference call's copy number not read, and a CNV without a carrier's copy number a loss; a male keeps one copy of chrX and chrY outside the PARs (one copy is neither, none a loss, two a gain), two in a PAR, and a female or a sample without a pedigree two; INS, INV, BND and TRA change none. The SV ranking (its candidate read faked) scores on it: a DEL:ME and a CNV with one copy as deletions, haploinsufficient in a gene with pLI 0.99, a CNV with three copies and a DUP:TANDEM as duplications, a son's chrX CNV with one copy as neither and his mother's as a loss, a DEL and a DUP as before; the SNV + SV second-hit badge counts the same SVs as copy losses. The SV list serves each SV with the same change (`copy_change`), and INS, INS:ME, INV, BND, TRA and CPX with none; a track reads its own sample's call (its read faked), so one CNV with one copy in the mother and three in her son is a loss on her track and in the family's list and a gain on his, and his chrX CNV keeping his one copy none (CLIN-54). |
| [backend/tests/test_structural_variant_length_type_filter.py](../backend/tests/test_structural_variant_length_type_filter.py) | The SV list's type and length filters (REQ-PGT-007): a type filter keeps the types asked for, a comma-separated list in any capitals, a subtype (`DUP:TANDEM`, `INS:ME:ALU`) kept by its type; a minimum length keeps the SVs at least that long, the bound included, an exact length those of that length, and with a type both must hold. A search with either filter is not answered from the native page, whose SQL applies neither: the list, its total and its summary by type and caller hold the matching SVs alone. A deletion over 10 Mb is found by a 10 Mb minimum and listed with its length. What a length filter measures (CLIN-25): a call's SVLEN without its sign, END minus POS only without one, so an insertion written with END at POS and SVLEN 5000 passes a 50 bp minimum and an exact 5000, as does a deletion with SVLEN -5000 stored without END; a translocation (BND, TRA, in any capitals, told by its type, not by a missing length) passes any minimum and matches no exact length, whatever its SVLEN or END; a call without SVLEN whose END is its POS is 0 bp. The same from the rows as ClickHouse returns them to the candidate read (SVLEN from the details row): the list, unranked and ranked, its total and summary, its CSV export, the genome track and the track availability; and for one call of each importer (a Sniffles2 or pbsv, Spectre or manual TSV upload; a NeedlR, HiFiCNV or chrM Sniffles2 package dataset), which stores the SVLEN its caller wrote. |
| [backend/tests/test_mitochondrial_analysis.py](../backend/tests/test_mitochondrial_analysis.py) | mtDNA variant collection, heteroplasmy, maternal transmission, review attachment. A conflicting ClinVar record makes the clinical status uncertain, not pathogenic. The maternal transmission follows the mother and father the pedigree links to the proband, and the proband's maternal sibship: in a three-generation pedigree the paternal grandmother (role "mother" after a PED import) is not the mother, nor the paternal grandfather the father. A proband's variant is "not assessed" in the mother when the pedigree links none, when she has no mtDNA data, or when she has no call at that site, and "not seen in mother" only where she was sequenced and lacks it; a couple without a proband has no maternal line (#758). The response lists the chrM structural variants (a deletion with its genes) with each sample's heteroplasmy (#759). |
| [backend/tests/test_coverage_readers_read_depth.py](../backend/tests/test_coverage_readers_read_depth.py) | The readers that take coverage as read depth read one track of read depth per sample (CLIN-46), against a fake store that keeps each sample's rows by source and returns every source's rows merged when a reader names none. The mtDNA coverage reads the proband's coverage BED, not her WisecondorX bins, the father's HiFiCNV depths stored as read, not his QDNAseq bins, and for the mother, whose only track is HiFiCNV's log2 ratio, her calls' depth; with `mtdna.mean_depth` limits configured each passes on its depth (before: each failed on a blend). A family whose only chrM track is WisecondorX's bins has no mtDNA coverage, count-only or full, and says so; a mother whose only chrM track is a log2 ratio is not taken as sequenced ("mother not assessed"). The NIPT coverage check without a per-target table reads the plasma's coverage BED, not its WisecondorX bins (the BED's median and its uncovered stretch), and reports no coverage from a HiFiCNV log2 track alone. Each coverage BED declares what it holds (TRACE-9; the store's records keep it): a BED declaring nothing, declared unknown or declared as a log2 ratio is read by neither reader as depth (the mtDNA coverage falls back on the calls' depth, the NIPT check reports no coverage; before, it was read as depth), and each reader names every track it leaves out, with the reason (the mtDNA QC notes, the NIPT check's notes). |
| [backend/tests/test_coverage_read_depth.py](../backend/tests/test_coverage_read_depth.py) | What each coverage track holds and which one a depth reader takes (`coverage_read_depth`, CLIN-46, TRACE-9): a coverage BED (`web`) holds what its record declares (`read_depth` or `log2_ratio`), and is unknown when it declares nothing, declares `unknown`, or two records disagree, whatever transform it names (before: read as depth); a track whose records all name `none` holds read depth, one named only with log2-ratio transforms a log2 ratio, one under a transform not known there or two that disagree is unknown; without a record WisecondorX's and QDNAseq's are log2 ratios and an unknown caller's is unknown; a declaration is read only of a BED; what a manifest or an upload declares maps to `read_depth`, `log2_ratio` or `unknown`. One track per sample: the BED declared as depth before another depth track, else the first by name, none with log2 ratios or unknowns only; each track left out is named with the reason (a log2 ratio, an undeclared BED, an unrecorded caller). The sources come from the stored rows on the chromosomes read and the transforms and declarations from their records (`metadata ->> 'content'` read as a bound key), which are not read when no sample has coverage there. |
| [backend/tests/test_coverage_bed_declares_content.py](../backend/tests/test_coverage_bed_declares_content.py) | Each coverage BED declares what it holds, read depth or a log2 ratio (TRACE-9; the stores faked at their edge). The BED upload route refuses a coverage BED without `content`, and `content` on another track type, with 422 before anything is read; the declaration is a query parameter with two values. What a BED declares is stored in its track's record and its rows' metadata, and the upload's record for the family (SAFE-19) carries it; another track type's records declare nothing. A package's coverage entry stores its manifest's `content`, and an entry without it as `unknown`, each sample's result naming it; what the import records for the family says each sample's declaration, one sample at a time, a later record of a sample's BED replacing the earlier declaration, an older record without one read as it was, and no overwrite comparing it (a re-upload with another declaration strikes the BED off a part-way restore's entry). The validation warns of a BED without `content` (`coverage_content_undeclared`) and refuses one that is neither value (`coverage_content_invalid`); a per-target table declares nothing. The track availability serves what each coverage track holds (`coverage_contents`). |
| [backend/tests/test_paraphase_pg.py](../backend/tests/test_paraphase_pg.py) | Paraphase SMN metrics (copy number, read counts, haplotype groups); the medical-region catalogue's aliases and clinical fields. A copy-number change is judged against each region's own norm times the copies the sample carries (#756): a man's single X (IKBKG, F8 int22h, the opsin array) reads normal, the same values in a woman are a loss, and an unknown sex on chrX is not called normal; NEB at 6 and a gene + pseudogene pair at 4 are no change; an exploratory region is judged on its gene copy number only; an allele-dependent region with a no-call reports a no-call; every catalogue region carries a norm and a chromosome. |
| [backend/tests/test_repeat_expansion_pg.py](../backend/tests/test_repeat_expansion_pg.py) | Repeat-expansion family table storage/retrieval (TRGT). A per-sample upload reads the target's column of a family VCF, binds a lone placeholder column to the target, and refuses a file whose columns name another sample (family member or another family's) or name the target twice, before any write. |
| [backend/tests/test_presence_count_only.py](../backend/tests/test_presence_count_only.py) | `count_only` presence mode for repeat/Paraphase tables. |
| [backend/tests/test_structural_variant_breakends.py](../backend/tests/test_structural_variant_breakends.py) | Manual SV record parsing incl. remote breakend partners. |
| [backend/tests/test_sv_export_mate_columns.py](../backend/tests/test_sv_export_mate_columns.py) | The SV CSV export names a breakend's mate (DATA-5). Through `GET /families/{family_id}/structural-variants/export` and the real SV search, unranked (the native page), filtered (a minimum length) and ranked: the header holds the SV list's *Remote chr* and *Remote start* beside the locus, after *End* (the ranked file after its *Priority* and *Rank*); a NeedlR breakend as the package import stores it and a Sniffles2 breakend as the upload stores it export each its own mate, the mate's chromosome written as the *Chromosome* column writes one; a deletion from either leaves both empty. |
| [backend/tests/test_sv_list_export_filters.py](../backend/tests/test_sv_list_export_filters.py) | The family SV list and its CSV export read one set of filters (ENG-2): both routes take all 26 SV filters from one dependency (`_family_structural_variant_filters`), after the sign-in, and declare none themselves; the export's query parameters are the list's without its page and genome-track options, each filter under the same name, type, default and alias on both. A request that sets every filter hands both searches the same `StructuralVariantQueryFilters`, each field set (a field no parameter sets fails the test), and the list's `overlap` reaches the list's alone. For each filter, and ranked, the list and the CSV hold the same SVs in the same order, some of the family's but not all: the real SV search over seven synthetic SVs, ClickHouse, the reviews, the gene's loci and the panel stood in for. An unreadable sample-filter number is refused after the 401 and the control-character 400, and with a 422 before the family lookup, as a filter of the wrong type is. |

### Variant classification (ACMG / CNV)
| Test file | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| [backend/tests/test_acmg_classification.py](../backend/tests/test_acmg_classification.py) | ACMG points, ClinGen thresholds, BA1 override, VUS tiers, no-evidence→VUS fail-safe. The server normalises a saved classification: it recomputes the class and points and ignores the client's total, and refuses an unknown criterion or strength, a criterion listed twice (whatever its case or spacing, accepted or not) and a strength the criterion does not take (BS1 or PM2 at very strong, PVS1 or BS1 at stand-alone, BA1 at strong), on an unaccepted criterion too (CLIN-3); a stored classification those rules refuse is still served as written. A stored classification that cannot be read is marked on the small-variant and the SV/CNV review instead of dropped, and logged without its values; a stored JSON null is no classification (#541). |
| [backend/tests/test_cnv_acmg_points.py](../backend/tests/test_cnv_acmg_points.py) | ClinGen-2019 CNV point system, class thresholds, empty/missing-flag/malformed handling. |
| [backend/tests/test_acmg_frontend_parity.py](../backend/tests/test_acmg_frontend_parity.py) | The server's criterion tables match the classification dialogs' (CLIN-3): it reads `frontend/src/lib/acmg/criteria.ts` and `frontend/src/lib/cnvAcmg/criteria.ts` and fails when they differ from `acmg_points.py` (the 28 codes, their directions, the strength points, each criterion's allowed strengths) or `cnv_acmg_points.py` (the loss and gain catalogues: codes, sections, default, minimum and maximum points). A save takes every strength the dialog offers a criterion, its default included, and refuses every other; it keeps every point value the CNV dialog offers. |

### Clinical traceability & audit
| Test file | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| [backend/tests/test_annotation_manifest.py](../backend/tests/test_annotation_manifest.py) | Annotation/version-manifest layer merge and canonical ordering; VCF-header refresh-merge semantics, including a re-import whose header names a tool only in a command line, which keeps the recorded version, and a PGT header's `##source=SHAPEIT5 phase_common 1.1`, which adds no version beside the pipeline record's 5.1.1; the reference-layer modules (assembly, gene loci, Monarch and HPO releases), each recorded as `unavailable` when its lookup fails, and HPO also when the loaded ontology has no release. A replacement is admin-only and writes one clinical audit event, with the replaced and the new manifest, before its single commit; an import and a replacement take the same per-family lock before they read, and an import leaves a manual manifest alone. |
| [backend/tests/test_vcf_header_provenance.py](../backend/tests/test_vcf_header_provenance.py) | VCF/TRGT header provenance parsing — caller/engine/DB versions (VEP/snpEff/bcftools/GATK/DeepVariant/Sniffles/Spectre/TRGT) and cross-modality merge. A version comes only from a line that states one: a command line (`##bcftools_viewCommand=view …`, `##SnpSiftCmd`, `##trgtCommand`, `##HiPhase_command`, `##paraphase_command`) records its tool without a version, and the version line gives it whether it comes before or after the command line or in another file of the merge; a version line gives its first version-shaped word (`5.1d` from SnpSift's `"SnpSift 5.1d (build …)"`), a structured one (DRAGEN; GATK 3's unquoted field, not its `version=false` argument; Sentieon) its `Version` field, and a value without one only when it is a single word with a digit that is not the tool's name (`r0.8`). `##source` gives a version only where it states one apart from the tool's name, after a `_` or `-` (`Sniffles2_2.2`, `cuteSV-1.0.13`) or as the second word (`pbsv 2.9.0`, `freeBayes v1.3.6`, HiFiCNV, `GLIMPSE_phase`), never a digit of the name (`Clair3`, `GLIMPSE2`, `Sniffles2`, `Mutect2`, `SHAPEIT5`, the demo's `CoGADemoFamilyClair3VEP`), SHAPEIT5's third word, a number glued to the name (`shapeit4.1.3`) or LoFreq's command line; Clair3 and Mutect2 make one module with their version lines, and Strelka's `##source_version` gives the version of the one tool `##source` names, none beside two. A VEP tab (TSV) header gives the cache, assembly, GENCODE and database versions (ClinVar, gnomAD, dbSNP, COSMIC, SIFT, PolyPhen, HGMD) without its noise or column descriptions; an SV VCF's `##INFO` descriptions give the versions of the databases it was annotated with; an empty header is safe. |
| [backend/tests/test_classification_drift.py](../backend/tests/test_classification_drift.py) | Evidence-snapshot build and drift detection across annotation versions. A missing annotation hash on either side reads `unknown`, never `current`, so it cannot clear the sign-out drift gate. The snapshot freezes the primary transcript's population frequencies and in-silico scores (CLIN-7), every field also without a value, exactly as the variant is served to the ACMG dialog, and a value JSONB cannot hold as none; a changed REVEL score or gnomAD frequency, also under an unchanged annotation-set hash, and a score that appears later, read as drift naming the value with its value from and to; values read back from JSONB, and numbers whatever their encoding, compare by value; a snapshot from before CLIN-7 stays current, only the fields a snapshot froze are compared, and a frozen group that cannot be read is `unknown`. The snapshot also freezes the primary transcript's consequence, impact and LOFTEE call, as served to the dialog, and, for a missense variant only (its consequence includes missense_variant, as the dialog tells one for PP2), the gene's missense Z read through the variant list's own gene lookup (the same gene, the newest profile, one query for a drift check's missense variants; none without a gene, a NaN as none; a stop gained's or an unannotated variant's gene not read); a changed consequence, impact or LoF call, and a missense Z moved by a gene profile refresh under an unchanged hash, read as drift naming each in the order a reviewer reads them; the same refresh is no drift for a stop gained (owner, 2026-10-20); a missense variant no longer called one names its missense Z as gone; a snapshot frozen before them is compared on what it holds, and an unreadable consequence or constraint group is `unknown`. |
| [backend/tests/test_clinical_audit.py](../backend/tests/test_clinical_audit.py) | Clinical-audit diff generation (classification/tags/notes), one event per change; for structural-variant / CNV reviews too: the reviewer's classification and the CNV (ClinGen) scoring (class, kind, points, accepted criteria) in one classification event, a deleted review recorded, point values never recorded as -0.0 (which would break the chain after the JSONB round-trip), SV events marked `modality: sv`. The classification event holds the whole stored record (every criterion's strength or points, acceptance, evidence and suggestion flag, the total and the VUS tier): a strength, points, evidence or suggestion change is recorded with the class unchanged and named in the summary (a long list shortened), a class change keeps its headline, and an unchanged re-save of the full record writes nothing. The trail is listed a page at a time (AUDIT-1): newest first in one total order (`created_at`, then `id`), with the trail's count from the page's own statement and one event more read to say whether older ones remain (`has_more`); the next page starts after the event `before` names, looked up by its canonical id within the family, and an unknown, another family's or a malformed id is answered 404 before any page is read; an empty trail is listed as empty; the page size is bounded (1–500) and read as a number; the route passes `limit` and `before` on and refuses a page size out of bounds with 422. |
| [backend/tests/test_phenotype_edit_audit.py](../backend/tests/test_phenotype_edit_audit.py) | Each change to a family's phenotypes is on its clinical audit trail (SEC-1; synthetic IDs, a session that answers the service's statements, `record_clinical_event` recorded): a phenotype added is one `phenotype` event with no before and the annotation after (member, term and label, status, onset, evidence, source, note), the family, its author and the member and annotation in its metadata, written before the one commit; each change waits for the family's last; adding a term and status the member has records nothing, or the change when a field differs; a change is one event with before and after and a summary that names the term, status and text fields that changed; a change that changes nothing records nothing; a removal is one event with no after; removing an unknown phenotype is 404 and records nothing; the audited state is text or none. |
| [backend/tests/test_review_concurrency.py](../backend/tests/test_review_concurrency.py) | Optimistic concurrency for review saves (#513): a save against the loaded version goes ahead; an older version, a racing first write and a removed review are refused with 409 and the current review; a client without a version keeps the unconditional write; a stale small-variant or SV save writes nothing, and the per-variant lock is taken before the read. |
| [backend/tests/test_report_signout.py](../backend/tests/test_report_signout.py) | Sign-out canonical content-hash (order-independent), drift gate (acknowledgement needs a reason), versioning, reported SV/CNV frozen into the snapshot, the report-vs-latest-sign-out content check (#508) — a record signed before CoGA added a reference module still verifies, is not called changed and is said not to hold it, while an HPO ontology loaded or replaced after sign-out is a change — and the assembly-scope gate: an unvalidated or unresolved assembly is refused before anything is built or written, whatever else is acknowledged (#515). The incomplete-import gate: a family flagged `import_incomplete` by a partly failed package import is refused (409 naming what failed; nothing written or audited) unless acknowledged with a reason (422 without); the flag (with the import job that holds each dataset's error) and the reason are frozen in the hashed snapshot and recorded in the audit event, and the gate is acknowledged independently of the drift and QC gates. A flag written before the job was recorded still gates. The import state is bound into the content hash and compared by the sign-out check; a sign-out stored before the gate (pinned, with the hashes its writer computed) still verifies. Reading any signed version, like the sign-out response, names what its record could not capture (a failed lookup, or a reference module added after it was signed; a record that names no looked-up modules has each one it lacks named); a snapshot a driver hands back as text is verified and served as the record. The SV/CNV evidence drift: a reported structural variant or CNV whose frozen evidence changed, or that froze none, blocks sign-out through the same drift gate and acknowledgement (one gate with the small variants), and the drift and each reported SV's evidence are frozen into the hashed snapshot and the audit event; a sign-out stored before SV/CNV evidence was frozen (pinned, with the hashes its writer computed) still verifies; the sign-out check does not compare the SV/CNV drift section it lacks but compares its reported SVs whole, so evidence frozen since reads as a change (#681). A family whose data is being written is refused with no override and nothing written: an import job of it queued, validating or running (`import_in_progress`, looked up by the family identifier), a writer holding its variant-write locks (`variant_writes_in_progress`), or an import claimed while the snapshot is read (the second job check); the locks are shared before the snapshot and before the sign-out chain's lock (#727). An import that stopped part-way, with no flag but its `import_unfinished` entry, is refused through the same gate (409 naming the job and the datasets it had not finished, with "overwrite"; nothing written) unless acknowledged; the entries are frozen into the hashed snapshot (`{}` when none) and the audit event, and with a failed import's flag make one gate and one acknowledgement; the entry reader is deterministic and fails safe (a value of another shape still gates), and the sign-out check names an import that stopped since sign-out. The flag reader freezes each failed dataset's job (`failed_jobs`, else the flag's job), and the gate message names each job when an earlier failure was kept with a later one. Each reason of a Sample-QC refusal names what its check read (CLIN-8): the sample ("Sample X: no chrX genotypes available…", two partners without chrX calls no longer the same sentence twice), the pair, the child with its parents, the NIPT father, as the QC produces the checks, in the list, the message and the prompt's summary of failing checks; fetal sex and the maternal lineage name none; the acknowledged sign-out's audit event records the named reasons while the snapshot keeps each check's own message, so a record signed before compares the same; an abbreviation or a name keeps its capital after the name, and a check without its message or its samples still gives a reason. The signed analysis record (owner decisions of 2026-10-14): the snapshot freezes what the analysis looked at (variant descriptions, gene panels applied, reference-data releases, repeat expansions, Paraphase) and the sentence that says what the record is; each section is bound into the content hash and compared by the check, and a record signed before them still verifies and reads, with nothing it predates called not captured; a sign-out carries its report code (in the response, the audit event and a listed version), and a code another record has is never given again (signed a microsecond later, under the report-code lock); a code finds its record as typed back, a typo is refused with 422, and an unknown code and one whose family is outside the caller's projects (or deleted, or re-created under its identifier) answer the same 404; the lookup route. A PGT family's embryo calls (REG-2): frozen into the hashed record, named on the trail when not captured, compared by the check (an embryo's call changed, or the family's embryos gone, is a change), named as not compared against a PGT family's record signed before the section, and not named at all for a family without embryos. A NIPT family's record: a failed NIPT quality check (no fetal-fraction estimate, fetal-sex signals that disagree, male DNA as the maternal plasma) gates the sign-out under the Sample QC's acknowledgement, named in `nipt_failed_checks`, beside a failed Sample QC too, frozen with its reason and audited; checks that passed, warned, could not run or could not be read do not gate; the NIPT section is bound into the content hash and compared; a NIPT record signed before it names it not compared; a family that is no NIPT family is gated, audited and checked as before, the check naming no NIPT section; NIPT results not captured are named by the check and the audit event. |

| [backend/tests/test_signed_record_sections.py](../backend/tests/test_signed_record_sections.py) | What the signed record freezes of what the analysis looked at (`services/signed_record_sections.py`): each reported variant described as the report's own variant lists serve it (gene, transcript, HGVS c. and p., consequence, impact, MANE, each sample's genotype with depth, allele depths and fraction; an SV's type, copy change, locus, length, genes, cytoband and each sample's copy number), one entry per callset or caller, none for a variant not reported, no list read without a reported variant; the gene reference's release per source for the reported genes, a partial refresh named, genes it lacks named, symbols bound as one array; the clinical CNV knowledgebase by its latest load, or none; each TRGT call with its status and thresholds and the catalogue's digest, which moves with a threshold; each Paraphase result with its status, the region's rule and norm and the catalogue; a section that cannot be read frozen as a fixed sentence (never the error's text) and named in `not_captured`, the others read each in a savepoint of its own, a reference-data part named on its own, a section a record predates no gap; what a section cannot use (no described gene, no assembly, a gene-reference row for a gene not looked up, a count that is not one, a region without a norm) left out, never guessed.
