A NIPT family holds its NIPT section (read in a savepoint of its own, for its reported variants; its analysis type read as a bound parameter); a family that is no NIPT family holds no such key; NIPT results that cannot be read are frozen as unavailable, without the error's text, and named. |
| [backend/tests/test_signed_record_nipt.py](../backend/tests/test_signed_record_nipt.py) | The NIPT section of the signed analysis record (`services/signed_record_nipt.py`; REG-2; synthetic per-sample callset at an FF of 20%): the section says what the NIPT page showed, computed by the same code (the fetal fraction, the de novo window, paternity, the fetal sex, the plasma sample and the quality failures as the summary endpoint serves them; the category counts and the funnel; the target coverage with its weak genes); each reported variant frozen as the NIPT variant list classifies it (category, probabilities, flags, the plasma's and the father's reads, a paternal allele's plasma depth from the targets); a reported de novo candidate with its triage as the de novo view gives it, a recurrent one with `excluded_recurrent`, and a maternal or paternal allele outside the fetal window with none; a reported variant the analysis does not classify, with why (artifact list, no plasma call, not in the data); the recessive fetal risk of the reported variants' genes as the recessive view gives it; the inputs (the target tables read with bound sample names, the artifact list's entries, its alleles' digest, what it removed and what the family's annotation kept, the model); a failed check computed from the data frozen with its verdict and gating; JSON-safe (no NaN, no negative zero), deterministic and hashing the same after the JSONB round trip; no coverage check and no sex profile without a target table; a family is a NIPT family by its analysis type; the trio and the artifact-list scope resolved from the family's active members (the cfDNA tag, else the mother); an unresolved trio frozen as such; each NIPT check that failed gates the sign-out, naming the plasma sample, and nothing gates for checks that passed, warned, could not run or could not be read. |
| [backend/tests/test_pgt_embryo_calls.py](../backend/tests/test_pgt_embryo_calls.py) | The PGT embryo call at the ROI as the backend makes it (`services/pgt_embryo_calls.py`, REQ-PGT-005): every case of `frontend/src/lib/__tests__/fixtures/pgtEmbryoCallCases.json` (every model; the disease haplotype resolved or not; affected, carrier, unaffected and uninformative calls; missing data at the ROI under every model, a grey or unconfirmed lane, a gap, a block over part of the ROI or off its chromosome; an obligate carrier and a member recorded as no carrier; an X-linked call of an unrecorded sex with both calls; a son in PAR1, across its boundary and where the PARs are not known; a model assumed from the carriers) answered as the browser's `haplotypeRisk.ts` answers it in `haplotypeRisk.test.ts`; the embryos called with the homologs they carry at the ROI; a block boundary inside the ROI or within 250 kb of it a recombination, one past the flank or on another chromosome not; a parent's phase correction near the ROI, up to 2 Mb before it, found and read as the family's metadata holds it; a model of no mode calls no one. |
| [backend/tests/test_pgt_analysis.py](../backend/tests/test_pgt_analysis.py) | The PGT analysis the family page shows and a sign-out freezes (`services/pgt_analysis.py`): each embryo called from the blocks the haplotype view reads, over the ROI and 1 Mb on each side, with the homologs it carries at the ROI, the recombination inside it and its phasing QC as the ROI marker review counts it over the ROI; the father's phase correction next to the ROI named and one far away not; the members, the haplotype files (a sample no longer a member left out) and a digest of the blocks read; the same analysis whatever order the store returns the blocks in, and a digest that moves with a block; without an ROI nothing read and no embryo called; without haplotypes to read no embryo called; an ROI with more sites than the marker review reads counts no QC and keeps the calls; the model as recorded (a driver's text too) or assumed; an ROI without a locus is none; a PGT family is one with an embryo. |
| [backend/tests/test_panel_use_audit.py](../backend/tests/test_panel_use_audit.py) | The gene panels a family's searches apply, on its clinical audit trail (REQ-CARR-003): the first search with a panel version records `panel_applied` with the panel's id, name, local version, gene count and the PanelApp (or Monarch) release, looked up again under the family's audit-chain lock, then committed; the same version again records nothing, a new version or PanelApp release records again, a second search that recorded it first under the lock leaves nothing; a value naming no panel or an unknown panel records nothing; the signed record lists each version once with its first use, sorted, an event read back as JSON text the same and one that is not JSON naming no panel; the small-variant and SV lists and exports and the NIPT coverage record the panel before they run. |
| [backend/tests/test_structural_variant_evidence.py](../backend/tests/test_structural_variant_evidence.py) | The evidence an SV/CNV classification rests on: saving a CNV scoring freezes the inputs the classification reads (type, genes and gene count, pLI, annotated inheritance), the event (source, locus) and a hash of the SV's annotation, with the versions of the SV callset and the reference gene loci; the frozen values are the ones the SV page serves; a save that leaves the scoring out keeps them, a re-sent scoring refreezes them, a cleared one clears them, an SV not in the data is refused (404, nothing written). The drift check: unchanged is current, a moved input is drifted and named, a missing SV is `variant_missing`, unreadable frozen evidence is `unknown` (never current), and only the fields a snapshot froze are compared. Two callers of one SV (TRACE-11, a NeedlR row and an upload row with the lower storage key): a scoring freezes the row of the caller it names (its pLI, inheritance and genes); one naming no caller takes the SV's one row and is refused when two callers share the id (422, nothing written); one naming a caller without a row is refused (404); a save without a scoring reads no row; the drift check reads each snapshot's own caller's row, so a change to the other caller's row is no drift, one to its own is, its own row gone is `variant_missing` while the other caller still calls the SV, and evidence naming no caller is `unknown`; the SV review request takes the caller, the small-variant one does not. |
| [backend/tests/test_assembly_scope.py](../backend/tests/test_assembly_scope.py) | Validated-assembly scope (TF-06 H12, #515): GRCh38 inside by default, T2T/GRCh37/aliases/unknown outside, the scope follows `VALIDATED_ASSEMBLIES`, an empty scope validates nothing, and the refusal message names the assembly and the scope. |
| [backend/tests/test_hash_chain.py](../backend/tests/test_hash_chain.py) | Tamper-evidence hash-chain primitives (#261): canonical determinism, genesis anchoring, and `verify_chain` flagging content tampering / deletion / reordering. |
| [backend/tests/test_integrity_anchor.py](../backend/tests/test_integrity_anchor.py) | Signed chain-head anchor (#263): Ed25519 sign/verify round-trip, unsigned fallback, deterministic head sort + order-independent anchor_root, `signed_core` isoformat. |
| [backend/tests/test_audit_log_pg.py](../backend/tests/test_audit_log_pg.py) | HTTP audit-log event JSONB serialization/storage; a value Postgres cannot store (a NUL in the path, query string, body, entity id or error; half a surrogate pair; `NaN`) is escaped and its column named in `request_meta._escaped`, and a clean row carries no flag. |
| [backend/tests/test_pg_storable.py](../backend/tests/test_pg_storable.py) | What Postgres cannot store, made storable and visible (`core/pg_storable.py`): a NUL becomes `\x00` and half a surrogate pair `\udXXX`, in values and keys at any depth; `NaN`/`Infinity` become strings; only the columns where that happened are named; everything Postgres stores (other control characters, a whole pair, big numbers) is left as it came; the serialised row reads back with no refused token or character. |
| [backend/tests/test_event_pipeline.py](../backend/tests/test_event_pipeline.py) | Audit/UI-event durability (TF-13 S-5): backpressure + synchronous fallback on a full queue, bounded batch-write retry, and never-silent drop accounting; a failed batch is logged by its error type, not by the error's text, which repeats the rows. An event written at once, without the queue (`AUDIT_LOG_MODE=sync`, or no worker running), is not counted when stored; when its write fails, it is counted and logged with its payload, not raised. After a batch's last failed attempt its events are written one at a time, each once: a row the database refuses (a foreign key) costs only itself, in both workers, and the rows stored are not logged; a failure of every row adds one write per event and counts each event once; a database that cannot be reached (refused connection, timeout, no free pooled connection; also the real writer and driver against a closed port) ends that pass after one write, and a one-event batch is not written again. |
| [backend/tests/test_small_variant_review_pg.py](../backend/tests/test_small_variant_review_pg.py) | Small-variant review persistence and payload serialization; clearing a review (the row deleted, or kept for its compound-het pairing) is recorded in the clinical audit trail in the same transaction, and so is a re-classification that changes only a criterion's strength. A review list selects every field a review is served with (the ACMG record included) and serves it as a single-review read does, and binds its variant ids as one text[] parameter (DATA-3); the review summary counts a review that holds only an ACMG record or a compound-het pairing. A save keeps a tag the review holds whatever became of its definition (a deleted tag, on the variant or on its compound-het pair) and reads the family's tags only when it adds one; a tag it adds that the family cannot use is refused, named alone. |
| [backend/tests/test_structural_variant_filter_presets.py](../backend/tests/test_structural_variant_filter_presets.py) | Structural-variant filter presets through the route, with its real body and answer models and the real service on a session that answers as Postgres would: the body the SV page sends saves a preset for the family (its UUID and `scope: 'family'` bound, the name and description trimmed), a reusable one (`scope: 'global'`) is saved for no family, a body without a scope is for the family, and the answer carries the scope and family; an unknown or null scope, an empty name (422) and a blank one (400) are refused before any statement; the list serves each preset's scope and family. Before, the route read its body as a small-variant preset, which has no scope since #681, and every save answered 500. |
| [backend/tests/test_structural_variant_review_pg.py](../backend/tests/test_structural_variant_review_pg.py) | Structural-variant reviews and their CNV (ClinGen 2019) classification (#526): the class and points are recomputed server-side, clamped per criterion, only accepted criteria count, unknown codes and kinds refused, and a code listed twice refused (CLIN-3); unreadable stored classifications flagged; the save path (insert, update in place, delete when emptied, stale save refused, unknown tag refused, lock before read; a tag the review holds kept after its definition is deleted, only a tag the save adds checked, and a deleted tag, once removed, not added back); a save that leaves out the CNV scoring keeps it, an unreadable one or one that lists a criterion twice included, while a sent scoring replaces it and null or no criteria clears it; a scoring that lists a criterion twice is refused before any write; every change the save makes (classification, CNV scoring, tags, note, deletion) is recorded in the clinical audit trail on a chain that verifies, and an unchanged or refused save, or a kept scoring, records nothing; a rescoring that changes only a criterion's evidence is recorded. |
| [backend/tests/test_small_variant_review_tags.py](../backend/tests/test_small_variant_review_tags.py) | Variant tag definitions (#526): key and colour validation, admin-only creation and deletion, a label that reads as a built-in tag (by its key or its label) or as another active tag refused, a new tag whose slug another tag holds as its key given a numbered key (never a built-in one), project scope and shared projects, listing order and project filtering, deleted tags listed only on request and flagged inactive; an edit keeps the key and changes the label only; deletion as deactivation that keeps the project links; every create, edit and delete under the definitions lock. |

### Access control & security
| Test file | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| [backend/tests/test_access_control.py](../backend/tests/test_access_control.py) | Project-scoped RBAC: viewer/admin access, cross-project visibility. The checkpoint refuses a family or sample outside the caller's projects with the status, body and headers an unknown one gets (404 `Family not found` / `Sample not found`), and the Family Builder does the same for another team's project (404 `Project not found`); through the app the two responses are identical, headers included, and only the hidden family's audit row carries `request_meta.record_hidden` (REQ-SEC-001). |
| [backend/tests/test_request_control_characters.py](../backend/tests/test_request_control_characters.py) | A control character (C0 or DEL) in a path parameter, a query parameter's name or a `family_id`/`sample_id` query value, and a NUL in any query value, is refused at sign-in (`dependencies.get_current_user` → `family_identifiers.request_value_problem`), through the real `get_current_user` with a signed token (synthetic IDs): a NUL, tab, line break, escape or DEL in a family's path, a `family_id`/`sample_id` query value or a query parameter's name is answered 400 before the family is looked up, the message naming the parameter and writing the character as an escape (a family or sample ID's message states the ID rule); a gene and an interval list typed one per line, and a tab in a list, still reach the search, a NUL in one does not and is named though a line break comes first; the answer is the same for a viewer and an admin; a missing, forged or unknown token still gets its 401 first; an admin route refuses it before the role check; the refusal's audit row names the caller; a request without one (a space included) reaches the route; and every operation with a path or query value signs its caller in, but for the QC-report download, which checks its own signed link first (REQ-SEC-001). |
| [backend/tests/test_access_rules.py](../backend/tests/test_access_rules.py) | The project-scoped access rules in `services/access_control.py` (#528): which roles are admins, the projects an admin or a member may see, and that a non-member may not see a record linked to them (its caller then answers 404, as for an unknown one). The access levels (SEC-1): a member is an analyst in each project where they are no reader; an analyst may change a record and a reader may not, though they may see it; a record of two projects may be changed by an analyst of either; a non-member changes nothing and a record of no project is an admin's; an admin and a superuser change every record whatever their memberships; a request changes records unless it is a GET, HEAD or OPTIONS or its route is marked `changes_only_own_data`, which leaves the route as it was; the change check refuses a reader with 403 and its reason and lets a read, an analyst and an admin pass; the audit trail is refused a reader. |
| [backend/tests/test_project_access_levels.py](../backend/tests/test_project_access_levels.py) | Project access levels (SEC-1): an analyst reads and changes a project's records, a reader only reads them (synthetic IDs; the real checkpoints and the real `get_current_user` with a signed token, the user lookup, the family rows, the session and the routes' services replaced). The family, sample and project (Family Builder) checkpoints let a reader read and refuse their change with 403 and the reason, still answer a family outside the reader's projects like an unknown one (404, visibility first), let a mixed member change where they are an analyst (a family of two projects included) and an admin or superuser change every family. Through the app: a reader is refused a phenotype added, changed or removed (403 with the reason, the service never reached), a small-variant and a structural-variant review, sign-out, the workflow fields and a new family in their project, and the clinical audit trail, while an analyst reads it; an analyst and an admin change phenotypes, and each change reaches the service with the family's ID and its author; a reader reads the phenotypes, and saves and deletes their own filter presets (small-variant, structural-variant and the account's own); a mixed member is refused only where they are a reader; `/auth/me` names the projects the user only reads. The catalogue of every route that changes something, walked from the app through FastAPI's `_IncludedRouter`: each is an admin's, the caller's own data (exactly the six marked routes), sign-in, or one of the eight routes refused a reader at a checkpoint; a new one fails until it is listed. Each member is stored with their level, a reader who is no member is refused before anything is written, an update without a reader list keeps each remaining member's level and makes one added an analyst; the family, the project list, the signed-in user and the user list say which projects the caller only reads. |
| [backend/tests/test_auth_rate_limit_pg.py](../backend/tests/test_auth_rate_limit_pg.py) | Failed-login throttling and reset; per-IP signup throttling (independent bucket). |
| [backend/tests/test_signup_throttle.py](../backend/tests/test_signup_throttle.py) | /auth/signup is rate-limited per IP (429 + Retry-After before any hash/create) and returns an identical generic 202 for new vs. already-registered emails (no account enumeration). A password under 15 characters is refused with 422 before throttle bookkeeping or account creation (#582). |
| [backend/tests/test_auth_login_timing.py](../backend/tests/test_auth_login_timing.py) | Login runs a dummy bcrypt verify on the no-such-account path so response time doesn't leak account existence; the equalizer hash is a valid bcrypt hash. |
| [backend/tests/test_auth_swagger_token.py](../backend/tests/test_auth_swagger_token.py) | Swagger/bearer token authentication. |
| [backend/tests/test_auth_token_verification.py](../backend/tests/test_auth_token_verification.py) | Sign-in tokens (REQ-SEC-003), through the real login route and `get_current_user`: the token issued at sign-in is HS256, signed with `SECRET_KEY`, lives `ACCESS_TOKEN_EXPIRE_MINUTES`, and signs its user in; a token signed with another key or algorithm, unsigned, expired, naming no user, or of an unknown or inactive user is refused with a 401, and without Azure AD a Microsoft token is refused unread. With Microsoft sign-in configured (SEC-4), against a stand-in directory made at run time: a Microsoft access token signs in the account of its object ID, never the one its e-mail address names; a first sign-in's audit row records the account made; an inactive account is told so; a refused token is a 401 with its reason logged and the token not, an expired one without a warning; a local token is refused without `AZURE_ADMIN_OVERRIDE`, even the emergency administrator's, and with it admitted for the emergency administrator only (not another admin or superuser, not that user name on a Microsoft account, not deactivated), each use logged and its audit row flagged; the override leaves Microsoft tokens alone. |
| [backend/tests/test_azure_token_check.py](../backend/tests/test_azure_token_check.py) | The check of a Microsoft access token (SEC-4, REQ-SEC-003), `core/azure.py`, against a stand-in directory whose keys and tokens are made at run time: a token of the directory for CoGA signs in its object ID (canonical form), with its scope among others too, and the key set comes from the directory's OpenID configuration; another audience, issuer, directory, authorised party, version 1.0, another scope, an application's token (roles, no scope), no or a malformed object ID, no directory, expiry past the minute's leeway, a token not valid yet, another key under the directory's key ID or under one it does not publish, `alg: none`, HS256 signed with the public key, no key ID and no token at all are each refused with their reason; a minute of clock drift passes; a rotated key is fetched once, a made-up one not again within five minutes and again after them; the keys are fetched again after a day; the last keys serve while Microsoft is unreachable, and without keys an unreachable directory refuses; a configuration naming another issuer or a key set off `login.microsoftonline.com` is not used. |
| [backend/tests/test_microsoft_sign_in_routes.py](../backend/tests/test_microsoft_sign_in_routes.py) | The sign-in routes with Microsoft sign-in, and role changes (SEC-4; REQ-SEC-002, -003, -004), through the real routes, `get_current_user` and the audit middleware: `GET /api/auth/config` says password sign-in without Azure AD and gives the application ID, authority and scope (default or configured) with it; with Azure AD and no override `/auth/login` and `/auth/token` refuse every account (403) before any lookup or throttle read; with the override the emergency administrator signs in (both routes), logged and flagged in its audit row, while a local viewer, another local admin and that user name on a Microsoft account are refused (403) and counted as failed, and a wrong password keeps its 400; there is no sign-up (403, nothing read or made). An admin sets an account's role, PATCHing it alone, and the audit row records it from and to (an activation change likewise, a change that changes nothing not at all); one's own role and the emergency administrator's are refused (403) and unchanged, and so is deactivating the emergency administrator, while activating it and deactivating another account go through; a viewer is refused, an unknown role is 422 and an unknown account 404. |
| [backend/tests/test_microsoft_accounts.py](../backend/tests/test_microsoft_accounts.py) | Accounts of Microsoft sign-in (SEC-4), `services/user_accounts.py` against a stand-in `users` table with its unique columns: the first sign-in makes a viewer's active account bound to the object ID, in no project, with the token's address, user name and name and a stored value no password matches, and says so for the audit; a later sign-in finds it by the object ID, follows a changed address and name, keeps its own when another account has the token's, and keeps its names when the token carries none; the address is the `email` claim, else `preferred_username`; a first sign-in whose address or user name another account has is refused (403) and links nothing, one without an address makes nothing; two requests of one first sign-in make one account. The emergency administrator is the local `ADMIN_USERNAME` account with an admin role; the account list says how each account signs in. The admin seed replaces the emergency administrator's hash when `ADMIN_PASSWORD` was rotated, leaves an unchanged one and a Microsoft account of that name alone, and makes the account when there is none. |
| [backend/tests/test_event_loop_offload.py](../backend/tests/test_event_loop_offload.py) | Slow CPU work runs off the event loop (#527): the bcrypt check at login and the per-gene phenotype scoring run in a worker thread; the signup notification goes to a configured `ADMIN_EMAIL` through `SMTP_HOST`, and is skipped when `ADMIN_EMAIL` is left at its default. |
| [backend/tests/test_password_hashing.py](../backend/tests/test_password_hashing.py) | Password hashing with bcrypt directly (#525): hashes stored by passlib verify, a password over 72 bytes still matches, new hashes keep the `$2b$12$` format, a value that is not a bcrypt hash never matches. |
| [backend/tests/test_reference_status_auth.py](../backend/tests/test_reference_status_auth.py) | `/assemblies/reference-status` requires auth (no unauthenticated provenance/operator-email leak) and serves a signed-in viewer; the reference and genomic-data endpoints (genes, chromosomes, the CNV catalogue, DGV, blacklist, segmental duplications, assemblies) answer 401 without a token. |
| [backend/tests/test_admin_role_checks.py](../backend/tests/test_admin_role_checks.py) | Every admin check counts `superuser` as an admin, as `ADMIN_ROLES` does: tags, gene panels, PED and manual families, import jobs, the gene profile and the user list admit both admin roles and refuse a viewer (another team's project answers the gene profile and the Family Builder with 404 `Project not found`, like an unknown one); a scan fails on any comparison of a role with the literal `"admin"`. |
| [backend/tests/test_admin_route_gating.py](../backend/tests/test_admin_route_gating.py) | Panel/tag-definition mutations and the import of a NIPT recurrent-artefact table enforce admin at the route (403 for a viewer); the dead `/admin/samples/{id}/projects` route is removed (404). Replacing a family's annotation manifest is refused to a viewer and allowed to an admin or superuser, and is stored as `manual` whatever `source` the request sends. |
| [backend/tests/test_request_uuid.py](../backend/tests/test_request_uuid.py) | A UUID in a request is read once and bound as its canonical text (`core/sql.py`: `canonical_uuid`, `require_uuid`; synthetic ids): its spellings in any case, without hyphens, in braces and after `urn:uuid:` read as the canonical text, and what `uuid.UUID` merely tolerates (an underscore, a space or a sign among the digits, `0x`, digits of another script, hyphens elsewhere, one brace) spells none. Over the routes, signed in as an admin on a session that finds nothing, a value that is no UUID is answered as an unknown UUID is, or with the route's own 400 for an invalid id, and is never bound: the raw-file download and verify, the import job, the HPO annotation update and delete, the NIPT artifact list and delete, the three filter-preset deletes, a species' assemblies, the user update, the clinical CNV, the five panel routes, the project update and delete (before, the first ones answered 500); a UUID in braces, after `urn:uuid:` or in capitals is bound as its canonical text (before, the braces and the prefix answered 500). A panel read by its id in braces keeps its genes, the variant explorer filters by such a panel, read as the family page reads it, and refuses a panel id that is no UUID with the family page's 400 without binding it (CLIN-48), a reference upload binds the assembly it found in every later statement, two spellings of one project are one project, and a viewer may name their own project in capitals (before, 403). |
| [backend/tests/test_request_body_uuid.py](../backend/tests/test_request_body_uuid.py) | A UUID a request body names a record by is read once and its record looked up before anything is written (`require_uuid`; synthetic ids). Over the routes, signed in as an admin on a session that finds nothing: the `assembly_id` of a NIPT artifact add, auto-seed and table import and the `project_id` of an import request that spells no UUID (a blank one too) is answered `404 Assembly not found` / `404 Project not found` before any query; a UUID of no record, as it is, in braces, after `urn:uuid:` or in capitals, is looked up by its canonical text alone, and nothing is inserted, audited or committed (before, each a 500: the uuid codec, the insert's uuid cast or its foreign key); a viewer is refused (403) before the id is read. On a session that finds the record, every statement binds the canonical id and the artifact list's audit event records it (an id in capitals was recorded as it came); an import request naming no project (`None`, `""`) is queued without one. |
| [backend/tests/test_encoded_id_urls.py](../backend/tests/test_encoded_id_urls.py) | The URLs the backend hands the browser carry each family and sample ID as one encoded path segment (SEC-2; `core/url_path.py`, which mirrors `apiPath`; synthetic IDs). Read as a browser reads them (`?` starts the query, `#` the fragment, dot segments resolved, the path decoded once), the local-mode alignment manifest, the signal-track manifest and the QC-report link name the family, sample and source they were issued for, for IDs holding `/`, `?`, `#`, `%`, `%41` and `..`, and the link's query holds its token alone. Through the app (httpx on the ASGI app, which decodes the path once as uvicorn does), following the manifests' URLs and the QC link reaches the sample's own reads, index, depth track and report for IDs holding `?`, `#`, `%`, `%41` and `..` (before: `?` and `#` cut the path short, and `%41` fetched another family's reads and depth track and was refused the report), and a sample ID such as `../F2/S2` never fetches family F2's files (before: F2's reads and depth track; now the alignment manifest lists no reads for it, since the lookup keeps to the family's own folders (SEC-20), and the reads' and the depth track's URLs answer 404). An ID holding `/` still answers 404: the server routes on the decoded path. |
| [backend/tests/test_alignment_lookup_family_folders.py](../backend/tests/test_alignment_lookup_family_folders.py) | The local-mode alignment lookup finds a sample's reads only in its own family's folders, `<data>/<family>` and `<data>/families/<family>` (SEC-20, SEC-13; `routers/cram.py`, synthetic IDs and files in `tmp_path`). The five layouts are probed in order, each finds the sample's CRAM or BAM and its index, the first that holds the file wins, and a link between the family's own folders is followed. With another family's reads in every layout, a sample ID that climbs into that family (`../F2/S2`, `../../F2/bams/S2`, `../families/F2/bams/S2`, `../../../F2/S2`, an absolute path) builds no path outside its own family's folders and finds nothing; a family ID that is no single folder name (empty, `.`, `..`, `../F2`, `F2/..`, `./F2`, `F2/`, `families/F2`, `F2/bams`, an absolute path) has no folder; a family named `families` keeps its package folder but does not read the packages folder as its flat one (it reached other packages), and a packages folder linked elsewhere in the data directory still serves its packages while the family whose flat folder holds it reaches none; a NUL finds nothing and raises nothing (before: F2's reads found and the manifest listed them, and each of these IDs was probed). A link that leads into another family's folder (the package or flat folder, a `bams` folder, a file) is not followed (before: the other family's file was served). When the family's folders lead out of the data directory, or an ID climbs out of every layout, nothing else is tried: the GET and the header read answer 404 (before: an unchecked `<data>/<family>/<file>` was served). Through the app (httpx on the ASGI app, a viewer, local storage) the manifest lists only the family's own reads and following them fetches them, and a folder linked into another family or out of the data directory gives no manifest entry and 404 to GET and HEAD. |
| [backend/tests/test_signal_track_lookup_family_folders.py](../backend/tests/test_signal_track_lookup_family_folders.py) | The local-mode signal-track lookup finds a track only in its own family's folders (SEC-25; `routers/signal_tracks.py` with `core/family_folders.py`, synthetic IDs and files in `tmp_path`). A path recorded in `samples.metadata['signal_tracks']` is found in the family's package folder, `<data>/families/<family>`, also as an absolute path into it or with a `..` that stays in it, and a family named `families` keeps its package; a link between the family's own folders is followed. With family F1's own track and F2's in its package and flat folder, a recorded path of F1 that climbs into F2's folders (`../F2/cnv/S2.depth.bw`, `cnv/../../F2/...`, `../../F2/...`, `../../families/F2/...`) or names F2's track by its absolute path finds nothing and the manifest lists no URL for it; a family ID that is no single folder name (empty, `.`, `..`, `../F2`, `F2/..`, `F1/../F2`, `./F2`, `F2/`, an absolute path) has no folder (before: each found F2's track). A link that leads into another family's folder (the package folder, a folder in it, also into F2's flat folder, a file) is not followed (before: F2's track was served). A recorded path or a link out of the data directory and a NUL find nothing, as before. Through the app (httpx on the ASGI app, a viewer, local storage) F1's manifest lists its own sample alone and following it fetches its track, while samples recording `..` into F2's package, F2's absolute path and a folder linked into F2's answer 404 to GET and HEAD (before: listed, and 200 with F2's track); F2's manifest still lists and serves F2's own. |
| [backend/tests/test_qc_report_lookup_family_folders.py](../backend/tests/test_qc_report_lookup_family_folders.py) | The local-mode QC-report lookup finds a sample's report only in its own family's folders (SEC-25; `routers/family_qc_reports.py` with `core/family_folders.py`, synthetic IDs and files in `tmp_path`, the packages folder the import root as deployed). A recorded `sequencing_qc.report` path is found in the family's package folder, also as an absolute path into it or with a `..` that stays in it; a missing file answers 404; the flat folder `<data>/<family>` is read only when it lies under the import roots. A recorded path that climbs into F2's package or flat folder, or names F2's report by its absolute path, answers 404 (before: a 400 meant for manifest paths, never served). A family named `families` does not read the packages folder, the import root itself, as its flat folder, and keeps its own package (before: it served F2's report); a family ID that is no single folder name (empty, `.`, `..`, `../F2`, `F2/..`, `F1/../F2`, an absolute path) has no folder when the data directory lies under the import roots (before: each served F2's report). A package folder linked to F2's is not followed (before: F2's report was served), nor a folder or file in it linked into F2's folders or out of the data directory (404; before: 400). Through the app (httpx on the ASGI app, a viewer) the link and the report open the sample's own report in its sandbox; a recorded path into F2's package, a package folder linked to F2's and a family named `families` answer 404 to the link and to the report (before: 400, then 200 with F2's report twice); F2's own report still opens. |
| [backend/tests/test_raw_import_file_paths_contained.py](../backend/tests/test_raw_import_file_paths_contained.py) | The admin page's raw-file download, Verify and file list, and the family and sample deletes' purge of managed copies, keep to CoGA's own folders (SEC-29; `services/raw_import_files_pg.py`, synthetic paths in `tmp_path`: an import root, a staging folder, a data directory and a folder that is none of them). A package file in a package folder not named after its family, an upload, a staged copy and a link to the package file are downloaded (through the app, a signed-in admin) and verified by their real path. A recorded path elsewhere, one that climbs out of the import root with `..`, a link in the package that leads out, a folder beside the import root or the upload folder sharing its name, the data directory outside the upload folder and a relative path answer 403 to the download and to Verify, are logged, and are never read (before: 200 with the file's bytes, or its checksum); the list shows them unavailable (before: present and downloadable). A missing file still answers 410 and a file in an object store 409. With `FAMILY_IMPORT_ROOTS` empty only the upload and staging folders are read. Verify asks the store about an object below the remote import root and about none in another prefix, bucket or store, past `..`, with an empty segment or with a query (403). A family delete unlinks the family's uploads, also one in the folder of a former ID, and a link inside the upload folder but never its target; it leaves the package file, a file elsewhere, one reached by `..` and one reached through a link or a linked folder, each skipped with a log line (before: deleted); a sample delete the same. |
| [backend/tests/test_operational_metrics.py](../backend/tests/test_operational_metrics.py) | `GET /metrics` (#734), read with prometheus_client's parser: off (404) without `METRICS_TOKEN`; 401 without the bearer token, with another scheme or a wrong token; the exposition with its content type, `no-store`, and the Python (and, on Linux, process) collectors; left out of the OpenAPI schema. Requests are labelled by route template (`/families/{family_id}`), a request no route matched by `unmatched`, an unusual method by `OTHER`, and the identifiers in the paths never appear; recording never raises. The build is named; each assembly's last integrity check reports 1 for its outcome (also `check_failed`, and an unexpected one) and 0 for the others, with its time, and a sweep that could not list the assemblies is flagged; the audit pipelines report their backlog and unpersisted events; the active import jobs per status with their staleness; a database fault leaves the jobs out and is counted while the rest is served. Neither the load balancer nor the frontend server routes `/metrics`. No counter's `_created` series is exposed; every `coga_` series an alert query in `terraform/monitoring.tf` reads is one the backend exposes (a misspelled name fails), and the uptime check's expected text is what `/api/health` answers (#737). |
| [backend/tests/test_config_security.py](../backend/tests/test_config_security.py) | Refuse-to-start on insecure default or weak secrets outside dev: a short `SECRET_KEY`, an empty `CLICKHOUSE_PASSWORD`, a missing or malformed `INTEGRITY_ANCHOR_SIGNING_KEY` (#522), a placeholder or blank `POSTGRES_APP_PASSWORD` when one is given (#364), an `INTEGRITY_ANCHOR_SIGNING_KEY` equal to `SECRET_KEY`, `AUDIT_LOG_MODE=off`; a complete production configuration starts; CORS origin regex must compile and be fully anchored. Outside development a CORS origin or pattern that admits a loopback origin (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `::1`, `0.0.0.0`, `*.localhost`) or any site (`*`, `^https?://.*$`) is refused, the default pattern included when none is set, while the deployment's own origins start; a `GIT_SHA` that is not 7–40 hex characters (`unknown`, empty, `dev`) is refused, a stamped one starts; development and test keep the local defaults and an unstamped build (#733). A `METRICS_TOKEN`, when set, must have 32+ characters and differ from `SECRET_KEY` and `INTEGRITY_ANCHOR_SIGNING_KEY`; unset, production starts; development takes any (#734). |
| [backend/tests/test_config_microsoft_sign_in.py](../backend/tests/test_config_microsoft_sign_in.py) | Microsoft sign-in settings (SEC-4, REQ-SEC-006): without a directory users sign in with a password, and an empty value (Terraform's unset variable) is no value; with both IDs Microsoft sign-in is on, the GUIDs compared in lower case and the scope `api://<client>/access_as_user` unless one is configured; a tenant without a client or the reverse, a tenant that is `common`, `organizations`, `consumers`, a domain or no GUID, a client that is no GUID, a scope that is no application ID URI and name, and a scope or the override without Azure AD are refused at startup; a deployment starts with Microsoft sign-in and refuses half of it. Terraform passes the four settings to the backend, its variables default to empty and the override to off, and a plan refuses half a configuration. |
| [backend/tests/test_db_migrate_app_role.py](../backend/tests/test_db_migrate_app_role.py) | The migration enables `coga_app`'s login from `POSTGRES_APP_PASSWORD` (#364, #627): the SCRAM-SHA-256 verifier reproduces the RFC 7677 exchange and gets a fresh salt each time; passwords SASLprep would change are refused; the password reaches Postgres only as a bound verifier, never in SQL text; the migration enables the login after the schema, and leaves the role alone without a password. |
| [backend/tests/test_backend_hardening.py](../backend/tests/test_backend_hardening.py) | Backend hardening (#522): the species list needs a signed-in user; no unsigned integrity anchor outside development; the HPO download is HTTPS-only, size-bounded, OBO-checked, checked against its SHA-256 and atomic; the knowledgebase script gets an allowlisted environment; QC-report link tokens are masked in the access log. |
| [backend/tests/test_clinical_cnv_kb_jobs.py](../backend/tests/test_clinical_cnv_kb_jobs.py) | The clinical CNV knowledgebase rebuild job (#526): status and the active job, one rebuild at a time (409), a missing script (503) or assembly (404), the background start, a successful build replacing the knowledgebase, a failed build leaving it untouched with the error and log tail recorded, the error naming the reason the build gave last (#721). A job never stays active once its run has ended: a job that cannot be taken is marked failed, and a failure that cannot be recorded is logged. The run heartbeats while the build runs (a failed heartbeat does not stop it), writes only as the worker that took the job, and stops a build that outlives `CLINICAL_CNV_KB_BUILD_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (marked failed) or whose job was closed meanwhile (nothing loaded); the status and a rebuild request first close a job whose worker is gone. |
| [backend/tests/test_clinical_cnv_knowledgebase_clinvar.py](../backend/tests/test_clinical_cnv_knowledgebase_clinvar.py) | The knowledgebase build's ClinVar support (#566, #623): the loss/gain side from `Type`, then the ISCN copy number (`x0`/`x1` loss, `x3`+ gain; on X/Y only `x0` and `x3`+), then the name's words; array records become support rows and count toward a region's loss and gain. The counts stay empty until ClinVar support is computed, and read 0 only once counted (#624, #625). |
| [backend/tests/test_clinical_cnv_knowledgebase_sources.py](../backend/tests/test_clinical_cnv_knowledgebase_sources.py) | The knowledgebase build's backbone sources (#721): each assembly reads the recurrent-CNV file ClinGen publishes (GRCh37: `…-hg37.bed`); both header shapes (`#chrom` on the track line or its own line, CRLF) give the named regions and drop the breakpoint markers; chromosome names read case-insensitively (`chrx` → X); a recurrent file that cannot be downloaded or holds no regions, a curation file that cannot be loaded, or a curation without records stops the build (`SourceUnavailable`), and the script then exits 1 with `Build stopped: …` as its last line. |
| [backend/tests/test_clinical_cnv_clinvar_support.py](../backend/tests/test_clinical_cnv_clinvar_support.py) | The knowledgebase's ClinVar support reaches `clinical_cnvs` and the API (#624, #625): counts and VariationIDs are loaded; a knowledgebase built without ClinVar, a named file without the columns and a BED-style file store NULL (not recorded), and 0 stays 0; `ClinicalCnvOut` serves them. |
| [backend/tests/test_client_ip.py](../backend/tests/test_client_ip.py) | Client address behind trusted proxies (#520): taken `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS` entries from the right of `X-Forwarded-For`, never the client-settable left-most; repeated headers form one chain; a short chain or no trust leaves the request as it was. |
| [backend/tests/test_deployment_config.py](../backend/tests/test_deployment_config.py) | Configuration guards (#520): compose publishes databases and the API on loopback only and gives the frontend no `.env`; CI service images are digest-pinned and `markdown` is version-pinned; compose, every CI job and the Terraform VM run the same Postgres and ClickHouse images (#524); Terraform mounts refdata read-only, requires TLS 1.2+, sets the proxy hops and import roots; one deploy trigger with persistent tfvars. The go-live switches (#364, #627): the deploy job stops before authenticating unless `gcp-deploy` has required reviewers; with the restricted database role the API loses the owner's password and waits for the migration job, which runs `app.db_migrate` under its own account; the ClickHouse egress lockdown allows only Google APIs, refuses a Docker Hub image and authenticates Artifact Registry pulls; ingress ranges must be CIDRs; every switch defaults off. Each main build deploys an image tag of its own, its commit, so a merge rolls out and re-runs the migration (#632); the PHI bucket's CORS policy lets only the app's origin read it, for IGV (#633); the backend's account can only read both buckets (#634). An unstamped image reports the version in `VERSION`, which the Dockerfile copies to `/VERSION`, and the release check passes, so `frontend/package.json` carries that version (#723). The backend image copies only `scripts/clinical_cnv_knowledgebase.py` (where `CLINICAL_CNV_KB_SCRIPT_PATH` points) and `scripts/import_dgv.py`. Every Cloud Run service and job that runs the backend image (the API and the db-migrate job) gets the app's own origin and an empty `CORS_ORIGIN_REGEX` (#733), and can start: the environment the `.tf` files give it, with and without the optional metrics token and import roots, loads the backend's settings in production mode, with strong stand-ins for its Secret Manager values and the commit `build.yml` stamps into the image, which no container overrides. The db-migrate job had `APP_ENV` without the cross-origin settings and stopped at import. Both services behind the load balancer send HSTS, with one policy (SEC-5): each container of the frontend image sets `ENABLE_HSTS`, unconditionally, to the exact string the frontend server compares with (`true`), the API's settings load with HSTS on, and the two servers send the same `Strict-Transport-Security` value; the frontend service had no `ENABLE_HSTS`, so the app page went without HSTS. The metrics collector's configuration, rendered as `templatefile()` would, scrapes `localhost:8000/metrics` with `Bearer ${env:METRICS_TOKEN}` and writes only to Managed Service for Prometheus; both backend containers read the token from `coga-metrics-token`, which only the backend's account may read and the migration job never gets, and the deployment guide has the operator add its value; the collector image is pinned by digest; the alerting warns when no address is set or the uptime check would meet the allowlist (#737). The CI `images` job builds both production images as `ci/cloudbuild.*.yaml` do: its build steps, run with a stand-in `docker` that records the command, pass the same Dockerfile, target, context and build arguments, with the backend stamped with the version and commit that `build.yml`'s prepare job computes and the frontend given the problem-report variable; the job runs on every pull request and push to `main`, has only the read-only token, and pushes nothing. No password or private key enters the Terraform state or plan (OPS-10): no resource sets `password`, `root_password`, `secret_data` (except the public ClickHouse certificate's version), `private_key_pem` or `ca_private_key_pem`, none generates a key or password, and a data source that looks up a secret version reads its number, not its value; the Cloud SQL owner's password goes in write-only, read ephemerally at the version that data source names, whose number is the write-only version, so a new secret version still changes the password; the ClickHouse CA and server keys are ephemeral and reach the certificates and Secret Manager write-only, a new certificate request re-issues the CA and stores the new server key, and the CA and the server certificate last and renew alike; `versions.tf` requires Terraform 1.11+, google 8.x and tls 4.4+, and the pull-request validation and the deploy run one pinned Terraform at least that new; the db-migrate job has deletion protection off, so the rollback to the owner role can remove it. |
| [backend/tests/test_coga_logging.py](../backend/tests/test_coga_logging.py) | `scrub_log` control-character neutralization (CWE-117 log forging): CR/LF/tab/DEL replaced, clean values pass through, oversized truncated. `describe_error` names a database error by its type, the driver's error type and the SQLSTATE, a ClickHouse error by its code and name (read from the driver's errors only), and any other error by its type, never by its text. `describe_traceback` gives every exception of a chain, oldest first, with its frames and that name and without its message (a direct cause, a context, a suppressed context, a cycle, the members of an exception group), and stops where Python does on a member raised while its group was handled, whose context is that group. The JSON formatter writes a line that carries an exception (`logger.exception()`, `exc_info` given as `True`, the exception or its tuple), from any logger and at any level, with the exception's kind in `error` and the frames of its chain in `traceback`, never its message, not even when another handler's formatter cached the full text on the record; a `traceback` the call passes is kept as given, and a line without an exception gets neither field. A line whose traceback cannot be written is still written, with the kind, by the formatter and by uvicorn's filter, and nothing is raised or printed to stderr. uvicorn's traceback of an unhandled error, in its default format, keeps the frames and names but none of the values the error quoted (`RedactServerErrorFilter`), and the app installs that filter once. What an event loop reports (a task's or future's exception nobody retrieved, one left as `asyncio.run` closes, a failing callback, plain or a partial) is logged on asyncio's loop and on uvloop's by `log_event_loop_exception`: the message as asyncio's fixed text up to the first object it quotes (a message it does not know is not written), the task by its name and coroutine, a callback and an asynchronous generator by their qualified names, debug-mode frames without their locals, any other object by its type, and the exception by its kind and frames, never the value the reprs or arguments quote; the handler never raises, so the loop never falls back on its own; the lifespan installs it, and on the uvloop loop uvicorn itself creates a request's background task that fails is logged that way. |
| [backend/tests/test_request_logging.py](../backend/tests/test_request_logging.py) | Request-logging sanitization, path/secret masking, db-update derivation (incl. stripping the `/api` mount prefix so the audit entity is the real collection). A failed audit write, in `AUDIT_LOG_MODE=sync` or in async mode with no worker running, does not fail the request: it is counted for the not-persisted alert and logged once with its masked payload, the error by its kind, never by its text, which quotes the statement and the row; so is a write that raises past the writer, and a row that cannot be built because the request's own error has no readable text. An unhandled error's 500 line names the error, its route and the frames of its chain, none of the values a failed statement quotes, while its audit row keeps the error's full text; a 5xx answer the route made itself is logged with its route and no traceback. |
| [backend/tests/test_http_resilience.py](../backend/tests/test_http_resilience.py) | Outbound resilience (#265): idempotent requests retry transient failures (transport errors + 429/5xx) with capped backoff; non-idempotent + 4xx never retry; bounded worst case; own-client body still readable; S3 Config has bounded timeouts + adaptive retries. |
| [backend/tests/test_csv_export.py](../backend/tests/test_csv_export.py) | CSV formula-injection guard: cells starting with `=`/`+`/`-`/`@`/tab/CR/LF are quote-prefixed; benign values untouched; survives a real csv writer/reader round-trip. |
| [backend/tests/test_html_sanitize.py](../backend/tests/test_html_sanitize.py) | Reference-HTML stored-XSS sanitiser: strips `script`/`img`/event-handlers and `javascript:` hrefs to a strict allowlist while keeping safe formatting and safe links. |

### Ingestion / storage / ClickHouse / files
| Test file | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| [backend/tests/test_clickhouse.py](../backend/tests/test_clickhouse.py) | ClickHouse client query/command/insert dispatch and recording; an insert hands the driver its table quoted and qualified, so the table of an assembly whose dataset key has a dot reaches ClickHouse intact. A transient error is retried once and logged by its kind, not by its text, which can hold the request URL and its bound parameters. |
| [backend/tests/test_clickhouse_dataset_key.py](../backend/tests/test_clickhouse_dataset_key.py) | Dataset-key normalization and injection-safe handling. |
| [backend/tests/test_genotypes.py](../backend/tests/test_genotypes.py) | One genotype classification (#511): every genotype in exactly one of hom-alt / het / hom-ref / no-call (haploid, multi-allelic, half calls, malformed), the short vocabulary is exhaustive and partitioned, the SQL condition shape (no-call as the complement), and sample filters and inheritance (X-linked hemizygous son, multi-allelic homozygote, haploid carrier ruling out dominant, X-linked SQL params) on haploid chrM/chrX and multi-allelic calls. |
| [backend/tests/test_sex_chromosomes.py](../backend/tests/test_sex_chromosomes.py) | Where a male is hemizygous (#545, #620): the GRC PAR1/PAR2 bounds of GRCh38 and GRCh37, 1-based and inclusive, on X and Y; chromosome names (`chrX`, `23`, …); assembly aliases; no hemizygous positions on an assembly with unknown PARs. `hemizygous_interval`: a block is one copy only when it lies wholly outside the PARs. |
| [backend/tests/test_carrier_screening.py](../backend/tests/test_carrier_screening.py) | Expanded carrier screening, the couple-level at-risk rule (REQ-CARR-002, #731): a gene both partners carry a variant in keeps each partner's variant (also one variant both carry, and genes matched by gene id when the symbols differ); a gene only one partner carries in, reference and missing calls, a variant without a gene, and a mitochondrial gene both partners carry a variant in (under `M`, `MT` and `chrM`) give nothing; the order is kept. X-linked (#732): a female partner's variant on chrX outside the pseudo-autosomal regions is kept on its own (also under `chrX` and `23`, and for a partner without a recorded sex or a second female partner); a male partner's is not, nor a chrY variant; in PAR1, and on an assembly without known PARs, only the both-partners rule applies; GRCh37 reads its own boundaries; both rules together keep each variant once, in order. The couple is a `couple` relationship (one naming an unknown sample is ignored), else mother and father, else a two-member family; without a couple there is no finding. |
| [backend/tests/test_clickhouse_family_variants.py](../backend/tests/test_clickhouse_family_variants.py) | Small-variant query, genotype/phasing parsing, inheritance + compound-het. The SV track (track mode) gets the total and the candidate cap, and asks SQL for its sample's SVs only, while the SV table's query is unchanged (#585, #590). The small-variant payload says where a male is hemizygous (`hemizygous_in_males`, #622). A capped candidate read (compound-het, recessive, carrier screening; the SV candidate cap) marks the page `candidates_capped` and its export truncated, and a prioritised window that overflowed and filtered to nothing still flags its ranking truncated (#725). Each capped page also says where it stopped (`candidate_limit`): the compound-het / recessive / carrier window it read (it grows with the page asked for), the SV candidate cap, or the prioritised window; a complete page reports none. An SV review-tag search, unranked or prioritised, finds a reported SV past the candidate cap, because the review selection narrows the read; without a selection the read carries no id restriction (#730). Expanded carrier screening hands the family's assembly to the couple rule, so a female partner's X-linked variant reaches the page on its own (#732), and its SQL prefilter leaves chrM out of the candidate window. The Sample QC's genotype sample takes the smallest storage keys over chromosomes 1–22, not the first sites by position, or over chrX outside the pseudo-autosomal regions (all of chrX where the assembly's PARs are not known), and keeps one copy of a variant stored twice. (#742) A failed variant query is logged by its ClickHouse code and name, its parameters by name and the query with its placeholders, never by the error's text, which can quote a value from the request. *MANE only* keeps a MANE Select or a MANE Plus Clinical transcript, in the Python matcher (either key spelling) and in SQL (CLIN-5). A gene panel's regions are joined to the family's own rows on 64-kb windows, not tested against each row, and a panel of 3,000 regions keeps its query compact (ENG-15). A gene panel of more than 1,000 regions keeps its regions beside its genes, bound once as arrays (owner decision 2026-10-14: it was matched on gene symbols alone). |
| [backend/tests/test_compound_het_phasing.py](../backend/tests/test_compound_het_phasing.py) | Read-backed compound-het phase: cis pairs dropped, trans labelled, haplotypes compared only within a phase set, hom/multi-allelic/unphased calls and half calls (one allele missing) left unresolved by the shared reader `phased_alt_haplotype`; each phased pair run through the SNV + SNV pair and the SNV + SV second-hit badge reaches the same read verdict. Without reads, the parents place the pair: one hit from each parent is trans by segregation, both from one parent drops the pair, and the reads outrank the parents; `phase_evidence` says which. |
| [backend/tests/test_compound_het_segregation_phase.py](../backend/tests/test_compound_het_segregation_phase.py) | Phase from the family, one rule for SNV+SNV compound hets and the SNV+SV second hit: each scenario runs through both paths and must reach the same verdict. Trans needs a parent who places each hit (one hit from each parent, or the only sequenced parent carrying exactly one); an unaffected relative carrying neither hit, or relatives not recorded as parents, leave it unknown; a hit both parents lack (de novo) is not placed; cis needs an unaffected carrier of both, or both hits traced to one parent with both parents genotyped; shallow reference calls and no-calls are not absence; a male is not traced where he carries one copy; a pair traced to one parent no longer counts towards the compound-het segregation mode. |
| [backend/tests/test_clickhouse_integrity.py](../backend/tests/test_clickhouse_integrity.py) | ClickHouse table-integrity / detached-parts checks. `CHECK TABLE` read as the ClickHouse client returns it (captured from the CI ClickHouse server: one flattened row for several parts, one empty value for none): every part judged by the server's verdict for the whole table, a failed later part named, a table without parts passing, a result that cannot be read never passing; the gene-index rebuild does not swap in a shadow table with a corrupt part. |
| [backend/tests/test_clickhouse_interval_tracks.py](../backend/tests/test_clickhouse_interval_tracks.py) | Interval-track presence detection and region filtering; a track's rows of some genes read by record_id, case-insensitive and bound as a parameter. |
| [backend/tests/test_clickhouse_variant_storage_ops.py](../backend/tests/test_clickhouse_variant_storage_ops.py) | Variant storage operations: the assembly listing (table prefixes deduplicated) and the storage status (missing tables, running mutations); per-sample counts of non-reference calls, scoped to a callset source; `OPTIMIZE` skips materialized views; an insert writes compact annotations, the gene index and the site QUAL, in chunks when large, and VEP's MANE Select and MANE Plus Clinical flags per transcript and per variant (the annotation index) on GRCh38, GRCh37 and T2T (CLIN-5); the gene-index rebuild is explicit, batched maintenance; the family and sample summaries are rebuilt; a delete is scoped to its source, or clears every table without one; setting the tables up only creates them. |
| [backend/tests/test_clickhouse_integrity_monitor.py](../backend/tests/test_clickhouse_integrity_monitor.py) | Scheduled integrity monitor (#269): each sweep records a dated result per assembly and escalates by status (ERROR on corrupt, WARN on degraded); a check that fails is recorded as failed, replacing the earlier result and without the exception text, and a sweep that cannot list the assemblies says so and keeps the earlier results; the state names the setting and the interval; disabled is no-op, worker sweeps then stops cleanly. |
| [backend/tests/test_admin_clickhouse_integrity_monitor_api.py](../backend/tests/test_admin_clickhouse_integrity_monitor_api.py) | `GET /api/admin/clickhouse/variants/integrity-monitor`: an admin gets the scheduled check's last result (the setting, the interval, the last sweep, and per assembly the report or that the check could not run, never the exception text); before the first sweep there is none; a viewer gets 403 and a request without a token 401. |
| [backend/tests/test_track_availability_presence.py](../backend/tests/test_track_availability_presence.py) | The aggregated small-variant presence query (#266) (track availability) — real query construction + per-sample explicit/non-ref logic + id→name mapping + empty short-circuit (CH execute mocked). |
| [backend/tests/test_object_storage.py](../backend/tests/test_object_storage.py) | S3 URI parsing and object-storage helpers, including a remote folder's name (its last segment, or the bucket). S3 package discovery reads every ListObjectsV2 page (more than 1000 packages under a root, a manifest past a package's first 1000 objects) and stops reading a package once it has seen both a manifest and a PED. |
| [backend/tests/test_s3_import_and_cram.py](../backend/tests/test_s3_import_and_cram.py) | S3 source authorization (refused without a configured root); a local package path refused without a configured root, allowed under it and refused on traversal; CRAM/BAM **access-before-serve** (a family outside the caller's projects answers 404 before any file or presigned URL is issued, a sample outside the family is refused, a member sample is served) and the alignment manifest (presigned URLs in S3 mode, relative ones locally). |
| [backend/tests/test_remote_package_alignments.py](../backend/tests/test_remote_package_alignments.py) | A package imported from a bucket (`gs://` and `s3://`): staging leaves CRAM/BAM files and their indexes in the store (a VCF's `.csi` is still staged) and only the alignments dataset may reference them; the alignments importer asks the store whether each file exists and records its URI; raw-file provenance still names the files left in the store; the CRAM endpoint serves the recorded object first (manifest, GET, HEAD), never pairs it with an index a layout probe found, falls back to the probes, and never signs a recorded location outside the configured bucket and FAMILY_IMPORT_ROOTS (another bucket or store, a sibling prefix, dot segments, a query, the wrong extension). |
| [backend/tests/test_remote_package_provenance.py](../backend/tests/test_remote_package_provenance.py) | A package imported from a bucket is traceable like a local one: every raw-file provenance row identifies its content. A staged file is hashed (SHA-256, size) from its staged copy; a file left in the store (an alignment) is identified by the store's own record under `metadata.store_object` (size, GCS generation or S3 version id, ETag, and the store's checksums labelled by algorithm, encoding and S3 full-object/composite type), never in the `sha256` column; a staged file carries that record too, for Verify; a store error costs one row its identity, not the package its record; a local package is still hashed where it lies. The family's package record (`package_import.folder_path`), the import log and the validation report the import job stores (manifest, PED and issue paths) name the source folder and its objects, never the staging copy. A manifest without `family_id` takes the source folder's name (trailing slash or not), which must match the PED as a local folder name must; the staging directory's `pkg-` name reaches nothing the import reports. |
| [backend/tests/test_family_package_raw_files.py](../backend/tests/test_family_package_raw_files.py) | Every file a package's datasets name gets its raw-file provenance row (`raw_import_files`: dataset, sample, SHA-256 and size) and its place in the sample's package record: a monogenic NIPT pair's per-target coverage tables (`target_table`) beside its VCFs, and a PCF entry's segment tables under each of their names (`maternal`, `mat`, `maternal_file`, `mat_file` and the paternal ones). A guard explores every key validation reads as a file, a fallback key included (`bed` without `target_table`, `mat` without `maternal`), and fails when the provenance list lacks one. |
| [backend/tests/test_raw_import_file_verify.py](../backend/tests/test_raw_import_file_verify.py) | File SHA-256 verification: verified / mismatch / missing / unverifiable. A file kept in an object store is checked against the store's record of it instead, without re-hashing: the object must exist with the recorded size and generation (GCS), version id (S3) or, unversioned, ETag; a vanished object is missing, a replaced one a mismatch, an unreachable store an error (502), not a verdict. The admin list does not call such a file missing (not checked when listing, not downloadable here), and its download is refused (409), not reported gone. The local files verified lie in an import root: Verify reads no other place (SEC-29). |
| [backend/tests/test_raw_import_provenance_failure.py](../backend/tests/test_raw_import_provenance_failure.py) | A raw-file provenance record that could not be written, or an upload that could not be re-read: the import stands, and the loss is logged with the family, dataset and file (#686). |
| [backend/tests/test_variant_upload_haplotype_golden.py](../backend/tests/test_variant_upload_haplotype_golden.py) | The haplotype blocks of five seeded families (quartet segregation at default and unit thresholds, no father so per-phase-set blocks, sparse sites with switch-error bursts, chromosome sizes known), block for block against a fixture recorded from the in-line builder before it was extracted (#528); the children's blocks of a chromosome come after its parents', and these families get no phase correction. |
| [backend/tests/test_small_variant_page_golden.py](../backend/tests/test_small_variant_page_golden.py) | Characterisation of `get_family_small_variants_page` (#528), recorded before it was split up (`fixtures/small_variant_page_golden.json`, 58 KB). 27 scenarios cover each path: the presence probe, the scope that matches nothing (panel, review, intervals), every scope filter at once, the prioritised ranking, the capped track (over, estimated, empty, a page), the native page (a page, an empty page, a clamped huge page, an SV second hit) and the Python path (compound het, recessive with a gene, carrier screening, track sampling, capped candidates). Each records every collaborator call, in order, with its arguments, and the page returned. Regenerate with `COGA_REGENERATE_GOLDEN=1` only for an intended change (#595). An unparseable interval list is recorded as a refusal (422), and a list of blank lines as no restriction (#615). A gene filter reads none of the gene's loci (ENG-1); an excluded gene's are read. |

| [backend/tests/test_variant_upload_service.py](../backend/tests/test_variant_upload_service.py) | VCF/VEP parsing → ClickHouse ingestion; phased-block (PS) preservation; a VEP TSV parse that fails (no header row, an over-long line after a row) leaves no temporary database; the upload reports the records read (the left-out ones too) and the bytes of its file read. A multi-sample VCF gives each sample its own call (REQ-DATA-001): GT, GQ, DP, AD, AF and PS read by FORMAT key from the sample's column in any FORMAT order, AD and AF with a value per allele, the depth from DP, else MED_DP, else MIN_DP, the fraction from AF, else VAF; a missing or truncated call's fields stay missing, a call without GT is a no-call, and a row lacking a sample's column is left out and counted; the site's QUAL is kept (none for `.`), and the stored row holds each call under its own sample. |
| [backend/tests/test_small_variant_sample_rewrite.py](../backend/tests/test_small_variant_sample_rewrite.py) | A per-sample small-variant upload (the mitochondrial calls, one file per sample) replaces only the calls of the samples its file names: the stored rows of the callset are read as stored, those samples' calls are dropped and the uploaded ones join their variant's row (its own fields kept, FILTER values joined, calls sorted by sample), so each variant stays one row per project; a row left with no call is dropped, a new variant is its uploaded row. Without `overwrite` the upload is refused (409) only when the file's samples already have calls in the callset; a failed rewrite writes the rows back as read and never clears the callset; a file with no `#CHROM` line is refused; the source-scoped read and rewrite touch only that callset and rebuild the summaries. The mito import's annotation lookup (an SQLite file in the temporary directory): the upload leaves a caller's lookup open and removes one it parsed itself; the import records the sample's haplogroup from it (the one most of its variants name) and removes it after an import and a failed upload. A chrM VCF without a variant record (a header alone, or records none of which the loader stores) fails the mito dataset before any sample's calls are replaced, the mother's valid file beside it written neither, with no lookup left (SAFE-29, the owner's decision; before, it removed the sample's stored calls and the dataset imported); the per-sample column check: the mother's file under the proband's entry is refused before its annotation is read, and a file with a second column gives the proband its own column's calls only. |
| [backend/tests/test_family_variant_writes_serialized.py](../backend/tests/test_family_variant_writes_serialized.py) | A family's variant writes run one at a time (storage faked as ClickHouse behaves, a rewrite's delete and inserts being separate steps; Postgres faked with its transaction-scoped advisory locks; each read waits until the other writer has read): two SV uploads of one family keep both samples' calls in one row, an admin SV delete and an upload keep each other's change, two per-sample (mitochondrial) uploads keep both samples' calls, and two whole-family uploads of one callset leave one file's rows, never both. Each writer takes its family's lock (small variants, SVs or both, small first, the key bound) before its first read and holds it until its commit: the SV upload, the four admin deletes, the package's NeedlR and CNV datasets, a PED overwrite (its families in one order, their variants deleted last under the locks it took, SAFE-25), a structure change's clear and an import's shell delete. An upload that waited for the deletion of its sample or family writes nothing (404). The package import holds both locks from before its snapshot until its restore, so an upload started meanwhile waits and is not undone by the restore; the import's own loaders take none (a task it started takes the lock itself once the hold has ended), a nested hold opens no second connection, a hold refuses a family whose sample is gone, and a lost lock connection does not fail the import. The report sign-out shares the locks (a shared try-lock, modelled as Postgres queues it): a write started during a sign-out waits for its commit, sign-outs do not keep each other out, and a sign-out is refused at once while the import's hold or another writer has them (#727). A structure change that clears the family's data deletes its ClickHouse data after its Postgres deletes, under the locks it took (SAFE-28). |
| [backend/tests/test_sv_upload_source_scope.py](../backend/tests/test_sv_upload_source_scope.py) | The per-sample SV upload judges and rewrites only its own source's rows (storage faked): another source's calls for the sample neither block the first upload (409) nor enter the overwrite's merge, so the source-scoped rewrite is handed that source's rows only; a second sample merges into the same source's record; `manual` is stored and scoped as `manual_upload`; an unknown `source_format` is refused (400) before any row is read; the storage read's source scope is a bound `e.source = …`, the predicate of the source-scoped delete. The admin per-sample SV delete reads every source and rewrites family-wide. |
| [backend/tests/test_track_loaders_replace_own_source.py](../backend/tests/test_track_loaders_replace_own_source.py) | A track loader replaces only its own source's track of a sample (SAFE-22; ClickHouse's interval table and the Postgres statements on `sample_interval_track_sources` faked at their edge). A BED upload of each track type with overwrite replaces the track a BED brought (`web`), rows and record, and leaves every other caller's (before: every source's track of the type went); a file that is not text or holds no record is refused (400) before anything is deleted (before: the rows were deleted first); without overwrite it is not refused for another caller's track (before: 409), is refused while rows a BED brought are stored that Postgres does not record (before: the file's rows were added to them), and replaces a record whose rows are gone. A file naming a sample the family lacks, with no record it can store, or with a gzip cut short or a line over the limit after its first records is refused (400, 413) with both callsets, the haplotype blocks, their records and the phase corrections as they were, nothing deleted or written (before: the callset deleted first, what was read stored and removed again; a gzip cut short answered 500); so is a record whose annotation cannot be read, and invalid imputed genotypes leave the blocks. A write that fails after the delete (an insert failing after the first batch) removes its rows and flags the family import-incomplete for the callset (`snv` with its source; `haplotypes` for imputed genotypes) with no job, in a commit of its own, leaving unrecorded what the upload would have recorded, a restore's whole-family entry as it was and an earlier flag's failures with their jobs; the flag goes once the callset is uploaded again (without overwrite too), not when another callset is; a failed write with nothing replaced leaves no flag, and a flag that cannot be written leaves the upload's own error. The upload's mark (SAFE-28), written on a connection of its own: the flag's write removes it, a failed write with nothing replaced removes it in its one commit, and when the flag cannot be written the mark stays, naming the callset. |
| [backend/tests/test_loaders_refuse_empty_files.py](../backend/tests/test_loaders_refuse_empty_files.py) | A loader refuses a file without a record before it deletes what the file replaces (SAFE-29; the TRGT upload's route and the package's dataset importers driven against fakes at the stores' edge: ClickHouse's interval table, Postgres's track records, TRGT calls, Paraphase results and family row). The decision kept: a chrM SV file with its header and no call replaces the sample's earlier calls with none, the others' stay, and one without its `#CHROM` line fails the `mito` dataset before anything is written. |

| [backend/tests/test_sv_rewrite_restores_deleted_rows.py](../backend/tests/test_sv_rewrite_restores_deleted_rows.py) | A rewrite of a family's structural variants puts back the rows it deleted when it fails (SAFE-28; ClickHouse's SV rows and the family row's import state faked at the stores' edge, the rewrite and its restore run as written). `rewrite_family_structural_variants` handed the rows it deletes: a write whose insert fails after the delete deletes the scope again and writes them back, raising the write's error, and so does one that fails in its delete; one whose rows cannot be put back raises `StructuralVariantsNotRestored` from the write's error. The SV upload (through its route) marks the family before its rewrite on a connection of its own and its commit removes the mark; a rewrite that fails leaves every sample's calls of the caller as they were and no mark (before: all of them gone, nothing marked); one whose rows cannot be put back, or whose process stops in it, leaves the mark naming that caller's calls of each sample, one sample at a time, and each sample's upload again with overwrite strikes its own off, the mark going with the last; an upload that fails before its rewrite leaves no mark. An update of a package's chrM calls (the real mito importer, no snapshot) puts another sample's chrM SV back, and when it cannot, flags `mito` for every sample whose chrM SVs the rows held (before: the other sample's SV gone; flagged for the dataset's own samples). |
| [backend/tests/test_upload_marks_family_before_writing.py](../backend/tests/test_upload_marks_family_before_writing.py) | A single-file upload marks the family before its first ClickHouse write (SAFE-28; the routes driven against fakes at the stores' edge, the mark's own connection committing at once). | [backend/tests/test_sv_upload_sample_column.py](../backend/tests/test_sv_upload_sample_column.py) | The per-sample SV upload reads its sample's own column (storage faked, the stored-sample lookup answered as the `samples` table would): a joint Sniffles or Spectre VCF stores that sample's genotypes and phase sets, its column found at any position, as named or with a tool suffix (`_sv_phased`); a lone column that names no known sample (`SAMPLE`, `Sample0`, a read file's name) is the sample's.
