Without END, or with END `.` (such a record was skipped before), a `<DEL>`, `<DUP>`, `<INV>` or `<CNV>` allele, subtypes and any capitals included, ends at POS + SVLEN without its sign, and an `<INS>`, a breakend, a single breakend or a sequence allele at POS + len(REF) − 1 (a sequence-resolved deletion over its REF); one of the four without SVLEN stays on its POS; a written END is kept, and a Spectre POS written as a range keeps its end. Without SVTYPE, or with SVTYPE `.`, a record takes the type its ALT states: a symbolic allele's name, BND for the four bracket forms and a single breakend (its mate read from the brackets), INS or DEL for a sequence longer or shorter than REF, `SV` for a sequence as long as REF, several ALT alleles, `.` or `*`; a written SVTYPE is kept. The NeedlR, HiFiCNV and chrM Sniffles2 package readers take a missing type by the same rule. Through the upload (SV storage and gene table faked), a deletion written with SVLEN alone is listed with the gene it spans downstream of its POS, a breakend is a BND with its mate, and an insertion is listed with the gene it lands in only. |
| [backend/tests/test_sv_length_from_alleles.py](../backend/tests/test_sv_length_from_alleles.py) | A structural variant written as a sequence allele without SVLEN is measured by its alleles (CLIN-41; the owner's decision, 2026-10-14). The rule (`sequence_allele_sv_len`, `record_sv_len`): len(ALT) − len(REF) for REF and ALT bases of other lengths, positive for an insertion and negative for a deletion, bases on both sides and any capitals included; none for a symbolic allele, breakend notation, several ALT alleles, `.`, `*`, an empty ALT or alleles as long as each other; a written SVLEN kept as written; the type a sequence allele states and its length agree. Every reader stores it: the Sniffles and Spectre uploads for an insertion and a deletion without SVLEN, with SVLEN `.` or with a written END, their END and so their id unchanged; a written SVLEN, a symbolic insertion or a breakend without one, and same-length alleles kept as before; a manual TSV row written as a sequence; the NeedlR family and per-sample readers, HiFiCNV and the chrM reader, with their own END rules and ids unchanged. The length filters measure it (a 50 bp minimum and an exact 350 keep the 350 bp insertion, an exact 100 the deletion, an exact 0 nothing); the SV list, ranked or not, shows 350 and −100 from the rows as stored, its CSV export holds the insertion, and the mtDNA page shows a chrM sequence deletion's length. Through the upload (storage faked) each call is stored with that SVLEN under its old id. A call stored without SVLEN is measured as stored, by END − POS. |

A single breakend (`G.`, `.G`) names no mate; a record that is no breakend (`<DEL>`, `<DUP:TANDEM>`, `<INS>`, `<INV>`, a sequence deletion) keeps none, its span, type and SVLEN (the sequence deletion's the length its alleles state, CLIN-41), and the id its coordinates and type give it. Records that merge by id keep their mate: one breakend in two samples' files, with or without an ID, and a line repeated in the family VCF; two breakends without an ID on one position keep their own mates, each under its own id. A breakend's id holds its mate, as an uploaded breakend's does, with a record ID or without (CLIN-43). The SV list serves the mate, the remote chromosome and start filters find the breakend by it (not by another chromosome or a later start), and the evidence a CNV classification freezes names it. |
| [backend/tests/test_needlr_coordinate_ids.py](../backend/tests/test_needlr_coordinate_ids.py) | A package's NeedlR SV is named by its coordinates and type, a breakend's with its mate, never by the record's ID (CLIN-43), through the family VCF and the per-sample reader. Two different deletions under one record ID (`Sniffles2.DEL.1S0`), in two samples' files (either first) or in one family VCF, are two SVs at their own positions with their own calls, as the SV list serves them; before, one SV at the first one's position held both calls. The id is the call's coordinates whatever its record ID (`SVDEL1`, `.`, an empty ID, one that names another SV's position), which the annotation keeps, and is the id a Sniffles upload gives the same deletion or breakend. A record without an ID keeps the id and the annotation earlier imports stored it with, and so the annotation hash a CNV classification froze. |

| [backend/tests/test_upload_safety.py](../backend/tests/test_upload_safety.py) | Bounded upload decode: read/decompressed size caps reject oversized uploads and gzip bombs (413); corrupt gzip / non-UTF-8 → 400. The same caps hold for a file read from a path (gzip told by its magic bytes, not its extension), and a multi-member (BGZF) gzip is decompressed in full, not to its first block, under the same bomb cap (#368). The reader is `bounded_download.gunzip_bounded`, answered with 413 and 400: two joined gzip files whose first member inflates past a decompression step are read whole (under a deadline: the old reader never returned there), and a member cut short or a cut-off header after the last member → 400, where the part that was there used to come back as the file. |
| [backend/tests/test_variant_upload_gene_lookup.py](../backend/tests/test_variant_upload_gene_lookup.py) | Gene lookup during variant upload: distinct sorted overlaps per window; one grouped query per chromosome set, none for empty input. |
| [backend/tests/test_clickhouse_variant_storage.py](../backend/tests/test_clickhouse_variant_storage.py) | Family-scoped variant counting and query building; every small-variant mutation stamps a new family data version (#509). Every SV insert and delete, a per-sample SV replace and a snapshot restore stamp a new SV data version, read as the token count and sum. `SNV_INDEL/entries` holds each call's FILTER values and caller metrics (`calls.filters`, `calls.metrics`), and a stored table without these columns is refused (nothing created, the assembly not marked ready). |
| [backend/tests/test_callset_storage_identity.py](../backend/tests/test_callset_storage_identity.py) | A stored variant row is one callset's (storage faked): the `SNV_INDEL/entries`, `SV/entries` and `SV/key_lookup` tables are created with sort keys that end with `source`; a small variant keeps one key whatever its callset, and an SV's key names its source while its variant id does not, a stored SV row written back keeping its key; a table found with an older sort key (quoted or not) is refused, with the table, its key, the needed key and the recovery named, by the table ensure (nothing created, the assembly not marked ready) and by the startup check, which reads every assembly's tables in one bound query and ignores import snapshots; startup runs that check straight after the ClickHouse schema and starts no monitor or worker when it refuses; the per-variant record that reviews and the drift check read orders the direct call first. |
| [backend/tests/test_clickhouse_value_bounds.py](../backend/tests/test_clickhouse_value_bounds.py) | A value beyond its ClickHouse column is stored as missing, never a failed write (SAFE-30), against a fake ClickHouse that records the column types the backend's CREATE statements give and serialises every insert for them with clickhouse-connect, so a value its column cannot hold fails as on the real client. `calls.dp` and `calls.ad` are created `UInt32` (`calls.gq` stays `UInt16`), a table with the `UInt16` types is altered once and only from those types; a small-variant upload stores a chrM call of 120,000 reads (60,000 per allele) as the file has it, an infinite GQ, a GQ beyond `UInt16`, a depth beyond `UInt32`, a negative phase set and a QUAL, allele fraction or caller metric beyond `Float32` as missing (an allele depth no column holds as 0), skips and counts a record whose POS no `UInt32` holds, and refuses a file of only such records with 400 before the stored callset is deleted; a contig named by a negative number is ranked with the other contigs in `xpos`; the per-sample files of a long-read couple keep a deep call and skip such a POS; an SV upload and the package NeedlR, HiFiCNV and chrM SV readers skip a record whose POS or END no `UInt32` holds and store an SVLEN, read support, phase set, copy number or QUAL no column holds as missing; the annotation scores and gnomAD counts are bound to their columns; a BED line or a package track row with a negative coordinate is left out (a BED of only such lines refused before the delete), and a track row's phase set no `UInt64` holds is stored as missing; no number parser raises on `inf`. |

| [backend/tests/test_bounded_download.py](../backend/tests/test_bounded_download.py) | Bounded outbound download + gunzip for reference/gene/Monarch fetches (#336): gunzip roundtrip / oversized-output / invalid-stream / a real decompression bomb halted at the cap. Every member of a multi-member gzip is read: two members, a bgzip file of ten blocks and its end-of-file block, the member after one that inflates past a decompression step (under a deadline from `_deadline.py`, since a reader that takes the next member from `unconsumed_tail` never returns). The cap counts the output of all members (their exact total passes, one byte less is refused, a bomb spread over 64 members is halted), and no more than the cap plus one byte is ever inflated. A truncated member (an empty input, a cut in the deflate data, the trailer or a later member) and bytes after the last member (garbage, zero padding, a cut-off header) are refused. Streamed size-cap abort, optional-404, and 5xx paths. |

An SV scores by its copy-number change (CLIN-47): a copy loss (DEL, DEL:ME, a CNV with one copy, none, or no copy number) as a deletion and, in a gene with pLI 0.9 or more, as haploinsufficient; a copy gain (DUP, DUP:TANDEM, a CNV with three copies) as a duplication, without that floor; a male's chrX CNV keeping his one copy, or a CNV keeping two, as neither; INS, INV and BND as before. |

The digest of a panel's genes and regions (CLIN-27) is the same for the same members in another order or read twice, none without a panel, and moves with a gene or a region; under one panel version, other members are another exact key and the same base key; both keys move with the regions the query kept to (its intervals; a gene filter keeps to none, ENG-1) and with the regions and genes it left out, and regions read in another order are the same keys; the superset candidates are read with their provenance. Both keys tell apart each inheritance mode, expanded carrier screening and the two together (what a ranked search keeps depends on them, CLIN-44), and the pedigree read from the family when it has no structure version covers each member's sex, as the structure hash does (the X-linked rules read it). A ranking cached before a SIFT or PolyPhen filter read the prediction with its score (`_ALGORITHM_VERSION` 3, CLIN-45) misses, as does one cached before the ranked search's record filter read every filter as the SQL does (`_ALGORITHM_VERSION` 5, ENG-1), and one cached before a gene panel of more than 1,000 regions, the Mendeliome, was matched by position too (`_ALGORITHM_VERSION` 6). |
| [backend/tests/test_ranking_cache_sub_panel.py](../backend/tests/test_ranking_cache_sub_panel.py) | A narrower panel served from a broader cached ranking only while that ranking's panel holds what it held when the ranking was computed (CLIN-27), through `_prioritized_small_variants_page` with the real keys, superset path and record filter over an in-memory cache, panel tables, gene reference and variants: a ranking cached before its panel gained a gene serves neither that panel (recomputed with the new gene's variant, then an exact hit) nor another panel the grown panel now covers; a ranking whose panel is unchanged, or changed version with the same genes, still serves a narrower panel or the panel itself, with the broader ranking's scores, as does a ranking without a panel; a row without the record, or whose panel was deleted (no 404), is passed over; a gene-reference reload that moves a panel gene serves neither the old exact key nor the old superset; a gene filter keeps the gene's variant just outside its loci, and a reload that moves the gene changes neither what it keeps nor its key (ENG-1); a ranking computed without the query's intervals (the warm's replay) is not served for the query. A served panel is what a fresh computation of the same panel gives (CLIN-34, CLIN-35), each compared on its variants, order, ranks, scores, segregation and total: under a Mendeliome stand-in of more than 1,000 regions, matched by its genes and its regions as every panel is (the stand-in's fetch models the SQL filter), a panel that reaches a variant by position only (its annotation names the gene by another symbol) is served from the stand-in's ranking, which holds that variant (owner decision 2026-10-14: it was matched on gene symbols alone, and the panel computed for itself); a panel whose PanelApp coordinates for a gene reach a variant no gene or locus of the stand-in reaches is computed for itself with that variant; a panel whose every variant the stand-in's ranking holds is served from it; a served panel is numbered from 1 within itself, on a later page too; with the real segregation over a trio, a panel that keeps one variant of a compound-het pair (through a gene overlapping it) is computed for itself, without the pair's mode, weight and score, or served from another ranking that holds that variant without its partner, while a panel that keeps the pair whole is served with it. |

A gene filter keeps a variant VEP assigns to the gene outside its loci, ranked or not, alone and paired (ENG-1). |

The annotation filters the family search applies per transcript (impact, consequence, ClinVar, HGVS, the frequencies with the rescue, the in-silico scores, *Canonical*, *MANE* and *LoF only*) are its own condition, in one subquery over the transcript rows and with the same parameters: *MANE only*, *Canonical only* or *LoF only* with an impact, or *MANE only* with a REVEL minimum, hold on one transcript together, and one in-silico predictor is enough. The gene, rsID and excluded ClinVar stay per variant, on the annotation index; a locus also bounds the transcript scan (CLIN-29). A gene panel is the family page's condition, with the same parameters, read for the explorer's assembly (CLIN-48): its regions (a join on 64-kb windows over the annotation index, the regions read as the family page reads them), the rows' own gene symbols and the gene index; a panel of regions only keeps the variants in its regions; a panel of genes with more than 1,000 regions is matched by its genes and its regions alike, its regions joined once (owner decision 2026-10-14: it was matched on gene symbols alone); a panel with neither keeps nothing without asking ClickHouse; a panel that does not exist answers the family page's 404; the cursor changes with the panel's genes and regions; the count and the export read the entry rows as alias `e`. The tag / classification filter reads its variant ids in variant_id order, one past its cap, and says when more matched; the review lookup binds any number of variant ids as one array parameter (50,001 ids: two parameters, not one per id). |

### Sample QC
| Test file | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| [backend/tests/test_sample_integrity_qc.py](../backend/tests/test_sample_integrity_qc.py) | Relatedness, paternity, sex inference, Mendelian consistency, QC aggregation (sample-swap/data-integrity); the NIPT profile summary names the checks that run. NIPT paternity reads the father's homozygous alleles (all expected) and heterozygous alleles (half expected) apart: another man's file fails, either kind decides when the other has too few sites, a homozygous rate between the bounds warns, and too few of both warns; an indeterminate fetal sex says whether its sites were too few or split between seen and absent. The overall verdict counts a check that could not run as a warning, never a pass, and a note names each sample whose sex could not be checked: a trio with one parent or no sample sexed, a single sample, a NIPT parent; only no check at all is not run, and a fail still outranks it (CLIN-1). |
| [backend/tests/test_validate_family_package_script.py](../backend/tests/test_validate_family_package_script.py) | `scripts/validate_family_package.py`: a valid package (the golden trio) is reported valid with exit 0; a folder without a package, or outside `FAMILY_IMPORT_ROOTS`, exits 1 with its reasons (#678). |
| [backend/tests/test_sample_integrity_service.py](../backend/tests/test_sample_integrity_service.py) | Family-scoped sample-integrity report generation. NIPT paternity ignores sites without a usable father call; haploid chrX calls are parsed, so a haploid-called father is sexed; a per-sample NIPT callset, whose Mutect2 GT is `0/1` at every call, is sexed from its allele depths (`vaf_genotype`); missing data stays missing; a sample without a call at a site of a callset it has calls in is read as reference there (one file per sample), never one with no call in it at all (see test_long_read_couple_package.py). Two sibling embryos identical over the first quarter of every chromosome read as siblings from the genome-wide sample of sites, where the first 30,000 sites of chr1–3 call them duplicates. (#742) A NIPT analysis or a genotype load that fails is logged by its kind, not by the error's text, and leaves a warning and a fixed note (`NIPT cfDNA analysis could not run.`, `Genotypes could not be loaded.`): the Sample QC as sign-out freezes it into the signed snapshot holds no part of the error, which quotes the failed statement and its parameters, or a value ClickHouse could not read. When the NIPT parents' genotypes cannot be read (the callset lookup or the chrX load fails), the same holds for their sex check (`The NIPT parents' sex could not be checked.`), and each parent's sex reads indeterminate, which the sign-out gate holds (`_unverifiable_swap_checks`); a family without a callset gets no check and no note. A trio, or NIPT parents, whose callset has no chrX site: the sex checks could not run, and the report and the Sample QC that sign-out freezes read warn, with a note naming the samples; the gate holds the NIPT parents and leaves the trio, which parent-child checks anchor, open (CLIN-1). The gate's reason for each NIPT parent names that parent, while the frozen check keeps its own message (CLIN-8). |
| [backend/tests/test_family_structure_validation.py](../backend/tests/test_family_structure_validation.py) | Pedigree-graph validation and parent-sex consistency; links of unknown degree (`relative`): accepted, refused when they add nothing, kept by an edit that does not send them. |

| [backend/tests/test_family_package_identifiers.py](../backend/tests/test_family_package_identifiers.py) | Family and sample IDs CoGA cannot store are refused before anything is written (synthetic IDs): every C0 control character and DEL is refused, and whitespace in an ID; printable text without spaces (accents included) is an ID; the message writes the character as an escape, and a validation issue never carries a raw control character. | [backend/tests/test_family_package_discovery_containment.py](../backend/tests/test_family_package_discovery_containment.py) | Discover reads only the package's own files, whatever an ID names (synthetic IDs and packages, decoy files beside them that a spy on `open`, `stat`, `lstat`, `scandir` and `glob` shows are never opened, looked up or named in the answer): a family ID from the request or the manifest that is a path out of the folder (`../decoy`, an absolute path, `../../outside/decoy`) reads no PED there (another folder's PED, or any `.ped` on the host, was read and its sample IDs and path returned) and is warned about (`family_id_leaves_package`); without a PED the draft proposes none outside the package; a PED (by the family ID, by listing, under `ped/`) or a NIPT VCF that is a symlink out of the package is not read, and a dataset's file that is one counts as absent (it was a 400); a sample ID that is a path out is looked up nowhere outside (Discover answered 400, and a `~` ID named the home folder or failed with a 500) and is warned about (`sample_id_leaves_package`); a NIPT parent ID with `..`, an absolute path or a wildcard reads no VCF beside the package (their headers were read, then a 400; a 500); a `*`, `?` or `[` in a family, sample or NIPT parent ID matches only itself, so none takes another's callset, CRAM or VCF, and an ID still finds the file named after it; the guard (`package_path_within`) refuses an absolute or climbing path before any look-up, reads `~` as a name, and stops a symlink out or a NUL; no path Discover builds from an ID leads out of the package unless the ID is a path out itself; the Discover route stays admin-only (a viewer gets 403 before Discover runs), and an admin's answer through it holds nothing of the decoy. |
| [backend/tests/test_family_package_path_guards.py](../backend/tests/test_family_package_path_guards.py) | Package paths (synthetic packages, a decoy manifest outside the import root that a spy on `open` shows is never read): a `manifest.yaml`, `.yml` or `.json` that is a symlink leading out of the package is refused (`manifest_outside_package`) by validation and Discover, which read it as the package's manifest and took its family; Write manifest answers 400 and leaves the outside file as it was (it was replaced, with or without overwrite a 409), and a dangling link out is refused and nothing created where it points (it was); the package list shows such a package under its folder's name (it showed the decoy's family and analysis type); a link that stays in the package is still read, listed and written through. A manifest path starting with `~` (`~/x.vcf`, an unknown user's `~`, `~` alone, after spaces) is refused with 400 and never expanded (`~/x.vcf` named the home folder's file, an unknown user's failed with a RuntimeError, a 500); a `~` further on is part of a name (`./~/x.vcf` was expanded too); validation refuses one as the PED, a dataset's file or the phenotypes (500 before), Discover drafts for a manifest naming one (500) and refuses one as the requested PED path (500), and a package folder under an unknown home folder is refused by Discover, validation and Write manifest (500). Symlink loops, each with this Python's `Path.resolve` and with Python 3.12's (emulated on 3.13): a package folder that loops is refused (a 500 on 3.12, "does not exist" on 3.13); a manifest path through a looping folder, or naming a looping file, is refused with 400 (500 on 3.12, a missing file on 3.13); a looping manifest is refused and not written through (Write manifest failed with an OSError); a looping PED found by listing (an OSError, 500), a file Discover finds by a pattern (500 on 3.12), a manifest's file and a long-read per-sample entry (Discover and the package list failed, 500) count as absent; the requested PED path through a loop is refused (400); a RuntimeError or an ELOOP OSError from resolving the package folder is refused as a loop, another error is not taken for one; the guard refuses a loop alike on every version and resolves the package's own files as before. The package list (a decoy package outside the import root, and a spy on what is listed, probed and opened): a package folder that is a symlink out of the import root is left out and nothing in it is looked up or read (it was listed by its path outside the root, under the decoy manifest's family and analysis type, its manifest read); one that loops is left out (as before), one linked into the staging root is left out and its manifest not read (it was listed), one linked under its own or another import root is followed and listed by the folder it leads to, read as before; selection takes every folder the list shows, and refuses the two it leaves out (`package_folder_not_allowed`). Through the routes (an admin, no database): Discover, Validate, Write manifest and the package list for each case answer 200 with the refusal or 400, never 500; the package list leaves out a folder linked out of the root or looping and names nothing outside it, and Validate refuses the linked folder typed. |

| [backend/tests/test_family_import_compensation.py](../backend/tests/test_family_import_compensation.py) | Failed-import compensation: shell delete clears interval tracks; incomplete-flag set/clear on family metadata; the flag names the import job (none for an import run outside a job) and does not copy the error texts, and removes the import's own `import_unfinished` entry in the same statement. The clear of a completed import, read under the row's lock, removes its own entry and each earlier import's entry it completed (every dataset that import had not finished imported again by an overwrite of the same scope, or nothing left unfinished); it keeps one an update completed, one with another dataset pending, one an overwrite of another SNV source, of fewer samples or of a family-level file in place of per-sample files replaced, one recording no scope, and one not in the shape an import writes, and says so when it cannot clear. A dataset's scope is what its loader replaces (the SNV callset's declared source, a per-sample dataset's samples, a family VCF winning over per-sample files); the pending datasets of an entry, and an import's key (its job, or a run of its own). The import_incomplete flag (the family row modelled per statement): a later failure keeps an earlier one's failed datasets, each with the job that holds its error, unless it imported them again; a completed import that did not import a failed dataset leaves it flagged (before: any completed import cleared the flag), one that imported it again in any mode, for its scope, clears it, a partial re-import shrinks the flag to what is still missing (its time and job kept), one of another SNV source or fewer samples leaves it, a flag recording no scope is cleared by its dataset alone, and one not in the shape an import writes stays. After a restore that failed part-way (SAFE-16): the flag names the import's datasets and the import's entry stays, naming, under `whole_family`, every dataset the family's imports recorded (`imported_scopes`), another import's unfinished one and the import's own, one sample at a time, a sample no longer the family's left out (before: the entry made way for the flag); before its restore an import marks every dataset of the family pending, or, if that cannot be written, every dataset of its own; an update leaves the entry, an overwrite of one sample removes that sample, one of the datasets that failed leaves another package's SV calls, and the last removes the entry, as does one overwrite of the whole family; a list not in the shape written is never completed; a second restore that fails keeps what the first left. A failure records what imported, and a completed import what it imported and what a stopped import it completed had finished, in `imported_scopes`, each scope once. Single-file uploads (SAFE-19; each upload's route driven against the family row, its stores faked): the small-variant, structural-variant, TRGT and BED uploads each record what they brought (`snv` with the stored callset, `sv_manual`, `repeats_trgt` and `bed_coverage`, for the sample) beside what imports recorded, read under the row's lock (`FOR NO KEY UPDATE`) and written before the upload's own commit (before: nothing was recorded); a restore that then fails part-way names it (before: the import's own datasets alone); an upload without overwrite strikes nothing off the whole-family entry, one with overwrite strikes off what it brought again, and the import of the rest removes the entry (before: the entry kept it, and only an upload can bring it); only the samples and the callset it brought (another sample's calls, the package's imputed callset stay); imputed genotypes are recorded as the `haplotypes` a package brings and complete them; each upload holds the family's write locks from its first read, the TRGT and BED uploads both (before: none); an upload whose record cannot be written fails, commits nothing of its own and removes its rows, and the small-variant upload, which had deleted the callset it replaced, then flags the family for it (SAFE-25); the dataset each upload is recorded under. An SNV import's callset (SAFE-23; real VCF files, `dataset_scopes` and the SNV importer against faked stores): an `snv` dataset whose `source_format` is `auto` is recorded as the callset its family VCF holds (`clair3` for direct calls, `glimpse2` for imputed genotypes, gzipped or phased with a header that says so), a declared one as declared, a monogenic NIPT's files as `nipt`, a long-read couple's as `clair3`, an unreadable file as `auto` (before: `auto` for every auto dataset and the NIPT files); two auto imports that stored different callsets do not strike each other off a whole-family entry, complete each other's stopped entry, or clear or drop each other's failure, while two of the same callset do (before: they did, whichever callsets they stored); an auto import and an upload of the same callset bring each other's again, of another callset not; a scope still reading `auto` (an older record) is completed by no import or upload, whatever it stored (before: by an import whose scope read `auto`); the SNV importer stores the file as the callset its import recorded, and refuses before any write, deleting nothing, a file that changed to another callset; the import loop records the resolved callset in its entry and `imported_scopes`, and hands it to the importer. Every failed scope kept (SAFE-26): two `auto` SNV imports that failed for different callsets, or a dataset that failed for other samples, stay flagged for each, each with its job (`failed_scopes`; before: the later scope replaced the earlier, and importing it again cleared the flag), until each has been brought again; the same scope failing again is one failure with its latest job; a later import strikes off only the scope it imported; an upload of one callset leaves another's failure; the flag then names, as its job, the one job that holds the errors left; a flag in the one-scope shape (`scopes`) is read as before and stays as stored until something leaves it, one whose `failed_scopes` is not as written stays; the job log names each job holding an error left. An `auto` SNV import's update check and clean-up (SAFE-27; the SNV importer against faked stores): an update counts the callset its import recorded, so imputed genotypes are imported beside direct calls and skipped once held, the skip naming the callset (before: clair3 counted whatever the file held); a failed import removes only the callset it stores (before: clair3); a file whose callset cannot be read is refused before anything is counted, written or removed, and one that changes between two reads is refused. What counts as brought again (SAFE-17): a dataset imported with warnings, or registered when its package carries nothing CoGA imports for it (marked `empty` in its scope: phenotypes without an HPO row, per-sample GLIMPSE2 BCFs, a family-level APCAD BED, a run record without versions or parameters), clears its failure, through the import loop too (before: only `imported` did), never one registered for want of an assembly or a readable file, nor an update's skip; an empty registration brings nothing again of data another import brought; a whole-family entry drops an empty dataset of its import once an overwrite registers it, never data the family holds; nothing empty is recorded in `imported_scopes`; phenotypes without an HPO row end `registered` (before: `skipped`); a dataset that ended with warnings counts as finished in its entry. Each single-file upload's mark (SAFE-28) is written before its first ClickHouse write and goes with its commit; one whose record cannot be written removes it in a commit of its own (the TRGT upload, Postgres alone, writes none). |

| [backend/tests/test_import_backup_cleanup.py](../backend/tests/test_import_backup_cleanup.py) | The backup tables a stopped overwrite import leaves are dropped (TF-06 H16). A backup is named after the import that owns it (`<assembly>/SNAPSHOT/<job id>/<table>`; a random token without one; a key that cannot name a table refused before anything is made). The listing reads `system.tables` with bound parameters and keeps only names in that form (so each is safe in a `DROP`), with their age in UTC. A job's backup is orphaned once the job is no longer queued, validating or running; one made outside a job once it is older than the grace, never when its age is unknown. A backup that cannot be dropped is left for the next attempt. The worker drops the backups of a job its claim ended as interrupted (logged, never raised, when it cannot) and runs the others; startup looks up only the owning jobs and drops what no running import owns, and a failed sweep never stops the start. |
| [backend/tests/test_import_crash_leaves_family_marked.py](../backend/tests/test_import_crash_leaves_family_marked.py) | A package import that stops part-way leaves its family marked (TF-06 H16; the family's `import_unfinished` modelled as each statement documents it, an import stopped inside a dataset by cancelling its task, so none of its fail-clean code runs). The import's entry is written before registration, with its job and datasets, and again once it holds the family's variant-write locks, and each dataset is recorded once it has finished (a failed one stays pending). An entry another import's clear removed while this one waited is written again before its first dataset (before: a stop in that dataset left neither entry nor flag); a stop while a failed overwrite is put back leaves every dataset pending (before: the ones the restore was rewriting still read finished); an import that fails after marking the family and before its first dataset leaves the failure flag naming all its datasets, one that fails before marking it leaves nothing. A failed dataset stays flagged through an import that completes without it, whose log says so (before: cleared), until one imports it again (an update will do); a later failure keeps the earlier failed dataset with its job (before: replaced); after a failed restore nothing the overwrite wrote counts as imported again: the overwrite names each of its datasets as failed, with its own job (SAFE-13). Stopped inside a dataset, the entry names what it had not finished and nothing is flagged or cleared; run to its end, the entry goes: with the clear (completed), swapped for the `import_incomplete` flag (failed), alone (a failed overwrite put back; its ClickHouse backup named after the import), or with the family (a new family nothing imported into). After a stop, an update completes but leaves the family marked and its log says why (it skipped the half-written dataset); an overwrite without that dataset leaves it, one with it clears it. A job run again keeps its earlier attempt's log lines; a running job keeps its heartbeat until it ends; once the job is no longer its worker's, the heartbeat stops, and stops the import with it (before: the import went on to its end); the worker runs no job the claim ended as interrupted. A stop while a failed overwrite is put back leaves every dataset of the family pending, the SV calls an earlier import brought too (SAFE-16; before: only the overwrite's own). |
| [backend/tests/test_import_job_names_its_family_first.py](../backend/tests/test_import_job_names_its_family_first.py) | A package import writes nothing of a family before its job names that family (TF-06 H16; the job row modelled as Postgres keeps it: an update applies when its WHERE clause matches and is seen once committed, an injected fault fails a statement as a database error does; each write of the family recorded with whether the sign-out's job check would find the import then). The job of a re-import whose request names no family commits `running` on the family before the import's first write, and every write happens under a job a sign-out finds. When that update fails, or matches no row because another worker claimed the job, the import writes nothing (no registration, no locks, no dataset); the job ends `failed` with the reason, or, when it is another worker's, is left to that worker; when not even the job's end can be recorded, nothing is written and the job stays claimed. A progress update that fails once the job names its family does not stop the import; a dry run and an invalid package name no family as running and write nothing (#736). |
| [backend/tests/test_import_stale_heartbeat.py](../backend/tests/test_import_stale_heartbeat.py) | A live import whose heartbeat goes stale (#746, TF-06 H16; the job row, the family's import state and its ClickHouse tables modelled as their statements document them). An import held inside a dataset whose heartbeat cannot be written runs on (a failed beat stops nothing); once a claim has ended its job and the heartbeat reaches the database again, the import is stopped where it is: its later datasets never run, the family keeps its `import_unfinished` entry with nothing finished and no flag, its variant-write locks are released, the job keeps the record the claim gave it, and the log names the job (before: it went on, finished its datasets and removed the entry under an interrupted job). An import that has ended is left to record its end; the worker's own stop goes on up, also when it comes as the heartbeat stops the import. An end or a progress update the job cannot record is logged by the job and the outcome alone, the progress once, and `_update_job_progress` says whether it recorded (before: nothing said). The job statements read no host clock: the heartbeat and the claim stamp `now()`, and the stale check compares with `now()`. A restore checks its whole backup before its first delete: with one table missing it deletes nothing and raises `FamilyRestoreRefused` (before: it deleted table by table up to the missing one); with all there it deletes (each mutation waited for) and refills each table in turn. An overwrite whose backups the claiming worker dropped keeps the family's rows as the overwrite left them, and its flag names coverage as failed and snv as imported (before: the restore deleted the small variants, and the flag still said snv did import). A restore that fails other than by that refusal (part-way in ClickHouse, the small variants deleted, or in Postgres after the ClickHouse restore) leaves a flag naming every dataset the import set out to import as failed, those it imported before and after the failure and one it only registered, each with the import's job and scope, and none as imported, and the sign-out reads them all (SAFE-13; before: the one that had failed alone); after one that failed part-way, an import of the dataset that had failed alone completes but leaves snv flagged, and its log says so (before: it cleared the flag, the small variants gone), and the import of snv clears it; a restore that succeeds still leaves no flag and removes the import's entry. A restore that failed part-way keeps the import's entry, every dataset of the family pending, and its log says to import the whole family again (SAFE-16; before: the entry made way for the flag); when the restore removed SV calls another import brought, an overwrite of the datasets that failed completes and clears the flag, but the entry keeps the SV calls and the gate stays shut, its log naming them (before: the gate opened, the SV calls gone); an update of the SV calls leaves it, and their overwrite opens the gate, as does one overwrite of the whole family. |
| [backend/tests/test_import_update_skips_by_stored_rows.py](../backend/tests/test_import_update_skips_by_stored_rows.py) | An update skips what the store holds, and a skip never counts as an import (SAFE-20, TF-06 H16; ClickHouse's interval table and the Postgres statements on `sample_interval_track_sources`, `sample_paraphase_results` and the family's row faked at their edge). An update reads a track's rows, not Postgres's record of it: coverage (BED and per-target table), APCAD (per sample and family VCF), PCF and WisecondorX tracks a part-way restore removed while Postgres still records them are imported again (before: skipped as there), and stored rows are left, recorded or not. A dataset an update skips whole ends `skipped` (before: `imported`, for coverage, APCAD, PCF, WisecondorX and Paraphase); the samples it skips of a dataset it imports for others, HiFiCNV's included, are recorded (`DatasetImportJob.skipped_samples`), and `imported_dataset_scopes` leaves them out of what the import imported again. Through the import and the real coverage importer, for a family whose coverage and small variants a failed restore flagged: an update that imports the coverage the restore removed clears its failure (before: cleared with the rows still gone), and one that skips the coverage, or one of its samples, leaves it flagged (before: cleared); the restore's whole-family entry stays. |

| Test file | Purpose |

| [backend/tests/integration/test_app_startup.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_app_startup.py) | Boots the app against real Postgres + ClickHouse (schema init, admin seed) and asserts it serves. |
| [backend/tests/integration/test_genotype_classes_clickhouse.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_genotype_classes_clickhouse.py) | #511 on real ClickHouse: every short genotype string and a set of long/malformed ones land in exactly the class `classify_genotype` gives them, through the SQL set lookup and its allele-by-allele fallback. |
| [backend/tests/integration/test_hemizygous_positions_clickhouse.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_hemizygous_positions_clickhouse.py) | #545 on real ClickHouse: the de novo pre-filter's hemizygous-position condition agrees with `hemizygous_chromosome` on both sides of every PAR boundary, under each chromosome name, for GRCh38 and GRCh37. |
| [backend/tests/integration/test_small_variant_filter_parity_clickhouse.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_small_variant_filter_parity_clickhouse.py) | ENG-1 on real ClickHouse: every field of `SmallVariantQueryFilters` keeps the same variants in the family search's SQL (`_fetch_small_variant_rows`, with its count) and in its Python (`_kept_small_candidates` and the inheritance rules), and exactly the variants the documented rule keeps. Seventeen synthetic variants of a trio are written through the import's write path, annotated as the import parses VEP's CSQ; the cases cover the location (chromosome, start, end, overlap), the intervals and excluded intervals, the variant type, callset and phase set, the gene, transcript, HGVS and rsID, impact and consequence, *Canonical*, *MANE* and *LoF only*, ClinVar (kept and left out, the frequency rescue), every frequency and count, the in-silico scores and predictions, the panel (one of more than 1,000 regions by its genes and its regions alike, owner decision 2026-10-14), the excluded genes (by name and by locus), the second hit, the per-sample genotype class, GQ, DP, AF and AD alt, the review selections, the inheritance modes and the default view's preset; for a search that pairs (compound het, recessive, carrier screening) the SQL prefilter keeps every variant the pairing keeps. A field added to the filters fails the test until it has a case. The family page opened plain and ranked keeps the same variants: the default view's preset, and a gene filter, alone and with de novo, compound het, recessive or carrier screening, keeps a variant annotated with the gene outside its loci in the gene reference. |
| [backend/tests/integration/test_genotype_site_sample_clickhouse.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_genotype_site_sample_clickhouse.py) | The Sample QC's genotype sample on real ClickHouse: exactly the family's smallest-key sites of the callset, on every autosome, the same on a second run, and every site when the family has fewer; chrX outside both PARs under either name (X, 23); never chrY, an unplaced contig, another callset or another family; a variant stored under two projects counted once. Through the QC service, sibling embryos identical over the first quarter of each chromosome read as siblings. (#742) |

| [backend/tests/integration/test_append_only_triggers.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_append_only_triggers.py) | Fires the append-only triggers on `audit_log_events`/`clinical_audit_events`/`report_signouts`/`qc_threshold_changes`: UPDATE/DELETE rejected; the FK→NULL unlink carve-out allowed (and nothing else, not even re-attributing a QC-limit change); signed report frozen. |
| [backend/tests/integration/test_audit_batch_row_retry_integration.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_audit_batch_row_retry_integration.py) | A row Postgres refuses costs only itself, through the real workers on real Postgres (TF-13 S-5): of three queued request-audit events, one names a deleted user (the `user_id` foreign key), and of three UI events, one has a time the driver cannot encode (year 1 at +05:00); each fails every attempt of its batch INSERT. Written one at a time after the last attempt, the other two of each batch are stored, in queue order, and only the refused event is counted and logged with its payload; a stored row is not logged and no line quotes the database's error. Before, the whole batch was lost. Its UI events are removed at the end. |
| [backend/tests/integration/test_review_list_acmg_integration.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_review_list_acmg_integration.py) | A review saved with ACMG criteria through the real writer comes back through the real list path on real Postgres with them (state, strength, evidence, the recomputed class, points and VUS tier), as a single-review read serves it; an unchanged re-save keeps them; a review that holds only an ACMG record counts in the family's review summary. |
| [backend/tests/integration/test_hash_chain_integration.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_hash_chain_integration.py) | End-to-end per-family hash chains (#261): real writers produce a chain `verify_*_chain` accepts — including structural-variant / CNV review saves interleaved with a small-variant event, with a CNV point total that rounds to -0.0, and criterion-level changes (a strength, evidence with non-ASCII text, a suggestion, an unaccepted CNV criterion) with the class unchanged; a privileged trigger-bypass UPDATE/DELETE is detected & localised; the FK→NULL carve-out does not break the chain. |
| [backend/tests/integration/test_clinical_audit_paging_integration.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_clinical_audit_paging_integration.py) | A family's clinical audit trail read a page at a time is the whole trail (AUDIT-1), on real Postgres with events from the real writer: of 205 events the default page holds the newest 200, counts 205 and says older ones remain, and the page after it the other five; pages of seven read one after another hold every event once, in the hash chain's order reversed, and the chain still verifies; an event written between two page reads is not on the older page, which neither repeats nor skips one, and the first page read again leads with it; a `before` naming another family's event, an unknown id or no UUID is answered 404; an empty trail is listed as empty. |
| [backend/tests/integration/test_import_backup_cleanup_integration.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_import_backup_cleanup_integration.py) | The backup tables of an import that stopped part-way are dropped, in real Postgres and ClickHouse: backups made under their import's key and listed by it from `system.tables`; the import jobs that may still run read from Postgres; at startup the backups of an ended job dropped while a running job's and a recent one made outside a job stay, and an old one made outside a job goes; the worker that ends a stopped job drops that job's backups. |
| [backend/tests/integration/test_import_crash_bookkeeping_integration.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_import_crash_bookkeeping_integration.py) | What an import that stops part-way leaves, in real Postgres: the `import_unfinished` entry is written and rewritten without touching the family's other metadata or another import's entry, and a value that is not a map is kept, not dropped; the failure flag, the end of a put-back import and a completed import's clear remove what they should (the map goes once empty, an entry goes only for an overwrite of its scope) and the sign-out reader reads what they leave; an import that failed before its first dataset has its entry swapped for the flag, and one that never marked the family changes nothing; a later failure keeps those failed datasets, each with its job, a completed import that imported some of them again (one for fewer samples than failed does not count) shrinks the flag to the others, and the import of the last clears it; the pipeline parameters are set without writing back a stale copy. Of three stale jobs the claim ends the one that was running (`failed`, interrupted, its log and dataset summaries kept, a line added), claims the validating one again (its log kept, a line added) and the queued one as before; a running job with a fresh heartbeat is left alone, and only the job's worker can beat its heartbeat. The heartbeat is stamped and judged by the database's clock: with this host's clock an hour ahead, a job whose heartbeat is nine minutes old is left alone and one eleven minutes old is ended, its end and its log line at the database's time (before: the host's clock took the live one for stopped); a beat and a progress update stamp the database's time, and an update from a worker that no longer holds the job changes nothing and says so (#746). After a failed overwrite whose restore failed part-way, the import's entry names every dataset the family's imports recorded (`imported_scopes`) besides its own, the sign-out readers read it with the flag, an overwrite of the datasets that failed leaves the SV calls in it, an update removes nothing, and the overwrite of the SV calls removes it; a refused restore still swaps the entry for the flag (SAFE-16). A single-file upload's record (SAFE-19): written in the upload's transaction (another session sees it only once the upload commits), the other metadata kept; a package import's bookkeeping waits for an upload's record that has not committed, and the record for the import's locked read, neither losing the other's scopes; a restore that fails part-way names the uploads' data, an import of the package's datasets leaves it in the entry, an upload without overwrite strikes nothing off, and the uploads with overwrite strike off what they brought, the last removing the entry. |
| [backend/tests/integration/test_signout_import_gate_integration.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_signout_import_gate_integration.py) | Incomplete-import sign-out gate on the real schema: a family flagged by the real import helper is refused sign-out (409 naming the failed datasets and the import job; no sign-out row, no audit event); an acknowledgement needs a reason (422); an acknowledged sign-out freezes the flag and the reason in the hashed snapshot, the list/detail reads extract them and the audit event records them. A sign-out stored before the gate still verifies, on read and in the chain, and reads as no acknowledgement recorded; the real clear helper leaves the flag after an import that did not import the failed datasets again and removes it after one that did; then sign-out needs no acknowledgement and the sign-out check sees the import state changed. |
| [backend/tests/integration/test_annotation_manifest_integration.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_annotation_manifest_integration.py) | An admin's replacement of a family's annotation manifest, on real Postgres: the real writers put a clinical audit event with the replaced and the new manifest on the family's hash chain, the chain still verifies, and when the event cannot be written the manifest is not replaced. With one writer paused mid-flight, an import started during a replacement leaves it in place, and a replacement started during an import records that import's manifest as its prior. |
| [backend/tests/integration/test_small_variant_evidence_drift_integration.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_small_variant_evidence_drift_integration.py) | The frequencies and in-silico scores a small-variant ACMG classification freezes (CLIN-7), on real Postgres and ClickHouse: variants written through the import's write path with VEP-parsed annotations; the real review save freezes the primary transcript's values, those the variant list serves the ACMG dialog; read back from JSONB, unchanged evidence is current, and so is a snapshot stored the way the writer stored it before; a re-import that moves REVEL and the gnomAD genome frequency reads as drift naming each value, with its value from and to, while the older snapshot stays current; sign-out is refused until the drift is acknowledged with a reason, and the signed record holds the frozen values and what moved, which the sign-out check computes again as unchanged; saving the classification again clears the drift. The same for the consequence, LoF call and gene missense Z: the saves freeze the MANE Select transcript's consequence and LOFTEE call, and a missense variant's missense Z from the gene's profile in Postgres, as the variant list serves them, a stop gained of the same gene freezing none; a gene profile refresh that moves the missense Z reads as drift for the missense variant only, and a re-import that adds a consequence term to the stop gained and turns LC into HC as drift naming both, while a snapshot frozen before these values stays current; the sign-out gate, the signed record and its check; saving again clears it. The snapshot's numbers survive the JSONB round trip. |
| [backend/tests/integration/test_sv_evidence_drift_integration.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_sv_evidence_drift_integration.py) | SV/CNV classification evidence on real Postgres and ClickHouse: the evidence is read as the SV page reads the SV, and another family's SV, one only in samples the view does not show, or one on an assembly without SV storage reads as absent. End to end through the real review save, manifest, drift query, ClickHouse reads and sign-out writer: a CNV scoring freezes its evidence and the SV callset's versions; a sign-out stored before SV evidence was frozen still verifies, the section it lacks is not compared, and its reported CNV reads as changed (#681); unchanged evidence signs out without acknowledgement; a re-import that changes the CNV's genes is refused (nothing written or audited) until acknowledged with a reason, frozen with the drift and audited; saving the scoring again clears the drift; a reported SV whose scoring froze no evidence gates; the whole signed history is one verified chain. An uploaded Sniffles call of the same deletion is a row of its own, read with its own evidence, alone when its caller is named, and none is read for a caller that does not call the SV (TRACE-11). In a family whose id gives the upload's row the lower storage key, the real save freezes the row of the caller it names, refuses a save naming no caller (422) or a caller without a row (404) and writes nothing then, and takes the one row of an SV one caller calls; the drift check compares evidence frozen from the NeedlR row with that row: current while it is unchanged, drifted when a re-import moves its genes, `variant_missing` once NeedlR's call is gone though the upload still calls the SV. |
| [backend/tests/integration/test_app_role_privileges.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_app_role_privileges.py) | Privilege separation (#262): the restricted `coga_app` role may INSERT/SELECT all five append-only tables (and delete users — the FK cascade still nulls the audit FK, `qc_threshold_changes.changed_by` included) but is denied UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE and DISABLE TRIGGER on them. The tables with a `*_block_mutation` trigger are read from the catalogue and must all be listed, so a new append-only table without its REVOKE fails the test. The schema-migration ledger `alembic_version` (ENG-3): `coga_app` holds only SELECT on it, and its INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE are refused. |

| [backend/tests/integration/test_reference_import_record_integration.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_reference_import_record_integration.py) | Reference import records on the real schema, in a rolled-back transaction: a gene import writes the GENCODE version and date it was given, and an upload writes `not stated` with no date, under its own source. |
| [backend/tests/integration/test_repeat_status_storage.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_repeat_status_storage.py) | Every status a TRGT call can get is stored (real Postgres): a male's two different FMR1 alleles (`review`, #757) and a female's premutation (`premutation`), which the `repeat_expansions` status constraint refused, failing the whole repeat dataset. A catalogue seeded with the built-in FMR1 row's old full-mutation threshold (200) holds 201 after the next start's seed, and a male's chrX allele imported with TRID `FMR1` is then stored as `premutation` at 200 and `pathogenic` at 201. Built-in rows seeded with their thresholds from before CLIN-22 hold their STRchive row's after the next start (BEAN1's intermediate threshold NULL); calls imported under the old rows by gene-name TRID keep their stored status, while the family table reads them with the catalogue row's thresholds at once (ATXN1 39 `pathogenic`, ATXN2 34 and TBP 41 `intermediate`, BEAN1 300 `pathogenic` with no intermediate threshold), as a new import then stores them. A call at a locus without thresholds (an uncatalogued TRID, STRchive's ADTKD_MUC1) is stored `not_assessed` beside a catalogued `pathogenic` one, and one stored as `normal` before reads not assessed in the family table at once (CLIN-26). At STRchive's CPEO_POLG (benign exactly 10, no threshold) a woman's 10/10 is stored `normal` and a man's 10/13 `not_assessed`, MUC1 stays `not_assessed`; her POLG call stored as `not_assessed` before reads normal in the family table at once (the benign range read from the catalogue's JSONB), while her genome track keeps the stored status until the call is imported again, then shows `normal` (CLIN-36, CLIN-14). |
| [backend/tests/integration/test_repeat_multi_locus_storage.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_repeat_multi_locus_storage.py) | The shipped STRchive release and ARX's repeat tracts on real Postgres (CLIN-31): a STRchive row the release does not list (another release's, as an earlier start left it) is gone after the next start, while the release's 75 loci and the built-in rows stay. TRID `ARX` at `PRTS_ARX`'s and at `EIEE1_ARX`'s region is stored and read by the family table against each tract's own thresholds (17 GCN repeats normal at `PRTS_ARX`, pathogenic at `EIEE1_ARX`); a call between the tracts is stored `not_assessed` with its reason, and the family table, whose query matched it to `EIEE1_ARX` by the gene's name, reads it not assessed with the reason as its note. |

| [backend/tests/integration/test_integrity_anchor_integration.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_integrity_anchor_integration.py) | Signed chain-head anchor end-to-end: a real signed anchor verifies; an owner-bypass re-chain (recomputed head row_hash) or truncation (deleted head) diverges from it; a tampered anchor signature is caught; anchors chain (prev_anchor_hash). Without a signing key an anchor is written unsigned and verifies as unverifiable, and one signed by a key that has since been rotated reads `unknown_key`, never a pass. |

It checks what its own jobs add, then removes them. |

| [backend/tests/integration/test_gene_panel_version_history.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_gene_panel_version_history.py) | Gene-panel version history on real Postgres (REQ-CARR-003): a create and an edit archive versions 1 and 2 and an edit that changes nothing archives none; the history lists them newest first, for the panel id in capitals and braces too, and each reads back with its own genes, regions (with their assembly) and description through the real jsonb round trip; the schema refuses a second archive under a version number already used, and that version stays as it was. A PanelApp import archives version 1; imported again with the same genes it keeps the version and archives nothing (the genes and regions read back from the real tables compare equal); with another gene it archives version 2, both read back with their genes and regions, PanelApp's version stays the release imported, and the panel's part of the ranking-cache key moves. Its records are its own and are removed at the end. |

| [backend/tests/integration/test_clinical_cnv_kb_one_active_job.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_clinical_cnv_kb_one_active_job.py) | One clinical CNV knowledgebase rebuild at a time, on real Postgres: a rebuild is refused (409) while another is queued or running (a running one used to let a second job in, which then stayed queued for good) and accepted once none is; two requests at the same moment get one job. A job whose worker is gone (still queued, or no heartbeat, past the ten-minute stale window) is closed as failed by the status or a rebuild request, which is then accepted, while one that still heartbeats is kept, and its old worker can no longer write to it. A fresh table gets the one-active index and the heartbeat columns. |

| [backend/tests/integration/test_variant_explorer_export_integration.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_variant_explorer_export_integration.py) | The Variant Explorer CSV export on real ClickHouse + Postgres (a seeded project, family, reviews and five variants): a lowered row cap cuts the file to the most-carried variants and reports `row-limit`, a cap at or above the match count does not; with the tag filter's cap lowered below the tagged variants, the search takes the first in variant_id order and reports `review-filter-limit`; the review lookup takes 40,001 variant ids (more than a statement's 32,767 parameters). |

Each lookup of an export's rows (the small-variant and SV reviews, the gene constraint by symbol and by gene id, the SV second hit) takes 50,001 ids and finds exactly the rows seeded for them, one past the 32,767th among them; no ids read no rows. One family's 50,001 small variants and 50,001 SVs, exported through the routes, give files of the first 50,000 rows named `…-TRUNCATED-first-50000.csv`, with `X-CoGA-Export-Truncated: true` and reason `row-limit`; their rows carry their reviews, the row past the 32,767th too, and the small variants their gene constraint and SV second hit. |
| [backend/tests/integration/test_variant_explorer_mane_only_integration.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_variant_explorer_mane_only_integration.py) | The explorer's *MANE only* end-to-end (CLIN-5, real ClickHouse): three variants written through the import's write path from VEP CSQ annotations — MANE Plus Clinical only, MANE Select, neither. With *MANE only* the search (count and rows) and the CSV export keep the first two and drop the third; without it all three are listed. |
| [backend/tests/integration/test_variant_explorer_per_transcript_filters_integration.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_variant_explorer_per_transcript_filters_integration.py) | The explorer keeps and drops what the family search does (CLIN-29, real ClickHouse): six variants written through the import's write path from VEP CSQ annotations whose transcripts disagree (HIGH off the MANE transcript, LoF off it, a damaging REVEL off it, CADD without REVEL, a common likely pathogenic one). For eleven filter sets (*MANE only*, *Canonical only* or *LoF only* with an impact; *MANE only* with a REVEL minimum or a ClinVar status as VCF writes it; CADD or REVEL; the frequency ceiling with and without the ClinVar rescue) the family page's search (rows and count), the family search's Python matcher, and the explorer's search (rows and count, also narrowed to the locus) and CSV export keep exactly the same variants. |
| [backend/tests/integration/test_variant_explorer_panel_regions_integration.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_variant_explorer_panel_regions_integration.py) | The explorer's panel-region join keeps exactly what the family page's region rule, tested against each row (`_small_region_filter_condition`), keeps (CLIN-48, real ClickHouse, no table written): both conditions run over the same 1,392 synthetic variants (chromosomes spelled 1, chr1, X, chrX, M, MT, chrM and an unplaced contig; positions on each side of three 64-kb window boundaries; REFs of 1 to 50 bases and longer than one and two windows), for regions spelled with and without chr, reversed, repeated, of one base, on or across a window boundary, spanning several windows, and without a chromosome. |
| [backend/tests/integration/test_family_panel_regions_integration.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_family_panel_regions_integration.py) | The family page's panel-region join keeps exactly what testing each row against every region keeps (ENG-15, real ClickHouse, no table written): both conditions run in the search's own WHERE over #857's 1,392 synthetic variants and regions (chromosomes spelled 1, chr1, X, chrX, M, MT, chrM and an unplaced contig; positions on each side of three 64-kb window boundaries; REFs of 1 to 50 bases and longer than one and two windows; regions spelled with and without chr, reversed, repeated, of one base, on or across a boundary, spanning several windows, and without a chromosome; regions that share bases, nested or in a chain across a boundary, as a gene's transcript loci do, which are bound merged), the family's rows split over two projects, beside the same rows of another family, of a project the search does not read, and collapsed (`sign = -1`). Without a locus and within five loci (overlapping or not, from or up to a position) the two keep the same rows, only the family's own, in both projects, and so does a third query that tests each region on its own, unmerged. |
| [backend/tests/integration/test_gcs_storage_integration.py](../backend/tests/integration/test_gcs_storage_integration.py) | GCS storage backend end-to-end against fake-gcs-server (real JSON API): seeds a bucket and asserts `object_exists`, `download_prefix` (nested-prefix layout), remote package discovery (`list_remote_package_candidates` — manifest vs `.ped` vs neither), `remote_object_exists` and `remote_object_identity` (size, generation, MD5/CRC32C). Imports a package's alignments from `gs://<bucket>/imports/<family>/` as Terraform lays it out: staging leaves the CRAM in the store, the importer records the object's URI on the sample (real Postgres), and the CRAM endpoint resolves that recorded object; the raw-file provenance rows (real Postgres) hash the staged file and identify the CRAM by the store's record, and Verify checks them against the store (an object below `FAMILY_IMPORT_ROOTS`, the only place it asks about, SEC-29): the same object verifies, a replaced one (same size, new generation) is a mismatch, a deleted one is missing. Endpoint from `GCS_ENDPOINT_URL`, else a container started via docker. Complements the mocked unit tests; signed-URL signing stays unit-only (needs real IAM SignBlob). |

### End-to-end (golden pipeline)
| Test file | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_import_golden.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_import_golden.py) | M1 golden-trio E2E (real Postgres + ClickHouse): imports a hand-curated package through `execute_family_package_import` and asserts every stage against `fixtures/golden_trio/EXPECTED.yaml` — datasets imported, pedigree/roles/HPO persisted, SNV count, compound-het pairing (trans by segregation), SV ingest (each NeedlR SV stored under the id its coordinates give it, the breakend's with its mate, its record ID kept in its annotation: CLIN-43), SNV+SV second-hit (trans/deletion-unmasked), repeat pathogenic classification, coverage tracks, Paraphase. Doubles as an IVDR verification dataset. The fixture is regenerated by [scripts/generate_golden_trio.py](../scripts/generate_golden_trio.py). |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_api_contract.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_api_contract.py) | M2 API-contract E2E (real stack, in-loop ASGI client): drives the viz-feeding HTTP endpoints over the golden-trio data and asserts their contracts — auth 401, small-variant page (`_id` alias, SNV/INDEL types, offset paging, compound-het groups), structural-variant page (summary, `length==svLen`, the NeedlR BND with the mate its ALT names and the DEL with none, each under the id its coordinates give it (CLIN-43), track-mode null-omission), a search by the mate's position (`remote_chr`, `remote_start`) that finds the NeedlR breakend alone and one by another chromosome that finds nothing (CLIN-40), structural-variant-lengths, shared-SV counts symmetry, BED json/text + 400/404, haplotypes + batch, track-availability, and the variant explorer (carrier counts, MNV classification, keyset pagination + carriers); a ClinVar-pathogenic variant survives a frequency ceiling in ClickHouse only with the override on (#534). |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_review_audit.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_review_audit.py) | M3 review/audit/sign-out E2E (real stack): PUTs a clinical review on the P/LP variant and asserts server-side ACMG recompute (bogus client totals ignored) + note/tags persisted; the review emits chained `clinical_audit_events` and `/admin/integrity/verify` re-walks them to `verified=true`; report sign-out versions (v→v+1) with a verified sign-out chain; the sign-out check matches straight after signing (real JSONB round-trip) and flags a review edited afterwards (#508), while the latest signed version, read back, still verifies and holds the review as signed. The small-variant list serves the reviewed variant with its ACMG criteria, as the save returned them. Chain tamper-evidence (trigger-bypass edit flips `verified` to false) and append-only immutability are exercised on a throwaway family via the direct audit service so FAM_TRIO's chain is never corrupted. |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_signed_analysis_record.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_signed_analysis_record.py) | The signed analysis record E2E (real stack, golden trio): a panel applied, applied again, edited to version 2 and applied by the SV search is on the clinical audit trail once per version; a small variant classified and an SV tagged for the report are signed out; the record holds each reported variant's description as reviewed, both panel versions, the reference-data releases, the HTT repeat call with its status and thresholds, the GBA Paraphase result and the sentence that says what it is, verifies, chains and still matches right after sign-out; its report code is in the response, the detail and the list, finds it as typed back, a swapped code is refused (422), and an unknown code and one outside the caller's projects answer the same 404; a record signed as by a build without the sections verifies, reads, is found by its code, and the check names the sections it predates as not compared. The module ends with nothing tagged for the report, so a module run after it on the shared golden trio (`test_e2e_report_lists_complete`) lists only its own reported variants. |

Each scenario uses a fresh family/sample id so the shared dev DB stays clean. |

| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_request_body_uuid.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_request_body_uuid.py) | A UUID a request body names a record by E2E (real Postgres and ClickHouse, in-loop ASGI client that answers an app failure with 500; synthetic project, family in it and variant tag; the seeded admin and a viewer of the project): the `assembly_id` of a NIPT artifact add, auto-seed and table import and the `project_id` of an import request that is no UUID, or a UUID of no record (as it is, in braces, after `urn:uuid:`), or a blank project, is answered 404 (before, 500) and nothing is written for it: no artifact, no audit event, no import job; each spelling of the real id (braces, `urn:uuid:`, capitals, no hyphens) names the record, and the artifact, its audit event and the job hold the canonical id; an import naming no project (`null`, `""`) is queued without one; the artifact list's audit chain grows by exactly the accepted changes and verifies; the viewer is refused (403) by each route whatever the id; each answer is in `audit_log_events` under its caller's name; every `*_id` field a request body carries is known as a UUID or as text, and every operation, with a value that is no UUID, a UUID in braces and a `urn:uuid:` UUID in each UUID body field in turn, answers as it answers an unknown UUID or with a 400 or 422, none with a 500, and makes nothing. A project's `reader_user_ids` is one of the UUID body fields swept. |

Four panels, made through the PanelApp import (PanelApp answered by the test) and the panel API (regions only; a gene and the region; a gene reached by its locus; more than 1,000 regions, matched by its genes and its regions alike, so it keeps the locus variant too, owner decision 2026-10-14), each sent as one query string to `GET /families/{family_id}/small-variants` (unranked and ranked) and to the explorer's search and CSV export at the variants' locus: all keep exactly the expected variants, and a panel that does not exist is answered 404 by each. On main the explorer kept every variant for the panel of regions only, only the gene's for the gene and the region, none for the locus, and listed every variant for a panel that does not exist. |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_family_panel_region_join.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_family_panel_region_join.py) | The family search keeps what it kept for a gene panel's regions, on every path (E2E, real stack, in-loop ASGI client, ENG-15): a trio made by the PED upload, under a new id each run, gets a VEP-annotated VCF through the family small-variant upload (variants on each side of a region's first and last base, deletions that end on a region's first base or one before it, one that starts in the 64-kb window before a region, variants in a region spanning several windows, a compound-het pair, a homozygous variant, a gene both parents carry in). A panel of PanelApp regions (the PanelApp import, PanelApp answered by the test), one of two genes with gene-reference loci seeded for the run (one spelled chr7, one over variants annotated with another gene), and the same two genes with 1,000 filler genes (more than 1,000 regions, as the Mendeliome has) go to the family search unranked, ranked, at a locus, with compound-het and recessive pairing, with expanded carrier screening (unranked and ranked), as the genome-browser track and to the CSV export (unranked and ranked), once with the window join and once with the per-row test patched back in, over an emptied ranking cache each time: every answer is the same (variants, order, pairs, totals and flags, scores and ranks, the exported file), each panel keeps exactly its expected variants, the panel of more than 1,000 regions on every path exactly what the panel of its two genes keeps, the variants reached by a gene's locus only included (owner decision 2026-10-14: it was matched on gene symbols alone), and the panel queries sent carry the join. The panels and seeded genes are removed at the end. |

Ten filter sets (none; each SIFT and each PolyPhen prediction alone; SIFT or PolyPhen), each sent as one query string to `GET /families/{family_id}/small-variants` unranked (the SQL), ranked (the Python matcher after it), in the recessive mode (the Python matcher) and the homozygous recessive mode (the SQL), and to the explorer's search and CSV export at the variants' locus. | [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_filter_transcript.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_filter_transcript.py) | A variant kept on another transcript than the one its fields describe names that transcript, in every view (E2E, real stack, in-loop ASGI client, CLIN-51): a family made by the PED upload, under a new id each run, one affected proband homozygous for each variant, gets a VEP-annotated VCF through the family small-variant upload, three variants (SIFT tolerated on the canonical transcript and deleterious on a second, the ready case of the CLIN-45 test; one deleterious transcript; synonymous on the canonical transcript and missense on a second). Five filter sets (none; SIFT deleterious; PolyPhen probably damaging; SIFT tolerated; impact HIGH or MODERATE), each sent to `GET /families/{family_id}/small-variants` unranked, ranked (computed, then served from the ranking cache), recessive and homozygous recessive, and to the family's CSV export unranked and ranked. Every view keeps each variant's own transcript, SIFT and PolyPhen, and names the transcript the filters held on (`filter_transcript`; the *Filter-matched* CSV columns) with its consequence, SIFT and PolyPhen exactly when the variant's own transcript does not meet them; the ranked score of the ready case is the same with and without a filter. On main no view names one. |

The child's Sniffles VCF, written without END or SVTYPE, is uploaded through `POST /structural-variants/upload/{sample_id}` and read back through `GET /families/{family_id}/structural-variants`: a 1 kb `<DEL>` with SVLEN alone is stored on 1:30000-31000, listed with the gene, and found by a search for the gene and for the SVs overlapping a region inside it, not by a search for the SVs within a region it reaches beyond; a breakend (`N]5:3000000]`) is a BND with its mate, found by a BND search and by a 10 Mb minimum length; an `<INS>` stays on its POS; a sequence-resolved 100 bp deletion is a DEL over its REF. A sequence-resolved 350 bp insertion written with SVLEN `.` (CLIN-41) is an INS on its POS: the list shows it as 350 bp and the deletion as −100 (the calls with SVLEN as written), a 50 bp minimum keeps it with every other call, and an exact length of 350 or 100 finds the insertion or the deletion alone. |

| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_per_sample_column.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_per_sample_column.py) | Per-sample file E2E (real stack, in-loop ASGI client): a package with each trio member's own TRGT (`<sample>_sort`), chrM and HiFiCNV (`Sample0`) file stores each member's own calls; a second import whose child entries point at the mother's chrM and HiFiCNV files fails both datasets and changes nothing; the mother's TRGT file uploaded for the child (`overwrite=true`) is refused (400) and the child's calls stay (the overwrite's delete rolls back); the family TRGT VCF uploaded for the child stores the child's column only. | [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_track_loaders_keep_other_sources.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_track_loaders_keep_other_sources.py) | Each track loader keeps the other callers' tracks E2E (real stack, in-loop ASGI client, SAFE-22): one sample's package with WisecondorX bins and segments, QDNAseq bins, a coverage BED, an APCAD file and HiFiCNV's calls, depth, copy number and minor allele fraction stores every caller's track, rows and records (before: the coverage BED removed the WisecondorX and QDNAseq bins); an overwrite with a new coverage BED and APCAD file replaces those two alone (before: HiFiCNV's depth and minor allele fraction went too).
