| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_small_variant_upload_reads_before_replacing.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_small_variant_upload_reads_before_replacing.py) | A small-variant upload with overwrite reads the whole file before it replaces the callset E2E (real stack, in-loop ASGI client, SAFE-25): a trio created through `POST /api/ped/upload` gets a directly called callset and imputed genotypes, with their haplotype blocks (rows and records). Uploads with overwrite naming a sample the family lacks, with no record it can store, or cut short as gzip (400; before: 500), and imputed genotypes cut short, leave both callsets, the blocks and their records as they were (before: the callset, and the blocks, gone); valid uploads with overwrite replace the callset, and the imputed genotypes and blocks. A write that fails after the delete (ClickHouse failing after the first batch, simulated) answers 500 with the callset gone and the family flagged import-incomplete for it, with no job, as its page reads it (`GET /api/families/{family_id}`); uploading it again removes the flag. | [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_writes_mark_before_delete.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_writes_mark_before_delete.py) | Writes that fail or stop after their delete leave the family marked E2E (real stack, in-loop ASGI client, SAFE-28): a trio created through `POST /api/ped/upload` gets two members' Sniffles calls. The admin delete of a member's SVs whose rewrite fails once puts the family's SVs back. A small-variant upload whose process stops after its delete (run in-process, its insert stopped) leaves the family marked for the callset, refusing sign-out, until the callset is uploaded again. A BED upload with overwrite marks and unmarks the family under its own locks. A structure change that clears the data and then fails (its structure version, simulated) leaves the family's variants (before: deleted). |

With `mtdna.mean_depth` limits set for the family, `GET /families/{family_id}/mitochondrial-dna` gives the proband her BED's depth, the father his HiFiCNV depths, the mother her calls' depth, and each passes (before: blends of about 32x, 0.1x and 7x, and each failed). | [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_family_variant_writes_serialized.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_family_variant_writes_serialized.py) | Concurrent variant writes E2E (real stack, in-loop ASGI client, each write on a Postgres connection of its own, each storage read waiting until the other writes have read): over the golden trio, PROBAND's, MOTHER's and FATHER's Sniffles uploads of one deletion leave one row holding the three calls; the admin delete of FATHER's SVs and MOTHER's upload of the deletion and a duplication keep each other's change (every call of FATHER's gone, in both sources; MOTHER's new calls kept); PROBAND's and MOTHER's per-sample mitochondrial uploads keep both samples' calls, one row per variant. After a forced part merge (`OPTIMIZE … FINAL`) every call remains, every SV entry keeps its details and lookup row, and the family SV page shows each call once. On Postgres, a writer holding the family's two locks finds its family and samples (with no sample named too), is refused (404) when a sample or the family is gone, and a second writer waits for the first one's commit. Removes the added rows, restores the annotation manifest and re-imports the golden trio afterwards. |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_signout_during_import.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_signout_during_import.py) | Sign-out during a package import E2E (real stack, in-loop ASGI client, a real re-import job through `POST /api/family-imports` and the worker's `run_family_import_job`): with every acknowledgement given, the sign-out is refused (409 `import_in_progress`, naming the job) while the job is queued and while it runs (held in its first dataset), refused at once (409 `variant_writes_in_progress`) while another writer holds the family's variant-write locks, and refused when an import is claimed while it reads its snapshot, that import writing no dataset until the sign-out has ended; nothing is written for a refusal; once the imports have finished the report signs out (#727). |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_import_incomplete_until_reimported.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_import_incomplete_until_reimported.py) | A family flagged import-incomplete stays flagged until what failed is imported again E2E (real stack, in-loop ASGI client, real import jobs of one new one-sample family through `POST /api/family-imports` and the worker's `run_family_import_job`, the report signed out after each with the drift and QC acknowledgements only): a cancel import whose SNV callset has no readable record and whose coverage imports flags the SNV callset (409 at sign-out); an update of coverage alone completes but leaves it flagged and says why in its log (before: cleared, and signed out); an update whose Paraphase file is not an object fails and the flag names both, each with its job, also in the gate message (before: Paraphase only); an update that imports a readable SNV callset and Paraphase file clears the flag, and the report signs out. |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_import_auto_snv_callsets.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_import_auto_snv_callsets.py) | An `auto` SNV import reads the callset it stores, and the flag keeps each callset that failed, E2E (SAFE-27, SAFE-26; real stack, in-loop ASGI client, seven real import jobs of one new one-sample family through `POST /api/family-imports` and the worker's `run_family_import_job`, the SNV dataset's `source_format` left at `auto`; the backup and the small-variant write made to fail in two of them): an update of imputed genotypes is imported beside the direct calls (before: skipped) and skipped once the family holds them, naming the callset; an overwrite of imputed genotypes without a backup whose write fails removes them alone, the direct calls staying (before: deleted too), and flags them with its job; a later such failure of the direct calls keeps both callsets, each with its job (before: replaced); the direct calls imported again leave the family flagged for the imputed genotypes, its log and the sign-out refusal naming their job (before: cleared, and signed out); the imputed genotypes imported again complete the family, which signs out. |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_import_crash_leaves_family_marked.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_import_crash_leaves_family_marked.py) | An import whose process stops part-way leaves its family marked E2E (real stack, in-loop ASGI client, real import jobs of two new one-sample families through `POST /api/family-imports`, run by the worker's `run_family_import_job` and stopped by cancelling the task, so no fail-clean code runs). One is stopped inside its SNV dataset after the loader's first flush (2 of 6 variants written), one between SNV and coverage. While the job reads `running` the sign-out is refused (`import_in_progress`); once its heartbeat is stale the worker's claim ends it `failed`, interrupted, instead of running it again (before: re-run, "Package validation failed", log replaced), keeping its log and dataset summaries; the family keeps its `import_unfinished` entry naming what it had not finished, and the sign-out is refused (409 `import_incomplete`, naming the job and the datasets) unless acknowledged, which freezes the entry (before: signed out as complete). A re-import by update completes but leaves the family marked (it skipped the half-written SNVs: still 2 of 6) and says why; one by overwrite imports 6 of 6 and clears it; for the family stopped between datasets, an overwrite of coverage alone does. An overwrite of the complete family stopped inside its SNV dataset leaves its five backup tables, named after its job, until the worker's claim ends the job and drops them (before: kept for good); the family is not put back from them and stays marked until the next overwrite, which leaves no backup. |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_import_job_names_its_family_first.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_import_job_names_its_family_first.py) | An import job names its family before the import writes it E2E (real stack, in-loop ASGI client, real re-import jobs of the golden trio through `POST /api/family-imports` with no family in the request, run by the worker's `run_family_import_job`; at the import's first write of the family the job row is read and the report signed out with every acknowledgement given): the job reads `running` on FAM_TRIO and the sign-out is refused (409 `import_in_progress`, naming the job). With a Postgres trigger refusing the update that records it, the import writes nothing of the family (its registration unchanged, no dataset) and the job ends `failed`, saying why; with every update of the job refused, its end included, nothing is written and the job stays claimed, naming no family. The trigger fires only for this module's packages and is dropped before its job rows are failed on the way out (#736). |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_import_stale_heartbeat.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_import_stale_heartbeat.py) | A live import whose heartbeat goes stale E2E (#746; real stack, in-loop ASGI client, real import jobs of two new one-sample families through `POST /api/family-imports` and the worker's `run_family_import_job`; the heartbeat made unwritable by failing each beat, the job made stale and claimed as the worker loop does). An import held inside its SNV dataset after the first flush runs on while its heartbeat cannot be written; once a claim has ended its job and the heartbeat can be written again, the import, let go on, is stopped where it is: 2 of 6 variants and no coverage, its `import_unfinished` entry with nothing finished, its variant-write locks free, the job's record as the claim left it, the stop in the log, and the sign-out refused (`import_incomplete`) (before: it imported 6 of 6 and the coverage, removed its entry, and the family signed out). An overwrite of a complete family, held at its coverage dataset with its heartbeat written no more, its five backup tables dropped by the claiming worker, fails coverage and finds its backup gone: the restore deletes nothing (the overwrite's 4 variants stay; before: none), the flag names coverage as failed and snv as imported, and the end its job could not record is logged. |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_import_restore_fails_part_way.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_import_restore_fails_part_way.py) | A failed overwrite whose restore fails part-way E2E (SAFE-13, SAFE-16, SAFE-19, SAFE-20, TF-06 H16; real stack, in-loop ASGI client, real import jobs of five new one-sample families through `POST /api/family-imports` and the worker's `run_family_import_job`, and structural-variant uploads through `POST /api/structural-variants/upload/{sample_id}`; a dataset's failure and ClickHouse's failure of the restore's re-insert of one table injected). The first family imported complete (6 small variants and coverage, recorded in `imported_scopes`) is overwritten with 4 other small variants and a coverage dataset that fails; the restore deletes the family's small variants and fails before it puts the 6 back (0 left), and its backup is dropped. The flag names coverage and snv as failed, each with the overwrite's job, and none as imported (before: coverage alone), the import's entry stays naming both (before: removed), the job's log says why, and the sign-out is refused (`import_incomplete`). An overwrite of coverage alone completes but leaves snv flagged and in the entry, its log naming it, and the sign-out is still refused (before: the flag was cleared and the family signed out with no small variants); an overwrite of the small variants (6 again) clears both, and the report signs out with no import acknowledgement. The second family's overwrite carries its small variants alone, which fail; the restore puts them back and fails in the tracks, after their delete: the coverage, which that import never carried, is gone. The flag names snv, the entry snv and coverage; an overwrite of the small variants clears the flag, but the entry keeps coverage and the sign-out is refused (before: the family signed out with its coverage gone); an overwrite of the coverage completes the family, and the report signs out. The third and fourth families' overwrites carry 4 other small variants and a coverage dataset that fails, and ClickHouse fails the restore's re-insert of the small variants (third) or of the tracks (fourth): Postgres records the coverage in both, ClickHouse holds its rows in the third only. An update of coverage alone skips the third family's coverage, which ends `skipped` and stays flagged, its log saying so (before: `imported`, and coverage left the flag); it imports the fourth's again, its rows back, and only then does coverage leave the flag (before: skipped from Postgres's record, its rows still gone, and coverage left the flag all the same). The restore's entry keeps the sign-out of both refused. The fifth family's SV calls come from an upload (a manual TSV), which the family records (`sv_manual`; before: not recorded); its overwrite of the small variants fails, and the restore puts them back and fails in the SV calls, after their delete. The entry names snv and sv_manual (before: snv alone); an overwrite of the small variants clears the flag, but the entry keeps sv_manual and the sign-out is refused (before: the family signed out with its uploaded SV calls gone); the calls uploaded again with overwrite complete the family, and the report signs out. The sixth family's direct calls come from a package that leaves `source_format` at `auto`, recorded as `clair3` (SAFE-23; before: `auto`); an overwrite of coverage alone fails, and the restore fails in the small variants after their delete, so the entry names the direct calls. An overwrite of imputed genotypes (`auto` too), stored as `glimpse2`, and coverage completes: the entry keeps the direct calls and the sign-out is refused (before: both scopes read `auto`, the entry went, and the family signed out with its direct calls gone); an overwrite of the direct calls completes the family, and the report signs out. |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_import_refuses_unstorable_ids.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_import_refuses_unstorable_ids.py) | IDs no family or sample can be stored under, E2E (real Postgres, in-loop ASGI client that answers an app failure with 500, synthetic packages): Discover and Validate answer a manifest `family_id` and a request `family_id` holding a NUL with `family_id_invalid` (was 500); an import request naming such a family is refused (400) before its job is written; a real import job of such a manifest, and of a PED whose sample ID holds an escape, ends `failed` with `family_id_invalid` or `sample_id_invalid` stored in its validation errors (before, the NUL left the job unable to record why, and the escaped sample was imported); the Family Builder refuses a sample ID with a line break (was stored) and a family ID with a NUL, the PED upload a sample ID with an escape (400); no family or sample is written. |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_import_package_paths.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_import_package_paths.py) | Package paths, E2E (real Postgres, in-loop ASGI client that answers an app failure with 500, synthetic packages): a package whose `manifest.yaml` is a symlink to a manifest outside the import root is refused by Discover and Validate (`manifest_outside_package`), Write manifest answers 400 and leaves the outside file as it was, and its import job ends `failed` with that error and writes no family (before, the outside manifest was read as the package's, and the job imported the family by it); a manifest naming a file under an unknown user's `~` is refused by Validate (400, was 500) and its job ends failed with the refusal (it failed on "Could not determine home directory."), while Discover drafts with the file absent (was 500); a manifest naming a file through a symlink loop is refused by Validate (400; a 500 on Python 3.12) and its job ends failed with the refusal; Discover drafts nothing for a PED whose absent father's ID holds an escape (`sample_id_invalid`); the package list leaves out a package folder that is a symlink to a package outside the import root and one that loops, and lists one linked to a folder in the root by that folder (it listed the first by its path outside the root, under the outside manifest's family and analysis type), and Validate refuses the first typed (`package_folder_not_allowed`). No family or sample is written. |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_member_edit_refuses_unstorable_ids.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_member_edit_refuses_unstorable_ids.py) | Member and structure edits refuse IDs no sample can be stored under, E2E (real Postgres and ClickHouse, in-loop ASGI client that answers an app failure with 500, a synthetic trio from the Family Builder): a rename (`PUT /families/{family}/members/{sample}`) to an ID with a tab (was stored), a NUL (was 500) or a space (was stored), and a father with an escape, are refused (400) with the rule's message; a batch edit whose second rename holds a line break is refused and its first rename is not written (both were stored); a structure edit adding a member with an escape or a space (was stored) or a NUL (was 500) is refused; the family's members, relationships, pedigree and structure versions, and the samples, are unchanged; the same routes then store a valid rename and a valid added member without the whitespace around their IDs. |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_report_lists_complete.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_report_lists_complete.py) | The report's reported-SV list E2E (real stack, in-loop ASGI client, #730): over the golden trio, with the SV candidate cap lowered to 1, the SV that sorts last is tagged `report` through the review API. A Python-filtered search reads a capped window without it (the control); the review-tag search, unranked and prioritised, returns it with total 1 and no cap flag; both report lists accept the report's page size (the API maximum); the review is cleared afterwards. |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_haplotypes.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_haplotypes.py) | Haplotype/lineage stage E2E (real stack): imports a phased GLIMPSE2 trio (its own family, so it doesn't clobber the golden trio's clair3 SNVs), then asserts haplotype interval-track blocks are ingested, the genome-wide lineage precompute runs and produces blocks, and the `/families/{id}/haplotypes` endpoint serves phased segments. Robust (blocks/precompute/endpoint) rather than exact IBD colours (those are unit-tested). |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_request_audit_unstorable_characters.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_request_audit_unstorable_characters.py) | A request is audited whatever characters it carries E2E (real Postgres, in-loop ASGI client, synthetic values, a project of its own): a NUL in a JSON field the endpoint ignores (the action still runs), half a surrogate pair, `NaN` and `-Infinity`, and `%00` in the path and a query key each leave their `audit_log_events` row, escaped and flagged in `request_meta._escaped`; a UI event with a NUL is stored the same way, and its request audited; the worker's batch write stores a batch holding such a row whole, and a clean row carries no flag. Before, each of these rows was lost (REQ-SEC-004). |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_ui_event_timestamp_bounds.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_ui_event_timestamp_bounds.py) | A UI event's impossible `occurred_at` loses no event E2E (real Postgres and its driver, in-loop ASGI client, synthetic values): a request holding `0001-01-01T00:00:00+05:00`, before the year 1 in UTC, and a naive `9999-12-31T23:59:59.999999` stores all its events, those two at the receive time with the client's value in `detail._client_occurred_at`, and the in-bounds one as sent; the worker's batch write of two users' events, one with the year 1, writes the batch whole. Before, the year-1 event was lost, the year 9999 written as `infinity`, and the batch lost with every event in it (AUDIT-2). |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_variant_tag_edits_are_saved.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_variant_tag_edits_are_saved.py) | A variant tag edit is saved E2E (real Postgres, in-loop ASGI client, synthetic values, two projects and a family of its own): a project-scoped tag shared with a second project is edited through `PUT /api/admin/variant-tags/{tag_key}` with the body the admin page sends on Save, then through `PUT /api/families/{family_id}/small-variant-tags/{tag_key}` with only the fields that change, and each edit is read back through both list routes and from Postgres, the shared project kept where the edit leaves it out; on both routes an unknown tag is not found (404) and an edit with no field is refused (400). Before, the lookup of the tag to edit named an ambiguous `project_id`, and every edit answered 500. |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_server_error_log_without_values.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_server_error_log_without_values.py) | A request that fails leaves none of its values in the application log E2E (real Postgres, a real uvicorn server on the test's event loop, the request middleware and one route, a synthetic value): the route binds a value from the request body into a statement Postgres refuses; the request answers 500, the middleware's 500 line and uvicorn's "Exception in ASGI application" traceback name the error (`DBAPIError (InvalidTextRepresentationError, SQLSTATE 22P02)`) and keep the route's frame, without the value or the parameters, and the request's audit row keeps the error's full text, which quotes the value, beside the body. |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_variant_tag_renames_keep_reviews.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_variant_tag_renames_keep_reviews.py) | A renamed or deleted variant tag stays on the reviews that hold it E2E (golden trio on real Postgres + ClickHouse, in-loop ASGI client, synthetic tag labels): two global custom tags are put on a small variant's review, on a compound-het pair (`1-4000-T-C` + `1-5000-A-T`) and on an SV's review. The first is renamed: its key stays, the stored reviews are untouched, the review-tag filters, searching by the key the tag list serves, still find the tagged small variants and SV, a review holding it saves, and a label that reads as another active tag's or a built-in's is refused (409). The second is deleted: it leaves the default family and admin tag lists and is listed, inactive, with `include_inactive` (family and explorer routes); the small-variant, pair and SV reviews holding it still save with it (each answered 400 before), it is removed from one, and adding it to a review, again or anew, is refused (400, named). New tags with the renamed tag's old label and the deleted tag's label get numbered keys and take over no review. The test's tags and the reviews it wrote are removed at the end. |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_request_control_characters.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_request_control_characters.py) | A control character in a request's path or query string is refused at sign-in E2E (real Postgres and ClickHouse, in-loop ASGI client that answers an app failure with 500, a synthetic family made through the Family Builder, the seeded admin and a viewer outside its project): `GET /api/families/<family>%00X` is answered 400 (was 500: Postgres refused the NUL in the family lookup), the same for the admin and the viewer and whether or not the ID before the NUL is a family's; a tab, an escape and DEL in the family's path are refused too (were 404); a NUL or a tab in a `sample_id` query value and a NUL in the sample of a member delete are refused, and the member is kept; a variant search with its gene and interval lists typed one per line answers 200, and 400 with a NUL in the gene list; without a token the answer is still 401; each refusal is in `audit_log_events` under the caller's name; and every operation of the API, with a NUL in each of its path and query parameters in turn, answers 400, but for the QC-report download (403, or 422 without its link), and none 500 (REQ-SEC-001). |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_inaccessible_records_look_unknown.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_inaccessible_records_look_unknown.py) | A family, sample or project outside the caller's projects answers like an unknown one E2E (real Postgres, in-loop ASGI client, synthetic values, two projects and a viewer of its own, dropped at the end): the admin creates a family in one project through the Family Builder; a viewer of the other project gets `404 Family not found` for that family, its member, the gene profile with its ID and an HPO write to it (nothing is written), and `404 Sample not found` for its sample's coverage, each exactly as for an ID nobody uses, and the family list leaves it out; the Family Builder and the gene profile answer `404 Project not found` for the other project and for a random one; every route with a family or sample ID in its path gives the same status, body and headers for both; the audit rows read back carry `request_meta.record_hidden` (`family`, `sample`, `project`) for the hidden records and nothing for the unknown IDs. Before, those a viewer may call answered `403 Not authorized` for the existing family or sample (REQ-SEC-001). |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_project_reader_role.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_project_reader_role.py) | A reader in a project reads its records and changes none; an analyst changes them (SEC-1; real Postgres and ClickHouse, in-loop ASGI client that answers an app failure with 500, synthetic users, projects and families removed at the end). The admin makes two projects through the API (R an analyst in A; R and P readers and Q an analyst in B): the project list, the user list and `project_users` hold the levels, an update without a reader list keeps them, a reader who is no member is refused (400). Every route that changes something, walked from the app through FastAPI's `_IncludedRouter` and asked by R for B's family, sample and project with a body it accepts, is refused with 403 and the reason (`Admin access required` for an admin's route), but for the six routes of the caller's own data (presets saved, an unknown one not found, interface events taken); nothing of B's family changes (phenotypes, reviews, sign-outs, audit events, workflow fields, no new family); each refusal is in `audit_log_events` under R's name. Every route that reads a family or sample answers R as it answers Q, but for the clinical audit trail (403 for R), and the family says it is read-only for R and P only. R adds, changes and removes a phenotype of A's family: three `phenotype` events on its trail, with their summaries, before and after, by R, and the chain verifies. P is told which projects they only read (`/auth/me`, the project list), is refused a phenotype, and records their interface events. |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_microsoft_sign_in.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_microsoft_sign_in.py) | Microsoft sign-in E2E (SEC-4; real Postgres, in-loop ASGI client, a stand-in directory made at run time, synthetic values deleted at the end): the emergency administrator signs in with the override on and sets up a project and family; a colleague's first Microsoft sign-in, sent twice at once, makes one viewer's account bound to the object ID, without a password and in no project, which sees no family until the project API adds it to the project; the emergency administrator gives it the admin role, after which it cannot change its own role or the emergency administrator's, nor deactivate the emergency administrator; a first sign-in with a local account's address is refused and links nothing; a later sign-in follows a new address; a deactivated account is told so; with the override off the emergency token and the password sign-in are refused, and there is no sign-up; the audit rows read back record the account made, the role and activation changes from and to, and flag every request of the emergency sign-in, its sign-in included. |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_alignment_lookup_family_folders.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_alignment_lookup_family_folders.py) | The alignment endpoints serve a sample's reads only from its own family's folders E2E (SEC-20; real Postgres, in-loop ASGI client, the seeded admin, synthetic IDs, local storage over a data directory of the test's own). Families made with the Family Builder: F2 with S2, F1 with S1, a sample `../F2/S2` and S3, F2's reads in every layout and F1's package `bams` folder a link to F2's, which holds a file named after S3. F1's alignment manifest lists S1 alone and its URLs fetch S1's reads and index (before: also `../F2/S2` with F2's reads, and S3 with the file the link leads to); S3's reads and index answer 404 to GET and HEAD (before: 200 with F2's file); F2's manifest still lists S2 and its URLs fetch S2's reads. |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_recorded_path_lookup_family_folders.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_recorded_path_lookup_family_folders.py) | Signal tracks and QC reports are served only from their own family's folders E2E (SEC-25; real Postgres, in-loop ASGI client, the seeded admin, synthetic IDs, local storage over a data directory of the test's own, its packages folder the import root). Families made with the Family Builder, the paths written into the samples' `metadata` rows as an edited row would hold them: F2 with S2, its track and report in its package; F1 with S1 (its own), S3 (`../<F2>/...`), S4 (F2's track by its absolute path) and S5 (through a folder of F1's package linked into F2's); F3, whose package folder is a link to F2's, with S6 naming F2's report. F1's signal-track manifest lists S1 alone and its URL fetches S1's track (before: also S3, S4 and S5); S3, S4 and S5's tracks answer 404 to GET and HEAD (before: 200 with F2's track); S1's QC-report link opens its report, S3's and S6's answer 404 (before: 400, and 200 with F2's report); F2's manifest and report still serve F2's own. |
| [backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_raw_file_paths_contained.py](../backend/tests/e2e/test_e2e_raw_file_paths_contained.py) | Raw-file download, Verify, the file list and the deletes keep to CoGA's own folders E2E (SEC-29; real Postgres and ClickHouse, in-loop ASGI client, the seeded admin, synthetic IDs and files in folders of the test's own: the import root, the data directory with its upload folder, and a folder that is neither). A family of two samples made with the Family Builder, its `raw_import_files` rows written as the import writes them and as an edited row would hold them: a package file (in a package folder not named after the family) and an upload are listed, downloaded and verified; a row naming a file elsewhere, a path that climbs out of the import root with `..` and a link in the package that leads out are listed unavailable (before: available) and answer 403 to the download and Verify (before: 200 with the file's bytes, and "verified"). Deleting the second sample unlinks its upload and leaves the file elsewhere that another managed row of it names (before: deleted, 2 counted); deleting the family unlinks its upload and one in the folder of a former ID, and leaves the package file and the file elsewhere that its managed rows name (before: deleted, 4 counted). |

### API / config / infra / other
| Test file | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| [backend/tests/test_api_route_prefix.py](../backend/tests/test_api_route_prefix.py) | API route-prefix consistency via the OpenAPI schema. |
| [backend/tests/test_api_id_schemas.py](../backend/tests/test_api_id_schemas.py) | API schemas accept UUID identifiers (user, project, family), a gene panel's structured regions and a ClickHouse string variant id. |
| [backend/tests/test_async_blocking_io.py](../backend/tests/test_async_blocking_io.py) | The alignment manifest (deduplicated, in order, the family's samples only), the CRAM header read offloaded from the event loop, and a bucket prefix downloaded object by object (directory markers skipped; an empty prefix gives zero). |
| [backend/tests/test_health_endpoint.py](../backend/tests/test_health_endpoint.py) | Health endpoint liveness without datastores. `/api/version` reports the build identity through the named `VersionOut` model, the type the app and report footers read. |
| [backend/tests/test_postgres_migrations.py](../backend/tests/test_postgres_migrations.py) | The Postgres schema migration without a database (ENG-3): the Alembic revisions form one line numbered from `0001`, so two pull requests that take the same number fail; every revision is forward-only; the checks revisions `0002` and `0003` set are their baselines' to the character; the ledger table is Alembic's own version table, and its REVOKE in `05_grants.sql` follows the broad GRANT; Alembic's commands that need a database stop outside the migration step; the step refuses `coga_app` and a ledger from a newer version before anything runs, takes its lock first, and until the release candidate re-applies the baselines before the revisions. |
| [backend/tests/test_postgres_schema_loader.py](../backend/tests/test_postgres_schema_loader.py) | SQL splitting and dollar-quoted PL/pgSQL handling in the schema-baseline loader: the append-only trigger function in `04_traceability.sql` stays one statement; the project-scoped tag definition in `03_assay.sql`, replayed on every boot, holds no backfill `UPDATE` that would reset project tags (#255). |
| [backend/tests/test_postgres_engine.py](../backend/tests/test_postgres_engine.py) | Postgres engine selection: Cloud SQL Python Connector (verify-full TLS) when enabled vs DSN when disabled. |
| [backend/tests/test_github_releases_service.py](../backend/tests/test_github_releases_service.py) | GitHub release summary/changelog formatting. |
| [backend/tests/test_ui_events.py](../backend/tests/test_ui_events.py) | UI-event path masking, label sanitization, batch insert; a value Postgres cannot store is escaped and its column named in `detail._escaped`, which a client cannot set. An event whose write fails as the request runs (sync mode, or no worker running) is counted and logged with its payload, while the rest of the batch is stored once and the batch answered 202, not 500. The client's `occurred_at` is kept from a day before to five minutes after the receive time, at either bound, with any offset, and without one (read as UTC); a microsecond outside, a clock ten hours ahead, the years 1 and 9999 that the driver could not convert to UTC or wrote as ±infinity, and the Unix epoch are stored as the receive time, with the client's value in `detail._client_occurred_at`, which a client cannot set. With the driver's conversion stubbed, such an event is stored with the rest of its request in sync and async mode, and the worker's batch holding it and another user's events is written whole, where every event of it was lost (AUDIT-2). |
| [backend/tests/test_apcad_band_targets.py](../backend/tests/test_apcad_band_targets.py) | APCAD downsample budget allocation across BAF bands. |
| [backend/tests/test_apcad_origin_fallback.py](../backend/tests/test_apcad_origin_fallback.py) | APCAD origin preference: unphased (HiFiCNV MAF) tracks fall back to `und`, phased tracks keep the filter and stay empty where no informative markers fall; the phased probe is a `LIMIT 1` over the primary key, and none runs when every origin is asked for. Markers without a quality score (a MAF bigWig) are spread across the chromosome instead of taken from its start (#421). |
| [backend/tests/test_family_package_bigwig.py](../backend/tests/test_family_package_bigwig.py) | bigWig reading (primary contigs only, karyotype order, zero-bin skipping) and which HiFiCNV file feeds which interval track. |
| [backend/tests/test_family_package_cnv_callers.py](../backend/tests/test_family_package_cnv_callers.py) | CNV-caller discovery (QDNAseq lrsvar BED and legacy CSV layouts, WisecondorX sample-prefixed names) and interval-track ownership on re-import. With update, a track whose rows are stored is left, recorded in `sample_interval_track_sources` or not, and one Postgres records whose rows are gone is imported again, its stale record cleared first (SAFE-20; before: skipped as there). The importer reads its file up to the first row before the track is cleared, and a file without one is refused with nothing cleared or imported (SAFE-29). |
| [backend/tests/test_coverage_normalization.py](../backend/tests/test_coverage_normalization.py) | Autosomal-median normaliser and the depth→log2-ratio transform that puts three CNV callers' coverage on one axis. A copy number becomes a log2 ratio against diploid, a homozygous deletion takes the depth track's floor, and copy number and depth agree on what a loss looks like (#420). |
| [backend/tests/test_track_transform_recorded.py](../backend/tests/test_track_transform_recorded.py) | A track whose values the package import transforms records the transform in its `sample_interval_track_sources` row (datastores faked at the session and the ClickHouse insert). Through the HiFiCNV import: the copy-number track, stored as log2 of the copy number over 2, records `log2_ratio_to_diploid`, as the depth track records `log2_ratio_to_autosomal_median` with its median (unchanged); the MAF track and QDNAseq's tracks, stored as their files hold them, record no transform (TRACE-4). A depth file without a usable autosomal median (no autosomal depth, or a median at or below zero) stores its depths as read and records `none` with that median, not `log2_ratio_to_autosomal_median`; the track availability serves each offered coverage track's recorded transforms (`coverage_normalizations`), none for a caller whose records name none or a track not offered, and reads them only when a member has coverage (TRACE-7), with what each coverage BED declared (none here). |
| [backend/tests/test_signal_track_router.py](../backend/tests/test_signal_track_router.py) | Signal-file serving for IGV: recorded-path resolution, containment against crafted family ids and paths, and the per-kind axis spec. In gcs/s3 mode: the importer records a bucket package's object URIs; the manifest signs the recorded object, the GET redirects to it and the HEAD reports its size; a recorded location outside the configured bucket and FAMILY_IMPORT_ROOTS (another bucket or store, dot segments, a query, the wrong extension) is never signed; a row without a URI falls back to `<prefix>/<family>/<recorded path>`, which cannot leave the family's folder, and is not probed for a family ID that is no single folder name (`other-family/cnv`, which reached that family's object, SEC-25; also a trailing or leading `/` and `..`); local mode still serves the data directory. |
| [backend/tests/test_admin_clickhouse_listing.py](../backend/tests/test_admin_clickhouse_listing.py) | Admin ClickHouse assembly listing: an assembly name with a space is listed under its dataset key (not skipped, not a 500), and a Postgres name and its ClickHouse key appear once. |
| [backend/tests/test_config.py](../backend/tests/test_config.py) | Settings parsing: list settings (`CORS_ORIGINS`, `FAMILY_IMPORT_ROOTS`, `VALIDATED_ASSEMBLIES`) read from the real process environment in comma-separated and JSON form, the GRCh38-only validated-assembly default, and build identity: a stamped `APP_VERSION` wins, an unstamped build reports the repository's `VERSION`, and a build without one records the `0.0.0+unknown` sentinel. |
| [backend/tests/test_small_variant_clinvar_frequency_rescue.py](../backend/tests/test_small_variant_clinvar_frequency_rescue.py) | ClinVar P/LP rescue overriding frequency thresholds, in the Python matcher AND the ClickHouse filter (same terms); ClinVar's `Pathogenic/Likely_pathogenic` aggregate split into its terms (#534). |
| [backend/tests/test_in_silico_prediction_filter.py](../backend/tests/test_in_silico_prediction_filter.py) | SIFT and PolyPhen filters read the prediction, not its score, alike in the ClickHouse condition and the Python matcher (CLIN-45). For every SIFT option (deleterious, deleterious low confidence, tolerated, tolerated low confidence) and PolyPhen option (probably damaging, possibly damaging, benign, unknown), over VEP's values with a score, without one, the low-confidence ones, other capitals and spaces, a value listing two predictions and a score alone, the SQL condition (evaluated on the value as the import stores it, with a stand-in for each ClickHouse function it has used) and `_annotation_matches_normal` both keep exactly the transcripts whose value names the option: `deleterious` keeps `deleterious(0.01)` and `deleterious`, not `deleterious_low_confidence(0.03)`. A transcript without the prediction is kept by neither; a filter value is read as a stored value is (capitals, spaces, a score); one that names no prediction (blank, a score alone) asks nothing in either path; SIFT or PolyPhen, one is enough; the Variant Explorer's transcript condition and the monogenic NIPT filters apply the same rule. The rule itself (`in_silico_prediction`, `in_silico_prediction_pattern`, `in_silico_prediction_matches` in `family_variant_filters.py`): the prediction a value names, a pattern of what RE2 and Python read alike, a filter value matched as text, never as a pattern. |
| [backend/tests/test_small_variant_filter_transcript.py](../backend/tests/test_small_variant_filter_transcript.py) | The transcript the annotation filters held on, named beside the primary one (CLIN-51). `_small_variant_out`: a variant kept by SIFT deleterious or PolyPhen probably damaging on its second transcript keeps its primary transcript's fields and names the second (`filter_transcript`) with its values. It names none without a search, without annotation filters (variant-level filters; a SIFT or PolyPhen value naming no prediction), when its primary transcript meets them, or without annotations. Of the transcripts the filters hold on it names the one picked as the primary is: MANE Select, then canonical, then the most severe, then the first stored. Any annotation filter held on another transcript names it (impact, consequence, transcript, HGVS, LoF only, REVEL, SpliceAI, CADD, ClinVar, frequency, canonical only, MANE only), with its values read as the variant's own fields read them (aliases, the SpliceAI maximum, another gene). Every view of the family page names it, whichever path builds the page (ClickHouse's page, compound-het and recessive pairs from the candidate read, homozygous recessive, the track and its sampled candidates, ranked computed and served from the cache, ranked compound het), as do a panel served from a broader ranking and the export, unranked and ranked; the ranking's scores are the same with and without the filter. |
| [backend/tests/test_small_variant_filter_parity.py](../backend/tests/test_small_variant_filter_parity.py) | The family search's Python record filter reads each small-variant filter as its SQL does (ENG-1), without a database (the integration test runs them on ClickHouse): a gene panel of more than 1,000 regions (the Mendeliome's size) keeps a variant in one of its regions as a smaller one does (owner decision 2026-10-14: it was matched on its genes alone); an excluded gene leaves out every variant annotated with it (symbol or Ensembl id, any capitals), besides those in its loci (`_kept_small_candidates`); *Also hit by an SV* keeps a variant whose genes hold one the family's SVs hit, and nothing when none is resolved; a chromosome filter names chrM by either name (M, MT, chrM, chrMT), and intervals, excluded intervals, excluded genes' loci and panel regions compare chromosomes in any capitals, with or without `chr`, chrM by either name; a location keeps a variant that starts in it (a deletion running past the end included), and with `overlap` one that overlaps it, from a start alone too (the SQL reads its end then, its xpos range from 0); the variant type keeps SNV, INDEL (an MNV included) and MNV as the SQL does, and a value that names none asks nothing in either path; the SQL reads a callset and an rsID without the spaces around them, and a blank one asks nothing (the imputed callsets stay hidden); the rsID filter searches the variant's stored rsID (the VCF's ID, else the existing variants VEP lists on the first transcript), not every transcript's list; a consequence value joining two terms (`missense_variant&splice_region_variant`) is read as either term, as the SQL splits it; a score of 0 meets a minimum of 0 (a SpliceAI 0.00 included), and a missing score reads as -1, as `ifNull(score, -1)` does; the frequencies, scores and a call's allele fraction, stored as Float32, are compared with their thresholds at that precision in both paths (`stored_precision`; the SQL's `toFloat32`), so a value at the threshold (CADD 25.3, REVEL 0.7, an allele fraction of 0.45, a gnomAD frequency of 0.001) meets it; the counts stay integers. |
| [backend/tests/test_variant_export_truncation.py](../backend/tests/test_variant_export_truncation.py) | CSV exports lift the page clamp to the export cap, report truncation beyond it (incl. a truncated ranking), flatten compound-het groups, and name a truncated file as such (#512); an export over a capped candidate read is truncated with reason `candidate-limit`, named `…-TRUNCATED-partial-search.csv` by the route, and the reason header is CORS-exposed (#725). The Variant Explorer export reads one row past its 50,000-row cap and reports a larger result truncated (`row-limit`, `…-TRUNCATED-first-N.csv`), a result through a capped tag or classification filter as `review-filter-limit` (`…-TRUNCATED-partial-search.csv`; it outranks the row cap), and names a complete file plainly with `X-CoGA-Export-Truncated: false`. |

| [backend/tests/test_variant_annotation_parser.py](../backend/tests/test_variant_annotation_parser.py) | VCF annotation parsing incl. SpliceAI / scientific notation. |
| [backend/tests/test_repeat_contraction_loci.py](../backend/tests/test_repeat_contraction_loci.py) | Repeat loci pathogenic by contraction (VWA1, MIR7-2): a benign count is never flagged, expansion loci keep their thresholds, and pathogenic_max is not treated as a ceiling. The status ranking puts a premutation between the grey zone and pathogenic, and `not_assessed` between review and the grey zone; a count at an uncatalogued locus reads `not_assessed` (CLIN-26), and at one with a benign range only, `normal` inside the range and `not_assessed` on either side of it (CLIN-36). |
| [backend/tests/test_repeat_fmr1_premutation.py](../backend/tests/test_repeat_fmr1_premutation.py) | An FMR1 premutation has its own status: 44 normal, 45–54 grey zone (`intermediate`), 55–200 `premutation`, 201 pathogenic; a locus without a premutation range keeps its intermediate range; the ranking puts a premutation between the grey zone and pathogenic; every catalogue entry of FMR1 (built-in and STRchive) carries the threshold and no other locus does; a female's and a male's premutation are stored as one; the family table reclassifies a call stored before the threshold existed. The full mutation starts at 201 under either catalogue row, the built-in `FMR1` and STRchive's `FXS_FMR1`, which the call's TRID picks (each boundary, 44/45, 54/55 and 200/201, run through the catalogue a start seeds and the import's lookup; the built-in row called 200 a full mutation); a male's single chrX allele of 200 is a premutation, written once or twice; the built-in row and STRchive agree; each start rewrites the thresholds an earlier start stored, and the family table reads a male's 200-repeat call stored as a full mutation as a premutation. |
| [backend/tests/test_repeat_multi_locus_genes.py](../backend/tests/test_repeat_multi_locus_genes.py) | A gene with several STRchive loci (CLIN-31): its name as TRID picks the locus whose STRchive coordinates on the family's assembly overlap the call's region. The catalogue a start seeds lists ARX's two and HOXA13's three tracts under the gene's name, which names no row by itself (the first tract by locus ID before); a STRchive or STRipy ID still names its locus. Each tract's own thresholds at the counts where the tracts disagree (`PRTS_ARX` 17 normal, pathogenic under `EIEE1_ARX`'s 17 before; `HFG_HOXA13-II` 18 pathogenic, normal under `-I`'s 22 before), on GRCh38 and T2T-CHM13 and through the per-sample ingest; a tag several loci share resolves the same way. A region that overlaps no tract or several, or an assembly without STRchive coordinates: `not_assessed`, with the reason stored with the call; a no-call stays `unknown`. The family table shows the reason as the call's note, and its query lets a name that reaches several STRchive loci choose none of them. |
| [backend/tests/test_repeat_not_assessed.py](../backend/tests/test_repeat_not_assessed.py) | A repeat call at a locus without thresholds reads `not_assessed`, never normal (CLIN-26): an allele of any size where no intermediate, premutation or pathogenic threshold exists and no benign range is stated, and a no-call there `unknown`; a row with one threshold keeps it; the known loci read as before (normal, grey zone, premutation, pathogenic, review, unknown); `not_assessed` ranks above normal and review and below the grey zone; the ranking, the API's status values and the stored status's check constraint list the same statuses. A count inside the benign range of a row without thresholds reads `normal` (CLIN-36): POLG's 10, both ends of a wider range; a count above or below it, and every count where the range lacks an end, stays `not_assessed`; a benign range changes nothing at a locus with a threshold (ABCD3, a grey zone alone). The seeded catalogue's rows without thresholds are exactly ADTKD_MUC1 (no range: every count not assessed) and CPEO_POLG (10 the one normal count). Through the catalogue a start seeds and the import's TRID lookup, a call under an unknown TRID, a TRID spelled differently (`HD-HTT`) or a row without thresholds (ADTKD_MUC1, CPEO_POLG outside 10) is stored `not_assessed` with no thresholds, POLG 10/10 under `CPEO_POLG` or `POLG` is stored `normal` (10/30 and 9/10 not assessed), the count read is TRGT's count of the catalogue motif (a POLG allele of 14 GCT copies, the common allele in the TRGT catalogue in use, not assessed), a male's two different chrX alleles there stay not assessed, and the per-sample and family ingests store it so; the family table reads such a call stored as `normal` before as not assessed at once (its locus too), a POLG call stored as `not_assessed` inside its benign range as normal, keeps a no-call `unknown` and the male chrX note; the genome track serves a stored `not_assessed`. |
| [backend/tests/test_repeat_thresholds_follow_strchive.py](../backend/tests/test_repeat_thresholds_follow_strchive.py) | The built-in repeat loci carry STRchive's thresholds (CLIN-22). A guard fails when a built-in row's intermediate or pathogenic threshold differs from its STRchive row's, or a built-in locus has not exactly one STRchive row. At the fifteen loci whose built-in thresholds changed (ATXN1, ATXN2, CACNA1A, ATXN7, TBP, ATXN8OS, CNBP, FXN, C9orf72, ATXN10, BEAN1, PPP2R2B, NOP56, JPH3, PABPN1), each side of every new and old threshold reads the same under the gene-name TRID (the built-in row) and the STRchive ID, run through the catalogue a start seeds and the import's lookup, and the call is stored with STRchive's thresholds; each start writes them over those an earlier start stored (BEAN1's intermediate as NULL); the family table reads a call stored under an old row with the catalogue row's thresholds, and takes both from the row it matched, a missing one included. |
| [backend/tests/test_strchive_release_pin.py](../backend/tests/test_strchive_release_pin.py) | The repeat catalogue is built from the shipped STRchive release, or the backend does not start (CLIN-31). The pin (`STRCHIVE_PINNED_LOCI_SHA256`) is the shipped file's SHA-256, no setting overrides it, and data-import.md and TF-08 name it. An identical copy in the bucket is read, as is the repository's copy when the configured file does not exist; another release, the same loci written out again, an edited repository copy and no file at all refuse the start before anything is written, with one logged error that names the file, both digests and the fix. The application's start stops at the catalogue (no ClickHouse, no workers) and starts on an identical copy. A start removes the STRchive rows the shipped release does not list, after its upserts and in their transaction. |
| [backend/tests/test_sv_filter_before_cap.py](../backend/tests/test_sv_filter_before_cap.py) | SV region push-down: gene/panel regions reach the SQL so the candidate cap bounds the ranking rather than deciding what the filter sees; span overlap, coordinate normalisation, dedupe. A review selection reaches the SQL the same way (#730): its variant ids as `e.variantId IN`, the excluded ones as `NOT IN`, an empty selection matches nothing, no selection adds no clause, and the ids are bound as parameters, never spliced. |
| [backend/tests/test_variant_cytoband_labels.py](../backend/tests/test_variant_cytoband_labels.py) | Cytoband lookup shared by small and structural variants: band per position, multi-band spans, chromosome aliases. |
| [backend/tests/test_variant_annotation_extraction.py](../backend/tests/test_variant_annotation_extraction.py) | Small-variant annotation extraction from the VEP `CSQ` field, the INFO fallback and VEP TSV entries. |

Its error log takes the request as `%s` arguments, never as the format string (#702). |

Each coverage track goes on the axis of what it holds, as the track availability serves it (TRACE-9): read depth, a log2 ratio, or neither for anything else (a BED that declares nothing, a transform not known, nothing served); no note where the legend says it all (a log2 ratio, a BED declared as read depth), a quiet note for HiFiCNV's depths stored as read (TRACE-7), and a warning for values of unknown kind, worded for a coverage BED or another caller. |

The one CNV point format: signed and to two decimals, so +0.90 (Likely pathogenic) reads apart from +0.99 (Pathogenic), a whole number as +1.00 and zero as 0.00, never -0.00 (#735). The kind and the section-3 gene count read an SV type without its subtypes (CLIN-47): DUP:TANDEM and INS:ME are gains, DEL:ME a loss, a CNV a loss as before, and a tandem duplication in a constrained gene gets the gain's section-3 tier and no 2H. The suggestions are made for the kind asked for (CLIN-55): a 30-gene deletion scored as a gain gets 3A and no 2H, a 40-gene duplication scored as a loss 3C and 2H, and every suggestion is a criterion of that kind within its range, as the server keeps it. |

An unaffected or carrier call needs the member's own haplotype across the ROI on each side it needs: no block, part of the ROI, a gap, a grey lane or an unconfirmed homolog → uninformative, under every model. X-linked recessive with the sex not recorded: called as a son and a daughter, at risk or uninformative when the calls differ. Pseudo-autosomal: in PAR1 a son is read on both copies (his father's risk haplotype at risk, the other one unaffected); across the PAR1 boundary, or on an assembly with unknown PARs, never on his maternal X alone. Parity with the backend's embryo calls: every case of `fixtures/pgtEmbryoCallCases.json` gives the same disease haplotype and the same call, warnings included, as `backend/tests/test_pgt_embryo_calls.py` gives. |

An en-dash range reads as a region; a colon or a BED-like line that does not parse, or an end before its start, reads as neither a gene nor a region; `intervalListProblems` names each unreadable interval entry as the backend refuses it (#615). |
| [frontend/src/lib/acmg/__tests__/evaluate.test.ts](../frontend/src/lib/acmg/__tests__/evaluate.test.ts) | ACMG criterion auto-suggestion for small variants. With the gene profile or the HPO terms failed (#616): PVS1 and BS2 say the gene profile could not be loaded, not an unconfirmed mechanism; PP4 is offered as Consider, "Not assessed", unless a phenotype score applies it. A son where he is hemizygous (#622): PM6/PS2 follow the parent who passes him the chromosome (a Y de novo with no maternal call applies PM6; an X variant carried by the mother is inherited from her; one shared with the father is not called de novo), and the trio rule holds in a PAR and for a daughter. A conflicting ClinVar record suggests neither PP5 nor BP6. PM6/PS2 compare the proband with the parents the pedigree links, not a grandparent with the same role: unlinked parents are not assessed; a reference parent read by fewer than 8 reads, or a homozygous proband outside a son's hemizygous X/Y (as the backend's de novo mode wants a heterozygous one), offers PM6 for review with the reason instead of applying it. PP3/BP4 read the AlphaMissense class the variant is served with (the value its evidence snapshot freezes) when REVEL and SpliceAI do not decide; before, the evaluator read a key no variant holds and found no class. |
| [frontend/src/lib/acmg/__tests__/evaluateMito.test.ts](../frontend/src/lib/acmg/__tests__/evaluateMito.test.ts) | Mitochondrial ACMG criterion evaluation. PP4 is offered for review when a lookup it needs failed (#616). A conflicting or uncertain status suggests neither PP5 nor BP6. PP1 is offered only for `maternal_shared`, not for `maternal_not_observed`, `maternal_only` or `family_private`. |
| [frontend/src/lib/acmg/__tests__/suggestedStrengths.test.ts](../frontend/src/lib/acmg/__tests__/suggestedStrengths.test.ts) | The strength the ACMG dialog starts a criterion at is one the criterion allows, which the server requires (CLIN-3): every criterion's default, and every strength the nuclear and the mtDNA pre-evaluation suggest over a sweep of consequences, frequencies, in-silico scores, ClinVar, gene, phenotype and family contexts. |
| [frontend/src/lib/acmg/__tests__/pedigree.test.ts](../frontend/src/lib/acmg/__tests__/pedigree.test.ts) | The proband's parents for PM6/PS2 read from the pedigree's parent-child links as the backend's de novo mode reads them: the parent on either side of a link; couples, a generic `parent` role and incomplete links skipped; a child's own father and mother, not a grandparent; a role held by two members names no one. |
| [frontend/src/lib/acmg/__tests__/score.test.ts](../frontend/src/lib/acmg/__tests__/score.test.ts) | ACMG classification computation from criterion selections. |

### Shared UI components
| Test file | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| [frontend/src/components/__tests__/AssemblyScopeBanner.test.tsx](../frontend/src/components/__tests__/AssemblyScopeBanner.test.tsx) | "Not validated for clinical use" label for an off-scope or unlinked assembly; nothing for a validated assembly or while the scope is still loading (#515). When the reference could not be loaded, it says the validated scope is unconfirmed, with a retry (#611). |
| [frontend/src/components/__tests__/ImportIncompleteBanner.test.tsx](../frontend/src/components/__tests__/ImportIncompleteBanner.test.tsx) | "Import incomplete" warning for a family whose package import partly failed: names the datasets that failed and those that imported, when, and the import job that holds each dataset's error (none for a flag without one); still warns when the flag records nothing to name; nothing for a complete import. It also warns while an import of the family has not finished (`import_unfinished`: running, or stopped part-way), naming the job, what it had not finished and what it had, with the advice to re-import with overwrite; with a failed import too, it names both. When a later failure kept an earlier one, it names each failed dataset's import job, and it says to import what failed again. After a failed overwrite whose restore did not finish, it names every dataset of the family still to import, whichever import or upload wrote it, and says to import the whole family again with overwrite, not only what failed, and to upload again what an upload brought (SAFE-16, SAFE-19); `wholeFamilyImportKeys` reads which entries are such, and the entry reads as any other, every dataset pending. The metadata readers take a set value of another shape as incomplete, as sign-out does, read each failed dataset's job (the flag's own when it names none) and the unfinished imports oldest first. A single-file upload's mark (SAFE-28) is named as such, with what it may have left partly written or missing and the advice to upload or import it again with overwrite, apart from a failed import; a deletion's as one that could not put the structural variants back; `unfinishedWriter` reads the writer from the key, and `wholeFamilyImportKeys` leaves both marks out. |
| [frontend/src/components/__tests__/QueryFailure.test.tsx](../frontend/src/components/__tests__/QueryFailure.test.tsx) | The shared failure notice: what could not be loaded, "this is not an empty result", the server's reason, what it means for the page, and a retry (#613). |
| [frontend/src/components/__tests__/FamilyLoadFailure.test.tsx](../frontend/src/components/__tests__/FamilyLoadFailure.test.tsx) | The family pages' failure state: "Family not found" only for a 404; any other failure "… could not be loaded", with the server's reason and a retry (#617). |
| [frontend/src/components/__tests__/Breadcrumbs.test.tsx](../frontend/src/components/__tests__/Breadcrumbs.test.tsx) | Breadcrumb navigation rendering with router context: variant filters kept from chromosome to genome view, the admin crumbs' targets, a clinical CNV's name instead of its id. Route words in sentence case and identifiers as stored; the families crumb leads to the dashboard, the admin families crumb stays on the admin page, and a reference doc's crumb leads to the user guide (#754). |
| [frontend/src/components/__tests__/Layout.test.tsx](../frontend/src/components/__tests__/Layout.test.tsx) | The main sections sit behind the arrow menu until it is opened (Admin added for an admin; no menu before sign-in); it closes on Escape, back to the arrow, on a click elsewhere and when a section is chosen (#754). Logout flushes telemetry, then clears the query cache and the session before navigating to login (#521). The footer carries the device label (TF-15 §1): the running build from `/api/version` ("Version unavailable" when it cannot be loaded), the in-house IVD status and the manufacturer; a problem report goes to the configured CMGGMC route, a production build without it shows no GitHub issue form. The menu holds Report codes, the lookup of a signed record by the code the EPD report quotes. After a Microsoft sign-in, logout also forgets the Microsoft account in the tab; after a password sign-in it has none to forget (SEC-4). |
| [frontend/src/components/__tests__/ModalDialog.test.tsx](../frontend/src/components/__tests__/ModalDialog.test.tsx) | Inline dialog shell (#529): a named modal dialog that closes on Escape, can refuse backdrop clicks (acknowledgement dialogs), and asks before Escape discards typed input. A dialog answers Escape from the moment it is drawn, not the dialog underneath: a default-priority open followed at once by Escape, which closed the outer dialog before (#601). |
| [frontend/src/components/__tests__/InfoTip.test.tsx](../frontend/src/components/__tests__/InfoTip.test.tsx) | Info tooltip shown on hover and on keyboard focus, hidden on leave. A static child (`staticChild`, such as the sequencing-QC chip without a report) takes keyboard focus without a button role or an `aria-label`, and the tip is its accessible description while it shows. |
| [frontend/src/components/__tests__/LoadingBar.test.tsx](../frontend/src/components/__tests__/LoadingBar.test.tsx) | Animated loading-status bar accessibility. |
| [frontend/src/components/__tests__/RequireAuth.test.tsx](../frontend/src/components/__tests__/RequireAuth.test.tsx) | Auth route-guard: unauthenticated → login (`?next=`). |

| [frontend/src/components/__tests__/SessionRedirect.test.tsx](../frontend/src/components/__tests__/SessionRedirect.test.tsx) | Authenticated-user redirect to dashboard target. |

The member dialog's own flows, recorded before it moved into `FamilyMemberDetailDialog`: adding a phenotype from the HPO search and a failed save, removing a phenotype, removing a member only once confirmed and never the family's last, and a pending edit shown on reopening (#528, #599). Failed requests (#614): the embryo calls at the ROI give "⚠ segregation not derived" per embryo; a failed presence check shows its workspace link and settles; curation counts and HPO terms read as unknown; a failed status list keeps the current status shown and unchangeable. A failed family reads "Family could not be loaded", not "Family not found", and retries; a failed member dialog says so instead of loading for good (#617). Embryo badges, from the calls the backend serves (`GET /families/{id}/pgt-analysis`), asked for only for a family with embryos and an ROI: an embryo uninformative for missing data reads Uninformative with the missing-data warning, one without a resolved disease haplotype says so, a recombination near the ROI is warned; an X-linked recessive embryo of unrecorded sex at risk reads Affected / at risk with "⚠ sex unknown" and both calls; a parent's phase corrected next to the ROI puts "⚠ phase corrected" on every embryo and leaves each embryo's call as it was. In the structure editor, a relative of unknown degree shows the member it is linked to, and relinked to both parents it is saved as a link to each. The **Variant summary** link is offered when the family has structural variants, or when that check failed, and never for small variants alone. The **Monogenic NIPT** link is offered to a family whose analysis type is `monogenic_nipt`, without the "no variant data" note, and to no other. Access levels (SEC-1): an analyst who is no administrator adds and removes phenotypes, but not the member's own fields; a reader sees the phenotypes and the workflow fields, with no control to change them, and the page says *Read-only access (reader)*. |

A failed family reads "Family could not be loaded", not "Family not found" (#617). A list cut at the classification limit says so. A category tick survives **Apply filters**; picking an inheritance view clears the ticks and offers the view's own control (the alleles the fetus did not inherit, the lowest de novo priority). The NIPT analysis is signed out through the family sign-out (the same request), and the signed version opens; a failed NIPT check opens the Sample QC acknowledgement, and the sign-out carries the reason; the page names the latest signed version, its report code and what changed since, and offers no sign-out off the validated assembly scope, nor to a reader in the family's project (disabled with the reason the report page gives, and none sent; SEC-1). |

The incomplete-import warning sits in the report header and prints with it: the live report prints without a sign-out to stop on a partly imported family. | [frontend/src/pages/families/__tests__/FamilyReportPage.test.tsx](../frontend/src/pages/families/__tests__/FamilyReportPage.test.tsx) | Clinical report. A signed case opens on its latest signed version, rendered from the frozen record alone (nothing live is asked for or shown): signer, date, content hash, the build that signed it and the one that rendered it, each reported variant's classification, criteria, evidence and note, the checks at sign-out and the frozen overrides, the evidence drift at sign-out counting and listing the SV/CNV classifications with the small variants (and saying when the record holds no SV/CNV drift check); what the record does not hold is said for the report and for each variant; a section the record does not hold, what a record could not capture, a record that fails its content hash (on screen and in print), a superseded version, and a version that cannot be loaded, does not exist or is not a number; Print prints the record; whether the family's data changed since is said off the printout; the version it shows downloads. The live report is labelled as not the signed version even when it matches, on screen and in print (changed, unverified and still-loading states), opens and is opened from the signed version, downloads it, and after sign-out shows the version just signed. Live provenance footer with the running build and the device label, drift badge (the SV/CNV classifications too, with what moved in their evidence; a change in their drift since sign-out is named; a small variant's frozen frequencies and in-silico scores that moved, with their values, CLIN-7, and a signed version showing the values its classifications froze and those that had moved at sign-out; likewise a moved consequence, LoF call or gene missense Z, and a signed version showing the consequence and missense Z frozen, saying nothing of them for a record frozen before them), sign-out/amend, audit timeline; drift, QC and incomplete-import overrides need a reason and carry the earlier ones on; the QC dialog shows each concern as the 409 gives it, naming its sample or pair (CLIN-8); sign-out failures are shown (#508). A failed reference, family or reported-SV request shows no report; a sign-out record that could not be loaded is neither "Draft" nor signed, and sign-out is not offered; a part that could not be loaded is marked in place and heads the printout, and retries. While the family's import is incomplete the report warns, naming what failed. An import that stopped part-way is listed in the incomplete-import dialog (what it had not finished), shown on the signed record, and named when it changed since sign-out; the dialog and the signed record name each failed dataset's job when they differ. A list of reported variants drawn from a capped candidate read says so on screen and in the print notice. Both lists ask for every reported variant (`page_size=10000`, not 500), and a list holding more reported variants than it shows says so on screen and in print (#730). A signed version writes a CNV classification's points as the classifier does (#738): the total and each ClinGen criterion signed and to two decimals (`+0.60`, `0.00`, `+0.90`, `-0.30`, `+1.00`), while a small variant's whole ACMG points keep their format. A signed version shows its Sample QC verdict as signed: a pass frozen beside a sex check that did not run, from before such a check counted as a warning, and a warning with the note naming the sample after (CLIN-1). The audit trail is read a page at a time (AUDIT-1): a trail longer than its first page says how many of its events it shows, in place and at the top of the printout, and **Show older events** reads the events recorded before the last one shown until the whole trail is shown, when both notices go; an older page that cannot be read leaves the events and the notices in place, says so, and is read on a second try; a whole trail says nothing and offers nothing to load. The signed analysis record (2026-10-14): the signed version shows the report code (printed) and the record's sentence, each reported variant's description as reviewed (genotypes included), the gene panels applied, the repeat expansions, the Paraphase results and the reference-data releases, in the footer too, from the record alone; a record signed before them says for each section that it does not hold it; a section not captured says so with its reason, and a variant without a description says so; straight after sign-out the page confirms the version and its code, off the printout; the live report says what a sign-out is and names the latest version's code. A PGT family's signed version shows its embryo calls as frozen (the ROI, the model, the disease haplotype, per embryo its call, the homologs it inherited at the ROI as the track names them, its warnings and its QC, the members' statuses and the haplotype file read), prints them and asks for none of the family's current calls; one without an ROI says no embryo was called; a PGT family's version signed before CoGA froze them says it does not hold them, one that could not capture them says so, and a family without embryos shows no such section. The import gate's dialog lists a single-file upload's and a deletion's mark as such, with no import job, and the signed record names them (SAFE-28). A reader in the family's project prints the report but cannot sign it out (with the reason), and the page asks no audit trail and says, on screen and in print, why it is not shown (SEC-1). A NIPT family's signed version shows its NIPT results, in print too (the fetal fraction and the checks with their verdicts, the coverage, the funnel, the categories, each reported variant with its de novo triage, a variant not classified with why, the recessive risk, what the analysis ran with); a NIPT family's record signed before them says it does not hold them, one that could not capture them says so, and another family's record says nothing of NIPT results. The sign-out and its gates run through the flow the NIPT page shares (`reportSignOut.tsx`), offered by the same rule (`signOutRefusal`). |

The newest clinical CNV knowledgebase rebuild of an assembly, when it failed, is shown with its reason until a later one succeeds (#721). |

No control or parameter for a filter the explorer does not apply, the ClinVar rescue sent; a capped total shown as N+; a loading, failed or empty assembly list is not read as no variants (#580). A failed search gives the server's reason and a retry, and the header does not count it as 0 variants (#613). A CSV export cut at its row cap, or searched through a capped tag or classification filter, is saved under a TRUNCATED name and announced; a complete one keeps its plain name and clears an earlier notice. |

The gene link uses `?gene=`, the parameter the gene page reads (#580). |

---

## Regulatory traceability

For the in-house IVD technical file, tests are traced **from requirements** in
[TF-09b — Requirements Traceability Matrix](regulatory/TF-09b-requirements-traceability-matrix.md)
(each `REQ-…` → implementation → verifying test → hazard) and the verification strategy is in
[TF-09 — V&V Plan](regulatory/TF-09-verification-validation.md). This document is the
complementary file→purpose index.

## Keeping this current

This catalogue is maintained by hand, but a **CI guard enforces it**: the `catalogue` job runs
[`scripts/check-test-catalogue.sh`](../scripts/check-test-catalogue.sh), which fails if any test
file in the tree is missing from this document — or if this document references a test file that
no longer exists. Run it locally before pushing:

```bash
./scripts/check-test-catalogue.sh
```

To regenerate the file list + per-file test counts when adding/removing tests:

```bash
# backend
for f in $(git ls-files 'backend/tests/*.py' 'backend/tests/**/*.py' | grep -E 'test_.*\.py$' | sort); do
  printf '%s\t%s\n' "$(grep -cE '^\s*(async )?def test_' "$f")" "$f"; done
# frontend
for f in $(git ls-files 'frontend/src/**/*.test.ts' 'frontend/src/**/*.test.tsx' | sort); do
  printf '%s\t%s\n' "$(grep -cE '\b(it|test)\(' "$f")" "$f"; done
# suite totals (a parametrized test counts once per case)
python -m pytest -q --co | tail -1
(cd frontend && npx vitest run | grep -E 'Test Files|Tests ')
```

When a test file is added, add a row to the relevant section here and (if it verifies a
technical-file requirement) update [TF-09b](regulatory/TF-09b-requirements-traceability-matrix.md).
